They have come in search of crowds, of “life,” and they gravitate naturally to the Bowery, the great democratic highway of the city, where the twenty-five-cent lodging-houses take them in. In the alleged reading-rooms of these great barracks, that often have accommodations, such as they are, for two, three, and even four hundred guests, they encounter three distinct classes of associates: the great mass adventurers like themselves, waiting there for something to turn up; a much smaller class of respectable clerks or mechanics, who, too poor or too lonely to have a home of their own, live this way from year to year; and lastly the thief in search of recruits for his trade. The sights the young stranger sees, and the company he keeps, in the Bowery are not of a kind to strengthen any moral principle he may have brought away from home, and by the time his money is gone, with no work yet in sight, and he goes down a step, a long step, to the fifteen-cent lodging-house, he is ready for the tempter whom he finds waiting for him there, reinforced by the contingent of ex-convicts returning from the prisons after having served out their sentences for robbery or theft. Then it is that the something he has been waiting for turns up. The police returns have the record of it. “In nine cases out of ten,” says Inspector Byrnes, “he turns out a thief, or a burglar, if, indeed, he does not sooner or later become a murderer.” As a matter of fact, some of the most atrocious of recent murders have been the result of schemes of robbery hatched in these houses, and so frequent and bold have become the depredations of the lodging-house thieves, that the authorities have been compelled to make a public demand for more effective laws that shall make them subject at all times to police regulation.

===Chinese===
大都市之于许多人，就像一支点亮的蜡烛之于飞蛾。它一团又一团地把他们招来，年年如此；他们怀着一种模糊的想法：天下若还有一处能混得下去的地方，必是这里；人这么多，总有什么会落到自己头上。这些人几乎全是年轻汉子，人生尚未落定，其中许多——或许大多数——刚从正经的好人家出来，毫无疑问怀着在这座城市起步、为自己闯出一条路来的诚实指望。趁四处张望的当儿，他们当中没有几个有多余的钱可供糟蹋，寄宿费便宜便成了一桩要紧事。更少有人了解这座城市和它的种种陷阱。他们是为寻人群、寻「生活」而来，便自然而然地被吸向鲍厄里——这座城市的大众通衢——二十五分钱寄宿所把他们收留进去。在这些大兵营号称的阅览室里——这样的兵营往往备有可供二三百人、甚至四百人栖身的铺位，姑且算铺位吧——年轻的外乡人遇上三类泾渭分明的同伴：一大群是同他一样的冒险家，在那里等着天上掉下点什么；数目少得多的一类，是体面的职员或工匠，或因太穷、或因太孤单而无力自成一家，年复一年这么过活；最后，便是那为自己的行当物色新丁的窃贼。年轻的外乡人在鲍厄里见到的种种光景、结交的各路同伴，都不是能巩固他或许从家里带来的道德操守的那一类；等他钱花光了，活计仍无着落，往下退了一级——长长的一级——退进十五分钱寄宿所时，他已准备好迎接那守在那里等他的诱惑者了；替这诱惑者撑腰的，还有一支刚从监狱里服满抢劫或偷窃的刑期回来的惯犯队伍。就在这时，他一直等着的那「什么」果然露头了。警方的统计记录着这一切。“十桩里有九桩，”伯恩斯探长说，“他到底成了窃贼，或者夜盗；倘若不然，他迟早还会变成杀人犯。”事实上，近来若干起最残暴的凶案，正是这些寄宿所里孵出来的抢劫阴谋的结果；寄宿所窃贼的劫掠闹得如此频繁、如此大胆，以致当局不得不公开要求制定更有效的法律，使这类房屋随时置于警察的监管之下。

===Original===
Inspector Byrnes observes that in the last two or three years at least four hundred young men have been arrested for petty crimes that originated in the lodging-houses, and that in many cases it was their first step in crime. He adds his testimony to the notorious fact that three-fourths of the young men called on to plead to generally petty offences in the courts are under twenty years of age, poorly clad, and without means. The bearing of the remark is obvious. One of the, to the police, well-known thieves who lived, when out of jail, at the Windsor, a well-known lodging-house in the Bowery, went to Johnstown after the flood and was shot and killed there while robbing the dead.

===Chinese===
伯恩斯探长指出，过去两三年间，至少有四百名青年因起于寄宿所的轻罪被捕，而其中许多案件，那是他们迈向罪恶的头一步。他还为那个尽人皆知的事实作证：法庭上被传来就多为轻罪的指控答辩的青年，四分之三不满二十岁，衣衫寒酸，身无分文。这话的指向一目了然。有一个警方熟知的窃贼，不入狱时就住在鲍厄里一家出名的寄宿所「温莎」（the Windsor）；约翰斯敦（Johnstown）发大水之后他去了那里，在劫掠死者时被开枪打死。

===Original===
An idea of just how this particular scheme of corruption works, with an extra touch of infamy thrown in, may be gathered from the story of David Smith, the “New York Fagin,” who was convicted and sent to prison last year through the instrumentality of the Society for the Prevention of Cruelty to Children. Here is the account from the Society’s last report:

===Chinese===
至于这套腐蚀人的勾当究竟如何运转——外加一笔格外下作的狠招——从「纽约费金」（“New York Fagin”，费金是狄更斯《雾都孤儿》中教唆孩童行窃的元凶）戴维·史密斯（David Smith）的故事里可以得个大概；去年，经防止虐待儿童协会（Society for the Prevention of Cruelty to Children）之手，他被定罪下狱。下面是协会最近一份报告中的记述：

===Original===
“The boy, Edward Mulhearn, fourteen years old, had run away from his home in Jersey City, thinking he might find work and friends in New York. He may have been a trifle wild. He met Smith on the Bowery and recognized him as an acquaintance. When Smith offered him a supper and bed he was only too glad to accept. Smith led the boy to a vile lodging-house on the Bowery, where he introduced him to his ‘pals’ and swore he would make a man of him before he was a week older. Next day he took the unsuspecting Edward all over the Bowery and Grand Street, showed him the sights and drew his attention to the careless way the ladies carried their bags and purses and the easy thing it was to get them. He induced Edward to try his hand. Edward tried and won. He was richer by three dollars! It did seem easy. ‘Of course it is,’ said his companion. From that time Smith took the boy on a number of thieving raids, but he never seemed to become adept enough to be trusted out of range of the ‘Fagin’s’ watchful eye. When he went out alone he generally returned empty-handed. This did not suit Smith. It was then he conceived the idea of turning this little inferior thief into a superior beggar. He took the boy into his room and burned his arms with a hot iron. The boy screamed and entreated in vain. The merciless wretch pressed the iron deep into the tender flesh, and afterward applied acid to the raw wound.

===Chinese===
“男孩爱德华·马尔赫恩（Edward Mulhearn），十四岁，从泽西城（Jersey City）的家里跑了出来，满以为在纽约或许找得到工作和朋友。他也许有点野。他在鲍厄里遇见史密斯，认出是个熟人。史密斯提出供他一顿晚饭一张床，他求之不得地接受了。史密斯把男孩领到鲍厄里一家下流寄宿所，把他介绍给自己的「同伙」，赌咒说不消一个星期，就要把他调教成一条汉子。第二天，他带着毫不疑心的爱德华走遍鲍厄里和格兰德街（Grand Street），领他见世面，并让他留意太太小姐们携带提包钱袋时何等漫不经心，把它们弄到手又是何等容易的一桩事。他怂恿爱德华试一手。爱德华试了，赢了。他多出了三块钱！这看上去确实容易。「当然容易，」他的同伴说。从那时起，史密斯便带着男孩出去干了好几票偷窃的勾当；可是他似乎总也学不到家，离不开「费金」那只警惕的眼睛盯视的范围去单独行事。他单独出门，多半空手而归。这不合史密斯的心意。就在那时，他想出了主意，要把这个不入流的小贼改造成一个上等的乞丐。他把男孩带进自己的房间，用一块烧红的烙铁烫他的双臂。男孩尖叫哀求，全然无用。那毫不留情的恶棍把烙铁深深按进细嫩的皮肉里，事后又往新烫开的伤口上涂了强酸。

===Original===
“Thus prepared, with his arm inflamed, swollen, and painful, Edward was sent out every day by this fiend, who never let him out of his sight, and threatened to burn his arm off if he did not beg money enough. He was instructed to tell people the wound had been caused by acid falling upon his arm at the works. Edward was now too much under the man’s influence to resist or disobey him. He begged hard and handed Smith the pennies faithfully. He received in return bad food and worse treatment.”

===Chinese===
“这样装备停当，爱德华便带着一条发炎、肿胀、疼痛的手臂，天天被打发出门；这个恶魔从不让他离开自己的视线，还威胁说，要是讨的钱不够，就把他的胳膊烧掉。他受命告诉人们，伤口是工厂里强酸溅到手臂上造成的。到这时，爱德华已深深落在此人的摆布之下，无力反抗，也不敢违拗。他卖力行乞，把讨来的铜板分文不差地交给史密斯。他得到的回报，是恶劣的饭食和更恶劣的待遇。”

===Original===
The reckoning came when the wretch encountered the boy’s father, in search of his child, in the Bowery, and fell under suspicion of knowing more than he pretended of the lad’s whereabouts. He was found in his den with a half dozen of his chums revelling on the proceeds of the boy’s begging for the day.

===Chinese===
清算的日子来了：那恶棍在鲍厄里迎面撞上了正在寻子的男孩父亲，因涉嫌对少年的下落知情不报而露了馅。人们在他的窟里找到他时，他正同半打狐朋狗友一起，挥霍着男孩当天行乞的进项。

===Original===
The twenty-five cent lodging-house keeps up the pretence of a bedroom, though the head-high partition enclosing a space just large enough to hold a cot and a chair and allow the man room to pull off his clothes is the shallowest of all pretences. The fifteen-cent bed stands boldly forth without screen in a room full of bunks with sheets as yellow and blankets as foul. At the ten-cent level the locker for the sleeper’s clothes disappears. There is no longer need of it. The tramp limit is reached, and there is nothing to lock up save, on general principles, the lodger. Usually the ten- and seven-cent lodgings are different grades of the same abomination. Some sort of an apology for a bed, with mattress and blanket, represents the aristocratic purchase of the tramp who, by a lucky stroke of beggary, has exchanged the chance of an empty box or ash-barrel for shelter on the quality floor of one of these “hotels.” A strip of canvas, strung between rough timbers, without covering of any kind, does for the couch of the seven-cent lodger who prefers the questionable comfort of a red-hot stove close to his elbow to the revelry of the stale-beer dive. It is not the most secure perch in the world. Uneasy sleepers roll off at intervals, but they have not far to fall to the next tier of bunks, and the commotion that ensues is speedily quieted by the boss and his club. On cold winter nights, when every bunk had its tenant, I have stood in such a lodging-room more than once, and listening to the snoring of the sleepers like the regular strokes of an engine, and the slow creaking of the beams under their restless weight, imagined myself on shipboard and experienced the very real nausea of seasickness. The one thing that did not favor the deception was the air; its character could not be mistaken.

===Chinese===
二十五分钱寄宿所还维持着卧室的门面，虽然那道齐头高的隔板——围出一小块刚够放一张帆布床、一把椅子，再容人腾挪脱衣的地方——已是天下最敷衍的门面。十五分钱的床则大大方方、毫无遮拦地敞在一间满是铺位的屋子里，床单黄得发暗，毯子脏得发臭。到了十分钱这一档，给睡客存放衣物的柜子不见了。再没有这个必要了。流浪汉的极限已到，除了按照通例该锁起来的寄宿客本人，再没有什么可锁的东西。通常，十分钱和七分钱的铺位，是同一种丑物的不同等级。一张勉强算是床的东西，带褥子和毯子，代表着流浪汉贵族气派的消费：他靠行乞走了好运，把将就睡进空箱子或灰渣桶的机会，换成了这些「旅馆」之一上等楼层上的一个栖身位。一条帆布，绷在粗糙的木料之间，没有任何铺垫，便充作七分钱寄宿客的卧榻——这位宁可要紧挨着手肘的烧得通红的炉子那份可疑的舒适，也不去坏啤酒窟里凑那份狂欢。那可不是世上最安稳的栖身之处。睡不安稳的人时不时滚下来，好在也掉不远，正落在下一层铺位上；随之而起的骚动，很快便被管事的老板用棍棒压平。寒冬的夜里，每个铺位都有了主，我不止一次站在这样一间寄宿屋里，听着睡客们如引擎冲程般规律的鼾声，听着房梁在他们辗转的重量下缓缓地吱呀作响，恍惚觉得自己置身船上，体味着货真价实的晕船恶心。唯一有损这场错觉的，是屋里的空气；那空气的成份是错认不了的。

===Original===
The proprietor of one of these seven-cent houses was known to me as a man of reputed wealth and respectability. He “ran” three such establishments and made, it was said, $8,000 a year clear profit on his investment. He lived in a handsome house quite near to the stylish precincts of Murray Hill, where the nature of his occupation was not suspected. A notice that was posted on the wall of the lodgers’ room suggested at least an effort to maintain his uptown standing in the slums. It read: “No swearing or loud talking after nine o’clock.” Before nine no exceptions were taken to the natural vulgarity of the place; but that was the limit.

===Chinese===
这类七分钱寄宿所之一的老板，我认识，是个据说家境殷实、体面端方的人物。他「经营」着三家这样的铺面，据说一年从这项投资里净赚 $8,000。他自己住在一所漂亮宅子里，离时髦的默里山（Murray Hill）地界不远；在那里，没人疑心他营生的性质。寄宿客房间墙上贴着的一张告示，至少表明他作过一番努力，要在贫民窟里维护自己上城人的体面。告示写着：“九点以后，不得说脏话、不得高声喧哗。”九点以前，这地方与生俱来的粗鄙下流无人置喙；但那条线就是底线。

===Original===
There are no licensed lodging-houses known to me which charge less than seven cents for even such a bed as this canvas strip, though there are unlicensed ones enough where one may sleep on the floor for five cents a spot, or squat in a sheltered hallway for three. The police station lodging-house, where the soft side of a plank is the regulation couch, is next in order. The manner in which this police bed is “made up” is interesting in its simplicity. The loose planks that make the platform are simply turned over, and the job is done, with an occasional coat of whitewash thrown in to sweeten things. I know of only one easier way, but, so far as I am informed, it has never been introduced in this country. It used to be practised, if report spoke truly, in certain old-country towns. The “bed” was represented by clotheslines stretched across the room upon which the sleepers hung by the armpits for a penny a night. In the morning the boss woke them up by simply untying the line at one end and letting it go with its load; a laborsaving device certainly, and highly successful in attaining the desired end.

===Chinese===
据我所知，没有一家领执照的寄宿所，连这样一条帆布床也收不到七分钱；不过无照的黑店有的是：花五分钱可以在地板上占一块地方睡，出三分钱可以蹲在避风的门厅里过一夜。再往下一档，便是警察局寄宿室，那里规制配备的卧榻，是木板软的那一面。这种警察床「铺」起来的法子，简单得耐人寻味。搭台子的那些活动木板，干脆翻个面，活儿就完了，偶尔再刷一道石灰水，给一切添点「香甜」。我只知道还有一种更省事的办法，不过据我所知，它还从未被引进这个国家。倘若传闻属实，欧洲老家的一些小镇上从前是实行过的。那「床」由横贯房间的几条晾衣绳充当，睡客们用腋窝挂在上头，一夜一便士。到了早晨，老板只需把绳子一头解开，连人带载一放，便把他们叫醒了；这确是一件省力的发明，而且在达到预期目的上极为成功。

===Original===
According to the police figures, 4,974,025 separate lodgings were furnished last year by these dormitories, between two and three hundred in number, and, adding the 147,634 lodgings furnished by the station-houses, the total of the homeless army was 5,121,659, an average of over fourteen thousand homeless men11 for every night in the year! The health officers, professional optimists always in matters that trench upon their official jurisdiction, insist that the number is not quite so large as here given. But, apart from any slight discrepancy in the figures, the more important fact remains that last year’s record of lodgers is an all round increase over the previous year’s of over three hundred thousand, and that this has been the ratio of growth of the business during the last three years, the period of which Inspector Byrnes complains as turning out so many young criminals with the lodging-house stamp upon them. More than half of the lodging-houses are in the Bowery district, that is to say, the Fourth, Sixth, and Tenth Wards, and they harbor nearly three-fourths of their crowds. The calculation that more than nine thousand homeless young men lodge nightly along Chatham Street and the Bowery, between the City Hall and the Cooper Union, is probably not far out of the way. The City Missionary finds them there far less frequently than the thief in need of helpers. Appropriately enough, nearly one-fifth of all the pawnshops in the city and one-sixth of all the saloons are located here, while twenty-seven percent of all the arrests on the police books have been credited to the district for the last two years.

===Chinese===
根据警方的数字，这些寄宿所——总数在两三百家之间——去年提供了 4,974,025 人次的住宿；加上各警察分局提供的 147,634 人次，这支无家可归的大军总计 5,121,659 人次，全年平均每晚有一万四千多名无家可归的男人（11）！卫生官员们——在事关自家职权范围的事务上向来是职业乐天派——坚称这个数字没有这里给出的大。但撇开数字上的些许出入不谈，更要紧的事实依然摆在那里：去年的寄宿记录比前年全面增长了三十多万人次，而且近三年来这门生意一直保持着这个增长比例——伯恩斯探长所抱怨的，正是这段时期制造出这么多烙着寄宿所印记的年轻罪犯。寄宿所一多半集中在鲍厄里地区，也就是第四、第六和第十选区，它们收容的寄宿客占了总数将近四分之三。据估算，每夜有九千多名无家可归的青年沿着查塔姆街和鲍厄里、在市政厅与库珀学院（Cooper Union）之间寄宿，这个数目多半离谱不远。城市传教士（City Missionary）在那里找到他们的次数，远不及急需帮手的窃贼来得频繁。倒也相称得很：全城当铺的近五分之一、酒馆的六分之一都开在这里；而过去两年，警局簿册上全部逮捕次数的百分之二十七，都记在这个区的账上。

===Original===
About election time, especially in Presidential elections, the lodging-houses come out strong on the side of the political boss who has the biggest “barrel.” The victory in political contests, in the three wards I have mentioned of all others, is distinctly to the general with the strongest battalions, and the lodging-houses are his favorite recruiting ground. The colonization of voters is an evil of the first magnitude, none the less because both parties smirch their hands with it, and for that reason next to hopeless. Honors are easy, where the two “machines,” intrenched in their strongholds, outbid each other across the Bowery in open rivalry as to who shall commit the most flagrant frauds at the polls. Semi-occasionally a champion offender is caught and punished, as was, not long ago, the proprietor of one of the biggest Bowery lodging-houses. But such scenes are largely spectacular, if not prompted by some hidden motive of revenge that survives from the contest. Beyond a doubt Inspector Byrnes speaks by the card when he observes that “usually this work is done in the interest of some local political boss, who stands by the owner of the house, in case the latter gets into trouble.” For standing by, read twisting the machinery of outraged justice so that its hand shall fall not too heavily upon the culprit, or miss him altogether. One of the houses that achieved profitable notoriety in this way in many successive elections, a notorious tramps’ resort in Houston Street, was lately given up, and has most appropriately been turned into a bar-factory, thus still contributing, though in a changed form, to the success of “the cause.” It must be admitted that the black tramp who herds in the West Side “hotels” is more discriminating in this matter of electioneering than his white brother. He at least exhibits some real loyalty in invariably selling his vote to the Republican bidder for a dollar, while he charges the Democratic boss a dollar and a half. In view of the well-known facts, there is a good deal of force in the remark made by a friend of ballot reform during the recent struggle over that hotly contested issue, that real ballot reform will do more to knock out cheap lodging-houses than all the regulations of police and health officers together.

===Chinese===
临近选举的时候，尤其是总统选举年，寄宿所便倾巢出动，站到「钱桶」（barrel，指竞选资金）最鼓的政治大佬一边。在我提到过的那三个选区——比哪个选区都更甚——政治角斗的胜利，明白无误地归于麾下兵马最强的那位将军，而寄宿所正是他最中意的募兵场。选民的「殖民」是头一等的大弊，并不因两党都把手弄脏其间而稍减分毫，反而正因如此，几乎无药可救。两台「政治机器」各据堡垒，隔着鲍厄里公开抬价竞买，比着看谁在票箱前舞弊得最明目张胆，彼此倒也难分高下。偶尔也有个头号舞弊犯被拿住受罚，比如不久前鲍厄里一家最大的寄宿所的老板。但这种场面多半是做给人看的，除非它背后藏着竞选里结下的、余恨未消的某种报复私心。毫无疑问，伯恩斯探长在这里说得字字有据：“这种勾当通常是替某个地方政治大佬办的；万一房主惹上麻烦，大佬会替他撑腰。”所谓撑腰，不妨读作：把被冒犯的司法机器拧一拧，好叫它的手落下来时不要压得太重，或者干脆落空。休斯敦街（Houston Street）上一家出了名的流浪汉巢穴——它就是靠这门勾当在历次选举中捞足了恶名的寄宿所之一——最近歇业了，而且再贴切不过地改成了一家酒吧台工厂：虽然换了个形式，依旧在为「这项事业」（the cause）的成功出力。必须承认，群集在西区那些「旅馆」里的黑人流浪汉，在拉票这桩事上比他的白人兄弟更有辨别力。他至少表现出几分真正的忠诚：把选票卖给共和党出价人，回回只要一块钱；而民主党的老板来买，他要一块五。鉴于这些尽人皆知的事实，一位选票改革之友在近来围绕那项激烈争持的议题角力时所说的话，便很有几分力量：真正的选票改革，比警察和卫生官员的全部规章加在一起，更能干脆利落地打垮廉价寄宿所。

===Original===
The experiment made by a well-known stove manufacturer a winter or two ago in the way of charity, might have thrown much desired light on the question of the number of tramps in the city, could it have been carried to a successful end. He opened a sort of breakfast shop for the idle and unemployed in the region of Washington Square, offering to all who had no money a cup of coffee and a roll for nothing. The first morning he had a dozen customers, the next about two hundred. The number kept growing until one morning, at the end of two weeks, found by actual count 2,014 shivering creatures in line waiting their turn for a seat at his tables. The shop was closed that day. It was one of the rare instances of too great a rush of custom wrecking a promising business, and the great problem remained unsolved.

===Chinese===
一两年前的冬天，一位知名的火炉制造商做了一桩慈善性质的实验，倘若能善始善终，本可以就这座城市里流浪汉的数目这个悬案提供人们渴求的答案。他在华盛顿广场（Washington Square）一带为游手好闲者和失业者开了一爿早餐铺子，向所有身无分文的人免费供应一杯咖啡加一个面包卷。头一个早晨，他接待了十二位客人；第二天约莫两百位。人数节节增长，直到两周末尾的一个早晨，经实地点数，队伍里有 2,014 个瑟瑟发抖的人，等着轮到自己上他的餐桌落座。当天铺子就关了门。这是顾客蜂拥过度、反把一门前程似锦的生意挤垮的罕见事例之一；而那个重大的问题，依然悬而未决。

## IX Chinatown / IX 唐人街
===Original===
Between the tabernacles of Jewry and the shrines of the Bend, Joss has cheekily planted his pagan worship of idols, chief among which are the celestial worshipper’s own gain and lusts. Whatever may be said about the Chinaman being a thousand years behind the age on his own shores, here he is distinctly abreast of it in his successful scheming to “make it pay.” It is doubtful if there is anything he does not turn to a paying account, from his religion down, or up, as one prefers. At the risk of distressing some well-meaning, but, I fear, too trustful people, I state it in advance as my opinion, based on the steady observation of years, that all attempts to make an effective Christian of John Chinaman will remain abortive in this generation; of the next I have, if anything, less hope. Ages of senseless idolatry, a mere grub-worship, have left him without the essential qualities for appreciating the gentle teachings of a faith whose motive and unselfish spirit are alike beyond his grasp. He lacks the handle of a strong faith in something, anything however wrong, to catch him by. There is nothing strong about him, except his passions when aroused. I am convinced that he adopts Christianity, when he adopts it at all, as he puts on American clothes, with what the politicians would call an ulterior motive, some sort of gain in the near prospect﻿—washing, a Christian wife, perhaps, anything he happens to rate for the moment above his cherished pigtail. It may be that I judge him too harshly. Exceptions may be found. Indeed, for the credit of the race, I hope there are such. But I am bound to say my hope is not backed by lively faith.

===Chinese===
在犹太人的会堂与弯道区（the Bend）的神龛之间，乔斯神（Joss，当时西方对华人所拜偶像的称呼）厚着脸皮安下了他那异教的偶像崇拜，而众偶像之中居于首席的，便是这位天朝崇拜者（celestial worshipper）自己的私利与私欲。任凭人们怎么说华人（Chinaman，当时用语）在自己本土落后于时代一千年，在这里，凭着那套叫“它生利”的成功盘算，他却分明与时代并驾齐驱。从他的宗教往下数——或者往上数，悉听尊便——究竟有没有一样东西他不拿来生利，实在可疑。冒着让某些好心的、但我恐怕也是过于轻信的人士痛心的风险，我愿预先声明我的看法；这看法基于多年的持续观察：一切想把“中国佬约翰”（John Chinaman，当时对华人的拟人化蔑称）造就成真基督徒的尝试，在这一代人中仍将归于流产；对下一代，我的希望若有、也只有更少。经年累月的愚妄偶像崇拜——那不过是一种蛆虫式的崇拜——使他丧失了领会一种信仰之温柔教诲所必需的禀赋；那信仰的动机与无私的精神，同样都在他的把握之外。他缺少一个让人抓得住的把手：对某样东西——哪怕再错的东西——的坚强信仰。他身上没有任何坚强之物，除了激情被唤醒之时的激情。我深信，他之接受基督教——在他当真接受的时候——就像穿上美式衣裳一样，抱着政客们所谓的“别有用心的动机”，图的是近在眼前的好处——洗衣营生，或许还有一位基督徒妻子，总之凡是他一时看得比自己那条宝贝辫子（pigtail）更重的东西。也许我把他评判得太过苛刻。例外或许找得到。的确，看在这个种族的体面上，我希望有这样的例外。但我必须说，我的希望并没有热切的信心作后盾。

===Original===
Chinatown as a spectacle is disappointing. Next-door neighbor to the Bend, it has little of its outdoor stir and life, none of its gayly-colored rags or picturesque filth and poverty. Mott Street is clean to distraction: the laundry stamp is on it, though the houses are chiefly of the conventional tenement-house type, with nothing to rescue them from the everyday dismal dreariness of their kind save here and there a splash of dull red or yellow, a sign, hung endways and with streamers of red flannel tacked on, that announces in Chinese characters that Dr. Chay Yen Chong sells Chinese herb medicines, or that Won Lung & Co.﻿—queer contradiction﻿—take in washing, or deal out tea and groceries. There are some gimcracks in the second story fire-escape of one of the houses, signifying that Joss or a club has a habitation there. An American patent medicine concern has seized the opportunity to decorate the background with its cabalistic trademark, that in this company looks as foreign as the rest. Doubtless the privilege was bought for cash. It will buy anything in Chinatown, Joss himself included, as indeed, why should it not? He was bought for cash across the sea and came here under the law that shuts out the live Chinaman, but lets in his dead god on payment of the statutory duty on bric-a-brac. Red and yellow are the holiday colors of Chinatown as of the Bend, but they do not lend brightness in Mott Street as around the corner in Mulberry. Rather, they seem to descend to the level of the general dullness, and glower at you from doors and windows, from the telegraph pole that is the official organ of Chinatown and from the store signs, with blank, un-meaning stare, suggesting nothing, asking no questions, and answering none. Fifth Avenue is not duller on a rainy day than Mott Street to one in search of excitement. Whatever is on foot goes on behind closed doors. Stealth and secretiveness are as much part of the Chinaman in New York as the catlike tread of his felt shoes. His business, as his domestic life, shuns the light, less because there is anything to conceal than because that is the way of the man. Perhaps the attitude of American civilization toward the stranger, whom it invited in, has taught him that way. At any rate, the very doorways of his offices and shops are fenced off by queer, forbidding partitions suggestive of a continual state of siege. The stranger who enters through the crooked approach is received with sudden silence, a sullen stare, and an angry “Vat you vant?” that breathes annoyance and distrust.

===Chinese===
作为一景来观，唐人街是令人失望的。它虽与弯道区比邻而居，却少有弯道区那种户外的喧腾与生气，也没有它那色彩斑斓的破衣烂衫和那如画的污秽贫穷。莫特街（Mott Street）干净得要命：街上打着洗衣业的印记；房屋大抵是老一套的廉租公寓（tenement）样式，全靠东一处西一处一抹暗红或明黄，才把它们从其同类日复一日的阴惨黯淡里搭救出来——那是些竖着挂、钉了红法兰绒飘带的招牌，用汉字宣告：查延庄医生（Dr. Chay Yen Chong）售卖中国草药，或者永隆公司（Won Lung & Co.）——古怪的矛盾——承揽洗衣，或者售卖茶叶杂货。有一座房子二楼的太平梯上摆着些小玩意儿，表明乔斯神或者某个堂口（club）在那里设有居处。一家美国专利药商抓住机会，用它那天书般的商标装点了背景——在这一堆招牌中间，它看上去和其余的一样满含异邦气。毫无疑问，这项特权是拿现金买来的。在唐人街，现金什么都买得到，乔斯神本尊也不例外——说真的，为什么买不到呢？他本人就是被现金从海外买来，并依据那条把活华人拒之门外、却在照章缴清古董关税之后放他那死去的神祇入境的法律，来到了这里。红与黄是唐人街的节庆色，正如也是弯道区的节庆色；但它们在莫特街并不像在拐角那头的桑树街（Mulberry Street）那样添出生气。毋宁说，它们仿佛降到了普遍的单调那一层，从门窗里、从那根堪称唐人街官方机关报的电线杆上、从店铺招牌上阴沉沉地瞪着你，一脸茫然空茫的凝视，不暗示什么，不问什么，也不答什么。对寻刺激的人而言，雨天的第五大道也不比莫特街更乏味。凡在酝酿的事都在闭门之后进行。鬼祟与深藏不露之于纽约的华人，一如他毡鞋上猫一般的脚步。他的生意如同他的家庭生活一样躲着光，倒不是因为有什么要遮掩，而是因为此人天生就是这个路数。也许，是美国文明对这位它请进门来的陌生客的态度教会了他这个路数。无论如何，他的写字间和铺面的门道本身就被古怪而拒人的隔板挡了起来，透着一股经年不散的围城气。从歪歪斜斜的入口进门的生客，受到的是骤然的沉默、阴沉的瞪视，和一声恼怒的 “Vat you vant?”（“你要什么？”），满含厌烦与猜忌。

===Original===
Trust not him who trusts no one, is as safe a rule in Chinatown as out of it. Were not Mott Street overawed in its isolation, it would not be safe to descend this open cellar-way, through which come the pungent odor of burning opium and the clink of copper coins on the table. As it is, though safe, it is not profitable to intrude. At the first footfall of leather soles on the steps the hum of talk ceases, and the group of celestials, crouching over their game of fan tan, stop playing and watch the comer with ugly looks. Fan tan is their ruling passion. The average Chinaman, the police will tell you, would rather gamble than eat any day, and they have ample experience to back them. Only the fellow in the bunk smokes away, indifferent to all else but his pipe and his own enjoyment. It is a mistake to assume that Chinatown is honeycombed with opium “joints.” There are a good many more outside of it than in it. The celestials do not monopolize the pipe. In Mott Street there is no need of them. Not a Chinese home or burrow there, but has its bunk and its layout, where they can be enjoyed safe from police interference. The Chinaman smokes opium as Caucasians smoke tobacco, and apparently with little worse effect upon himself. But woe unto the white victim upon which his pitiless drug gets its grip!

===Chinese===
莫信不信任任何人的人——这条守则在唐人街内外同样稳妥。若不是孤悬一隅的莫特街仍慑于威势，走下这道敞开的地下室通道原是并不安全的；通道里飘出烧着的鸦片的刺鼻气味，还有铜钱落在桌面上的叮当声。话虽如此，下去虽则安全，闯进去却无益处。皮鞋底踏上台阶的头一声响，谈话的嗡嗡声便戛然而止，蹲在番摊（fan tan）赌局旁的那一群「天朝人」（celestials，当时对华人的称呼）也住了手，用凶光盯住来人。番摊是他们主宰一切的激情。警察会告诉你，寻常华人随便哪一天都宁赌不食，而这话背后有他们充足的经验作保。只有铺上那位兀自抽个不停，除了烟枪和自己的享受，对其余一切全不在意。以为唐人街里密布着鸦片馆（opium “joints”），是个错误。唐人街外面的这类馆子比里面的多得多。「天朝人」并没有垄断烟枪。在莫特街，这类馆子毫无用处。那里没有一处华人住家或地洞般的窝，不备着自己的铺位和一应烟具，尽可安然享用，不怕警察搅扰。华人抽鸦片，如同高加索人抽烟草，看来对他本人也无更坏的后果。可是这无情的毒品一旦攫住它的白人受害者，祸殃便临头了！

===Original===
The bloused pedlars who, with arms buried half to the elbow in their trousers’ pockets, lounge behind their stock of watermelon seed and sugarcane, cut in lengths to suit the purse of the buyer, disdain to offer the barbarian their wares. Chinatown, that does most things by contraries, rules it holiday style to carry its hands in its pockets, and its denizens follow the fashion, whether in blue blouse, in gray, or in brown, with shining and braided pigtail dangling below the knees, or with hair cropped short above a coat collar of “Melican” cut. All kinds of men are met, but no women﻿—none at least with almond eyes. The reason is simple: there are none. A few, a very few, Chinese merchants have wives of their own color, but they are seldom or never seen in the street. The “wives” of Chinatown are of a different stock that comes closer home.

===Chinese===
穿布衫的小贩们双臂埋在裤袋里、直没到半截胳膊肘，懒洋洋地靠在他们那堆西瓜子和甘蔗后面——甘蔗按买主钱袋的厚薄切成一段段——要他们向野蛮人兜售货色，他们是不屑的。凡事大都反着来的唐人街，把双手插袋立为节日的做派，居民们也个个追随这份时新：或穿蓝衫，或穿灰衫，或穿褐衫，油亮的辫子编得垂到膝下；或把头发剪得短短，压在「美利坚」（“Melican”，华人对 American 的发音）式样的大衣领子上。各色人等都见得着，唯独没有女人——至少没有生杏眼的。原因很简单：根本没有。寥寥可数、极其寥寥的几位华人商人有自己同肤色的妻子，但她们在街上难得一见，或者干脆见不着。唐人街的“妻子们”属于另一种血统，一种近在家门的血统。

===Original===
From the teeming tenements to the right and left of it come the white slaves of its dens of vice and their infernal drug, that have infused into the “Bloody Sixth” Ward a subtler poison than ever the stale-beer dives knew, or the “sudden death” of the Old Brewery. There are houses, dozens of them, in Mott and Pell Streets, that are literally jammed, from the “joint” in the cellar to the attic, with these hapless victims of a passion which, once acquired, demands the sacrifice of every instinct of decency to its insatiate desire. There is a church in Mott Street, at the entrance to Chinatown, that stands as a barrier between it and the tenements beyond. Its young men have waged unceasing war upon the monstrous wickedness for years, but with very little real result. I have in mind a house in Pell Street that has been raided no end of times by the police, and its population emptied upon Blackwell’s Island, or into the reformatories, yet is today honeycombed with scores of the conventional households of the Chinese quarter: the men worshippers of Joss; the women, all white, girls hardly yet grown to womanhood, worshipping nothing save the pipe that has enslaved them body and soul. Easily tempted from homes that have no claim upon the name, they rarely or never return. Mott Street gives up its victims only to the Charity Hospital or the Potter’s Field. Of the depth of their fall no one is more thoroughly aware than these girls themselves; no one less concerned about it. The calmness with which they discuss it, while insisting illogically upon the fiction of a marriage that deceives no one, is disheartening. Their misery is peculiarly fond of company, and an amount of visiting goes on in these households that makes it extremely difficult for the stranger to untangle them. I came across a company of them “hitting the pipe” together, on a tour through their dens one night with the police captain of the precinct. The girls knew him, called him by name, offered him a pipe, and chatted with him about the incidents of their acquaintance, how many times he had “sent them up,” and their chances of “lasting” much longer. There was no shade of regret in their voices, nothing but utter indifference and surrender.

===Chinese===
从唐人街左右两侧人烟稠密的廉租公寓里，走出来的正是那些罪恶巢穴及其恶魔毒品手下的白人奴隶——这毒品给「流血的第六选区」（the “Bloody Sixth” Ward）注入了一味阴毒远甚于坏啤酒窟（stale-beer dive）所曾领教、也远甚于老酿酒厂（the Old Brewery）那“暴毙”的毒。莫特街和派尔街（Pell Street）上有几十座房子，从地下室的“馆子”直到阁楼，实实在在塞满了这些不幸的受害者——这癖嗜一旦沾身，便要求把一切体面的本能都祭献给它那填不满的欲壑。莫特街在唐人街入口处有一座教堂，像一道屏障，立在唐人街与更远处的廉租公寓之间。教会的青年们多年来对这骇人的邪恶打了一场不停歇的仗，却几乎没有真正的战果。我记得派尔街有一座房子，被警察突查过无数回，里面的人被整批送上布莱克韦尔岛（Blackwell's Island），或送进教养院，可到今天它仍密布着几十户唐人街的常规人家：男人是乔斯神的崇拜者；女人则清一色是白人，是些尚未长成人身的姑娘，什么都不拜，只拜那支把她们身心都奴役了的烟枪。她们轻易便被诱出那些配不上“家”这个名号的家，此后便难得、或者永不回头。莫特街交出它的受害者时，去处只有慈善医院（Charity Hospital）或乱葬岗（Potter's Field）。论堕落地之深，没有人比这些姑娘自己知道得更透彻；也没有人比她们更不放在心上。她们谈论此事时的那份平静——一面又不合逻辑地坚执着那桩骗不了任何人的婚姻虚构——实在叫人心灰。她们的苦命特别爱作伴，这些人家的串门又如此频繁，外人极难把他们分辨清楚。一天夜里，我同本区的警察队长巡看这些巢穴，撞见一伙姑娘正凑在一处“过烟枪”。姑娘们认得他，叫得出他的名字，请他抽一管，还同他闲聊她与他相识的种种旧事：他“送她们上去”过多少回，她们还能“撑”多久。她们的声音里没有一丝悔恨的影子，只有全然的冷漠与听任。

===Original===
One thing about them was conspicuous: their scrupulous neatness. It is the distinguishing mark of Chinatown, outwardly and physically. It is not altogether by chance the Chinaman has chosen the laundry as his distinctive field. He is by nature as clean as the cat, which he resembles in his traits of cruel cunning, and savage fury when aroused. On this point of cleanliness he insists in his domestic circle, yielding in others with crafty submissiveness to the caprice of the girls, who “boss” him in a very independent manner, fretting vengefully under the yoke they loathe, but which they know right well they can never shake off, once they have put the pipe to their lips and given Mott Street a mortgage upon their souls for all time. To the priest, whom they call in when the poison racks the body, they pretend that they are yet their own masters; but he knows that it is an idle boast, least of all believed by themselves. As he walks with them the few short steps to the Potter’s Field, he hears the sad story he has heard told over and over again, of father, mother, home, and friends given up for the accursed pipe, and stands hopeless and helpless before the colossal evil for which he knows no remedy.

===Chinese===
她们身上有一点十分显眼：一尘不染的整洁。这是唐人街的标志，无论外表还是身体上都是。华人把洗衣业选作自己的招牌行当，并非全出偶然。他天性像猫一样爱洁，而在残忍的狡黠、被激怒时的凶残狂暴这些品性上，他也像猫。在整洁这一点上，他在家中寸步不让；在其他各点上，则带着狡黠的顺从，屈就姑娘们的任性——这些姑娘以极独立的姿态“作他的主”，一面在自己憎恶的轭下愤愤磨牙、怀着报复的心，一面心里雪亮：一旦烟枪沾了唇、把灵魂永远抵押给了莫特街，这轭便再也挣不脱了。毒发折磨身体时她们请来神父，对他佯称自己仍是自己的主人；可他知道那是句空话，最不信的正是她们自己。当他陪着她们走完到乱葬岗的那短短几步路，他听见那听过一遍又一遍的伤心故事——父亲、母亲、家和亲朋，都为那支该诅咒的烟枪舍了——然后站在这他明知无方可施的巨大邪恶面前，绝望而无助。

===Original===
The frequent assertions of the authorities that at least no girls under age are wrecked on this Chinese shoal, are disproved by the observation of those who go frequently among these dens, though the smallest girl will invariably, and usually without being asked, insist that she is sixteen, and so of age to choose the company she keeps. Such assertions are not to be taken seriously. Even while I am writing, the morning returns from one of the precincts that pass through my hands report the arrest of a Chinaman for “inveigling little girls into his laundry,” one of the hundred outposts of Chinatown that are scattered all over the city, as the outer threads of the spider’s web that holds its prey fast. Reference to case No. 39,499 in this year’s report of the Society for the Prevention of Cruelty to Children, will discover one of the much travelled roads to Chinatown. The girl whose story it tells was thirteen, and one of six children abandoned by a dissipated father. She had been discharged from an Eighth Avenue store, where she was employed as cash girl, and, being afraid to tell her mother, floated about until she landed in a Chinese laundry. The judge heeded her tearful prayer, and sent her home with her mother, but she was back again in a little while despite all promises of reform.

===Chinese===
当局屡屡断言，至少没有未成年的姑娘在这片华人的浅滩上毁身沉没；而常到这些巢穴中走动的人的观察，却证明此说不确——尽管个头最小的姑娘总会一口咬定、而且往往不等人问，就说自己十六岁了，到了可以自择交往对象的年纪。这样的断言，不必当真。就在我写作的当口，一份经我之手传阅的分区晨报，还载着一条逮捕一名华人的记录，罪名是“诱骗小姑娘进他的洗衣店”——那是遍布全城的唐人街上百个前哨站之一，好比蜘蛛网外围的丝线，把猎物牢牢缠定。查一查防止虐待儿童协会（Society for the Prevention of Cruelty to Children）本年度报告里的第 39,499 号案卷，就会找到一条走得熟烂的通往唐人街的路。案卷所述的那个姑娘十三岁，是被一个酗酒父亲抛弃的六个孩子之一。她原在第八大道一家店铺当收银女孩（cash girl），遭辞退后不敢告诉母亲，四处漂荡，最后漂进了一家华人洗衣店。法官垂听了她含泪的祈求，把她交母亲领回了家；可任凭许下多少改过的诺言，没多久她又回去了。

===Original===
Her tyrant knows well that she will come, and patiently bides his time. When her struggles in the web have ceased at last, he rules no longer with gloved hand. A specimen of celestial logic from the home circle at this period came home to me with a personal application, one evening when I attempted, with a policeman, to stop a Chinaman whom we found beating his white “wife” with a broom-handle in a Mott Street cellar. He was angry at our interference, and declared vehemently that she was “bad.”

===Chinese===
她的暴君深知她还会回来，便耐心守候他的时机。等她在网中的挣扎终于止息，他便不再戴着手套统治了。那个时期家庭圈子里的天朝逻辑标本，有一晚落到了我头上，成了切身的一课：那晚我同一名警察试图制止一个华人——我们撞见他正在莫特街一间地下室里，用扫帚柄毒打他的白人“妻子”。他对我们的干涉十分恼怒，激愤地宣称她“坏”。

===Original===
“S’ppose your wifee bad, you no lickee her?” he asked, as if there could be no appeal from such a commonsense proposition as that. My assurance that I did not, that such a thing could not occur to me, struck him dumb with amazement. He eyed me a while in stupid silence, poked the linen in his tub, stole another look, and made up his mind. A gleam of intelligence shone in his eye, and pity and contempt struggled in his voice. “Then, I guess, she lickee you,” he said.

===Chinese===
“S'ppose your wifee bad, you no lickee her?”（“假如你老婆坏，你不打她吗？”）他问道，仿佛这样一条常识命题是无可上诉的。我向他保证我不打，说这样的事根本不会进我的脑子；这话惊得他哑口无言。他呆呆地沉默着打量我一阵，捅了捅洗衣盆里的衣裳，又偷觑一眼，拿定了主意。一线悟性的光在他眼里一闪，怜悯与轻蔑在他的声音里交上了锋。“Then, I guess, she lickee you,”（“那我想，是她打你。”）他说。

===Original===
No small commotion was caused in Chinatown once upon the occasion of an expedition I undertook, accompanied by a couple of police detectives, to photograph Joss. Some conscienceless wag spread the report, after we were gone, that his picture was wanted for the Rogues’ Gallery at Headquarters. The insult was too gross to be passed over without atonement of some sort. Two roast pigs made matters all right with his offended majesty of Mott Street, and with his attendant priests, who bear a very practical hand in the worship by serving as the divine stomach, as it were. They eat the good things set before their rice-paper master, unless, as once happened, some sacrilegious tramp sneaks in and gets ahead of them. The practical way in which this people combine worship with business is certainly admirable. I was told that the scrawl covering the wall on both sides of the shrine stood for the names of the pillars of the church or club﻿—the Joss House is both﻿—that they might have their reward in this world, no matter what happened to them in the next. There was another inscription overhead that needed no interpreter. In familiar English letters, copied bodily from the trade dollar, was the sentiment: “In God we trust.” The priest pointed to it with undisguised pride and attempted an explanation, from which I gathered that the inscription was intended as a diplomatic courtesy, a delicate international compliment to the “Melican Joss,” the almighty dollar.

===Chinese===
有一次我由两名警察侦探陪同，远征去给乔斯神拍照，在唐人街引起了一场不小的骚动。我们走后，某个没心没肺的促狭鬼散布流言，说他的照片是要挂进警察总局的案犯照片陈列室（Rogues' Gallery）的。这般侮辱太重，不做些赎罪便无法轻轻放过。两头烤猪让莫特街这位受了冒犯的君上重开笑颜，也让他的随侍祭司们心满意足——这些祭司在崇拜事务中出着非常实际的一臂之力，可谓充任神明的肠胃。供在他们的纸扎主子面前的好东西，都由他们吃掉——除非像有一回那样，哪个亵渎神明的流浪汉溜进去抢了先。这个民族把敬神与生意合在一起办的务实作风，实在令人赞叹。我被告知，神龛两侧墙上涂满的潦草字迹，写的是教会或堂口诸顶梁柱的名姓——乔斯庙（Joss House）一身二任——好叫他们今生便得报偿，无论来世遭逢什么。头顶上另有一行题字，则无须通译。用人们熟悉的英文字母、整句照搬自贸易银元（trade dollar）的，是这样一句感言：“In God we trust”（“我们信仰上帝”）。祭司毫不掩饰他的得意，指着它力图解释一番；我从中领会到，这题字意在致一种外交上的礼遇，是对“美利坚神”（“Melican Joss”）——万能的美元——的一声精巧的国际恭维。

===Original===
Chinatown has enlisted the telegraph for the dissemination of public intelligence, but it has got hold of the contrivance by the wrong end. As the wires serve us in newspaper-making, so the Chinaman makes use of the pole for the same purpose. The telegraph pole, of which I spoke as the real official organ of Chinatown, stands not far from the Joss House in Mott Street, in full view from Chatham Square. In it centres the real life of the colony, its gambling news. Every day yellow and red notices are posted upon it by unseen hands, announcing that in such and such a cellar a fan tan game will be running that night, or warning the faithful that a raid is intended on this or that game through the machination of a rival interest. A constant stream of plotting and counterplotting makes up the round of Chinese social and political existence. I do not pretend to understand the exact political structure of the colony, or its internal government. Even discarding as idle the stories of a secret cabal with power over life and death, and authority to enforce its decrees, there is evidence enough that the Chinese consider themselves subject to the laws of the land only when submission is unavoidable, and that they are governed by a code of their own, the very essence of which is rejection of all other authority except under compulsion. If now and then some horrible crime in the Chinese colony, a murder of such hideous ferocity as one I have a very vivid recollection of, where the murderer stabbed his victim (both Chinamen, of course) in the back with a meat-knife, plunging it in to the hilt no less than seventeen times, arouses the popular prejudice to a suspicion that it was “ordered,” only the suspected themselves are to blame, for they appear to rise up as one man to shield the criminal. The difficulty of tracing the motive of the crime and the murderer is extreme, and it is the rarest of all results that the police get on the track of either. The obstacles in the way of hunting down an Italian murderer are as nothing to the opposition encountered in Chinatown. Nor is the failure of the pursuit wholly to be ascribed to the familiar fact that to Caucasian eyes “all Chinamen look alike,” but rather to their acting “alike,” in a body, to defeat discovery at any cost.

===Chinese===
唐人街也征用了电报来传播公共信息，只是这套机巧它抓的是相反的一头。电线之于我们办报有怎样的用处，电线杆之于华人便有怎样的用处。我曾说它是唐人街真正的官方机关报的那根电线杆，立在莫特街乔斯庙不远处，从查塔姆广场（Chatham Square）望过去一览无余。殖民地的真正生活——它的赌博新闻——就汇聚在那上面。每天，看不见的手往杆上张贴黄红两色的告示，宣布当夜某间地下室将开番摊赌局，或者警告众信徒：因某个对头的密谋，某场赌局将要挨抄。无休无止的布局与反布局，构成了华人社会与政治生活的一轮又一轮。我不敢妄称懂得这块殖民地的确切政治构造，或它的内部统治。即便把那些关于一个握有生杀之权、并有力量强制执行其法令的秘密会社的传闻，当作无稽之谈撇在一边，证据也已足够表明：华人只在万不得已时才认为自己受此地法律的约束；他们受治于自己的一套法典，而这法典的精髓，便是除非迫于强制、否则一概拒绝其他一切权威。倘若华人殖民地间或出现的某桩骇人罪行——比如一桩我记得清清楚楚、狰狞至极的凶杀：凶手用一把切肉刀从背后捅进受害者（双方自然都是华人）的身体，刀刀深及刀柄，足足十七刀——激起了民间的偏见，叫人疑心那是“奉命”行凶，那也只能怪被疑者自己，因为他们仿佛总要万众一心地站出来包庇罪犯。追索罪案的动机与凶手，难乎其难；警察对两者之一摸到线索，已是所有结果中最罕见的一种。追捕一个意大利凶手路上的障碍，比起在唐人街遭遇的阻挠，简直算不得什么。而追捕之不得手，也不能全归咎于那个尽人皆知的事实——在高加索人眼里“华人长相全一个样”——倒应归于他们“行动也一个样”：结成一体，不惜任何代价挫败侦破。

===Original===
Withal the police give the Chinese the name of being the “quietest people down there,” meaning in the notoriously turbulent Sixth Ward; and they are. The one thing they desire above all is to be let alone, a very natural wish perhaps, considering all the circumstances. If it were a laudable, or even an allowable ambition that prompts it, they might be humored with advantage, probably, to both sides. But the facts show too plainly that it is not, and that in their very exclusiveness and reserve they are a constant and terrible menace to society, wholly regardless of their influence upon the industrial problems which their presence confuses. The severest official scrutiny, the harshest repressive measures are justifiable in Chinatown, orderly as it appears on the surface, even more than in the Bend, and the case is infinitely more urgent. To the peril that threatens there all the senses are alert, whereas the poison that proceeds from Mott Street puts mind and body to sleep, to work out its deadly purpose in the corruption of the soul.

===Chinese===
话虽如此，警察还是给了华人一个名号：“那边最安静的人”——所谓那边，指的是那出了名的动荡的第六选区；他们也确实安静。他们比一切都要紧的心愿只有一桩：别来搅扰。考虑到种种情由，这也许是个再自然不过的愿望。倘若驱使这愿望的是一种可嘉的、甚或仅仅可以容许的抱负，那么顺着他们一点，或许对双方都有益处。但事实太明白地显示并非如此；恰恰在他们那种闭关自守与深藏不露之中，他们成了社会一个持续而可怕的威胁——至于他们的存在给工业问题添的乱，尚且不论。在唐人街——纵然它表面井然有序——最严厉的官方查究、最严酷的压制手段都是正当的，比在弯道区更有过之，而且事态之紧迫，更不可同日而语。那边咄咄逼人的危险，人的感官全都警醒；而从莫特街散出的毒，却让心智与肉体一同昏睡，好在灵魂的朽坏里遂行它致命的图谋。

===Original===
This again may be set down as a harsh judgment. I may be accused of inciting persecution of an unoffending people. Far from it. Granted, that the Chinese are in no sense a desirable element of the population, that they serve no useful purpose here, whatever they may have done elsewhere in other days, yet to this it is a sufficient answer that they are here, and that, having let them in, we must make the best of it. This is a time for very plain speaking on this subject. Rather than banish the Chinaman, I would have the door opened wider﻿—for his wife; make it a condition of his coming or staying that he bring his wife with him. Then, at least, he might not be what he now is and remains, a homeless stranger among us. Upon this hinges the real Chinese question, in our city at all events, as I see it. To assert that the victims of his drug and his base passions would go to the bad anyhow, is begging the question. They might and they might not. The chance is the span between life and death. From any other form of dissipation than that for which Chinatown stands there is recovery: for the victims of any other vice, hope. For these there is neither hope nor recovery; nothing but death﻿—moral, mental, and physical death.

===Chinese===
这又可能被记成一纸苛刻的判词。我可能被指煽动对一个与人无忤的民族的迫害。绝非如此。就算华人在任何意义上都算不上人口中可取的成分，就算他们在此地派不上什么有用的用场——无论他们昔年在别处有过怎样的作为——对此也有一个充分的回答：他们已经在这里了；既然把他们放了进来，我们就必须把这局面往好里办。在这个题目上，眼下正是需要极坦率的直言的时候。与其放逐华人，我宁肯把门开得更大——为他的妻子；把他来或留的一个条件定为：携妻同来。那么，他至少有可能不再是、也不再一直是眼下这副样子：我们中间一个无家可归的异乡客。真正的华人问题——至少在我们这座城市，依我看——正系于此。断言他的毒品和他卑贱情欲的受害者横竖总会变坏，是在预设结论、回避论证。她们可能变坏，也可能不。这一点机会，便是生与死之间的一线。唐人街所代表的那种放荡之外的任何放荡，都有回头之路；任何其他恶习的受害者，都还有指望。唯独她们，既无指望也无回头路；只有死——道德的死、精神的死、肉体的死。

## X Jewtown / X 「犹太镇」

===Original===
The tenements grow taller, and the gaps in their ranks close up rapidly as we cross the Bowery and, leaving Chinatown and the Italians behind, invade the Hebrew quarter. Baxter Street, with its interminable rows of old clothes shops and its brigades of pullers-in﻿—nicknamed “the Bay” in honor, perhaps, of the tars who lay to there after a cruise to stock up their togs, or maybe after the “schooners” of beer plentifully bespoke in that latitude﻿—Bayard Street, with its synagogues and its crowds, gave us a foretaste of it. No need of asking here where we are. The jargon of the street, the signs of the sidewalk, the manner and dress of the people, their unmistakable physiognomy, betray their race at every step. Men with queer skullcaps, venerable beard, and the outlandish long-skirted kaftan of the Russian Jew, elbow the ugliest and the handsomest women in the land. The contrast is startling. The old women are hags; the young, houris. Wives and mothers at sixteen, at thirty they are old. So thoroughly has the chosen people crowded out the Gentiles in the Tenth Ward that, when the great Jewish holidays come around every year, the public schools in the district have practically to close up. Of their thousands of pupils scarce a handful come to school. Nor is there any suspicion that the rest are playing hookey. They stay honestly home to celebrate. There is no mistaking it: we are in Jewtown.

===Chinese===
跨过鲍厄里（the Bowery），把唐人街（Chinatown）和意大利人抛在身后，闯入希伯来人（Hebrew）的聚居区，一路只见廉租公寓（tenement）越见高耸，楼群之间的空当也在迅速合拢。巴克斯特街（Baxter Street）——那一排排望不到头的旧衣店，还有成队的「拉客郎」（pullers-in）；这地方得了「海湾」（the Bay）的诨名，或许是致敬那些远航归来、泊岸于此添置行头的水手，也可能是缘于那一纬度上被大量点叫的「大杯」啤酒（schooner）——连同贝亚德街（Bayard Street）的犹太会堂（synagogue）和熙攘人群，早给我们递过这里的预兆了。这里用不着问身在何处。街上的土话，人行道上的招牌，人们的举止与衣着，他们一望即知的面相，每一步都在泄露他们的族属。戴着古怪小圆帽、蓄着庄严长须、穿着俄国犹太人那种异邦味道的长摆长袍（kaftan）的男人们，同这个国家里最丑的和最俏的女人们挤挤挨挨。这对照触目惊心。老妇个个是丑婆，少妇个个是仙姬。十六岁便做了妻子和母亲，三十岁上就老了。上帝的选民把第十选区（Tenth Ward）的外邦人（Gentiles）排挤得何等彻底——每年犹太大节期一到，本区的公立学校实际上只得关门。成千上万的学生里，难得有一小撮到校。倒也没有人疑心其余的孩子在逃学。他们老老实实待在家里过节。这错不了：我们到了「犹太镇」（Jewtown）。

===Original===
It is said that nowhere in the world are so many people crowded together on a square mile as here. The average five-story tenement adds a story or two to its stature in Ludlow Street and an extra building on the rear lot, and yet the sign “To Let” is the rarest of all there. Here is one seven stories high. The sanitary policeman whose beat this is will tell you that it contains thirty-six families, but the term has a widely different meaning here and on the avenues. In this house, where a case of smallpox was reported, there were fifty-eight babies and thirty-eight children that were over five years of age. In Essex Street two small rooms in a six-story tenement were made to hold a “family” of father and mother, twelve children, and six boarders. The boarder plays as important a part in the domestic economy of Jewtown as the lodger in the Mulberry Street Bend. These are samples of the packing of the population that has run up the record here to the rate of three hundred and thirty thousand per square mile. The densest crowding of Old London, I pointed out before, never got beyond a hundred and seventy-five thousand. Even the alley is crowded out. Through dark hallways and filthy cellars, crowded, as is every foot of the street, with dirty children, the settlements in the rear are reached. Thieves know how to find them when pursued by the police, and the tramps that sneak in on chilly nights to fight for the warm spot in the yard over some baker’s oven. They are out of place in this hive of busy industry, and they know it. It has nothing in common with them or with their philosophy of life, that the world owes the idler a living. Life here means the hardest kind of work almost from the cradle. The world as a debtor has no credit in Jewtown. Its promise to pay wouldn’t buy one of the old hats that are hawked about Hester Street, unless backed by security representing labor done at lowest market rates. But this army of workers must have bread. It is cheap and filling, and bakeries abound. Wherever they are in the tenements the tramp will skulk in, if he can. There is such a tramps’ roost in the rear of a tenement near the lower end of Ludlow Street, that is never without its tenants in winter. By a judicious practice of flopping over on the stone pavement at intervals, and thus warming one side at a time, and with an empty box to put the feet in, it is possible to keep reasonably comfortable there even on a rainy night. In summer the yard is the only one in the neighborhood that does not do duty as a public dormitory.

===Chinese===
据说世界上再没有哪个地方，一平方英里上挤着这么多人。寻常的五层廉租公寓，到了勒德洛街（Ludlow Street）会长高一两层，宅后的地皮上再添一栋楼；饶是这样，「招租」（To Let）的告示在这里仍是最难得一见的东西。这里就有一栋七层高楼。负责这一巡区的卫生警察会告诉你，楼里住着三十六户人家；不过「人家」这个词，在这里和在大道上，含义大不相同。就在这栋报告过一例天花的房子里，有五十八个婴儿，外加三十八个五岁以上的儿童。在艾塞克斯街（Essex Street），一栋六层廉租公寓里的两小间屋，硬是塞下了一「家」人：父亲、母亲、十二个孩子，外加六个搭伙客（boarder）。搭伙客在「犹太镇」家庭经济里的分量，一如搭铺客在桑树街弯道（Mulberry Street Bend）的分量。这些便是人口打包装箱的样本——正是这样的堆挤，把这里的纪录推到了每平方英里三十三万人的密度。我前面指出过，旧伦敦最稠密的拥挤，也从未超过十七万五千。连巷子都被挤出了局。穿过黑洞洞的门厅和污秽的地窖——那里同街面上每一尺地皮一样挤满脏孩子——便到了宅后深处的聚落。窃贼被警察追赶时，自知道怎么摸进去；寒夜里溜进来争夺院里面包房烤炉上方那一小块暖处的流浪汉，也一样轻车熟路。在这个忙忙碌碌的劳作蜂巢里，他们是格格不入的人，自己也心里有数。这地方同他们毫无共同语言，也同他们那套人生哲学——世界欠懒人一口饭吃——毫无共同之处。这里的生活，几乎从摇篮里起就意味着最苦的劳作。作为债务人的世界，在「犹太镇」没有信用。它开出的支付承诺，买不走海斯特街（Hester Street）上叫卖的一顶旧帽子——除非有抵押背书，而抵押须是按最低市价折算的已付劳动。但这支劳动大军总得有面包。面包便宜又管饱，面包房比比皆是。廉租公寓里凡是开着面包房的，流浪汉就会趁隙溜进去——只要溜得进。勒德洛街下段附近一栋廉租公寓的后院，就有这么一个流浪汉的宿窝，冬天从不少住客。讲究地每隔一阵在石板地上翻个身，一次焐热一面身子，再寻一只空箱子把脚伸进去，这样哪怕雨夜也能过得相当舒服。到了夏天，这院子是左近四邻唯一不用来充当公共寝室的院子。

===Original===
Thrift is the watchword of Jewtown, as of its people the world over. It is at once its strength and its fatal weakness, its cardinal virtue and its foul disgrace. Become an overmastering passion with these people who come here in droves from Eastern Europe to escape persecution, from which freedom could be bought only with gold, it has enslaved them in bondage worse than that from which they fled. Money is their God. Life itself is of little value compared with even the leanest bank account. In no other spot does life wear so intensely bald and materialistic an aspect as in Ludlow Street. Over and over again I have met with instances of these Polish or Russian Jews deliberately starving themselves to the point of physical exhaustion, while working night and day at a tremendous pressure to save a little money. An avenging Nemesis pursues this headlong hunt for wealth; there is no worse paid class anywhere. I once put the question to one of their own people, who, being a pawnbroker, and an unusually intelligent and charitable one, certainly enjoyed the advantage of a practical view of the situation: “Whence the many wretchedly poor people in such a colony of workers, where poverty, from a misfortune, has become a reproach, dreaded as the plague?”

===Chinese===
节俭是「犹太镇」的口号，也是全世界犹太人的口号。它既是这地方的力量所在，又是它的致命弱点；既是它的头号美德，又是它的奇耻大辱。在这些成群结队从东欧逃出迫害、投奔而来的人身上——在那个旧世界，免于迫害的自由唯有黄金才买得到——节俭已化成一种压倒一切的激情，又反过来把他们套进了比逃离的那副枷锁更坏的奴役。金钱是他们的上帝。比起哪怕最菲薄的一笔银行存款，生命本身也没有多少分量。在别处，生活都不像在勒德洛街这样，露出一副如此赤裸裸、彻头彻尾唯物的面孔。我一再遇见这样的实例：这些波兰或俄国的犹太人，一面刻意把自己饿到体力衰竭的边缘，一面以惊人的强度日夜赶工，只为攒下一点钱。一场复仇的报应——涅墨西斯（Nemesis）——紧追着这场亡命的逐富；天下再找不出工钱更低的一群人。有一回，我把这个问题提给他们本族的一位人士——他是个当铺老板，而且是同行里少见的明白人、慈善人，想必享有一种纵观全局的实践眼光：「在这样一个劳动者成群的聚居地里，贫穷已从一种不幸变成了一种耻辱，人人避之如瘟疫，哪来这么多穷愁潦倒的人？」

===Original===
“Immigration,” he said, “brings us a lot. In five years it has averaged twenty-five thousand a year, of which more than seventy percent have stayed in New York. Half of them require and receive aid from the Hebrew Charities from the very start, lest they starve. That is one explanation. There is another class than the one that cannot get work: those who have had too much of it; who have worked and hoarded and lived, crowded together like pigs, on the scantiest fare and the worst to be got, bound to save whatever their earnings, until, worn out, they could work no longer. Then their hoards were soon exhausted. That is their story.” And I knew that what he said was true.

===Chinese===
「移民给我们送来一大批，」他说，「五年里平均每年两万五千人，其中七成以上留在了纽约。半数人从落脚头一天起就需要、也确实领着联合希伯来慈善会（United Hebrew Charities）的接济，免得饿死。这是一种解释。除了找不到活计的那一类，还有另一类：活计干得太多的人；他们干活、攒钱、过日子，像猪一样挤作一堆，吃的是最省的伙食、能弄到手的最坏的东西，铁了心要把挣来的一切都攒下，直到磨损殆尽、再也干不动了。这时候，他们的积蓄很快见了底。这就是他们的故事。」我知道他说的是实情。

===Original===
Penury and poverty are wedded everywhere to dirt and disease, and Jewtown is no exception. It could not well be otherwise in such crowds, considering especially their low intellectual status. The managers of the Eastern Dispensary, which is in the very heart of their district, told the whole story when they said: “The diseases these people suffer from are not due to intemperance or immorality, but to ignorance, want of suitable food, and the foul air in which they live and work.”12 The homes of the Hebrew quarter are its workshops also. Reference will be made to the economic conditions under which they work in a succeeding chapter. Here we are concerned simply with the fact. You are made fully aware of it before you have travelled the length of a single block in any of these East Side streets, by the whir of a thousand sewing-machines, worked at high pressure from earliest dawn till mind and muscle give out together. Every member of the family, from the youngest to the oldest, bears a hand, shut in the qualmy rooms, where meals are cooked and clothing washed and dried besides, the livelong day. It is not unusual to find a dozen persons﻿—men, women, and children﻿—at work in a single small room. The fact accounts for the contrast that strikes with wonder the observer who comes across from the Bend. Over there the entire population seems possessed of an uncontrollable impulse to get out into the street; here all its energies appear to be bent upon keeping in and away from it. Not that the streets are deserted. The overflow from these tenements is enough to make a crowd anywhere. The children alone would do it. Not old enough to work and no room for play, that is their story. In the home the child’s place is usurped by the lodger, who performs the service of the Irishman’s pig﻿—pays the rent. In the street the army of hucksters crowd him out. Typhus fever and smallpox are bred here, and help solve the question what to do with him. Filth diseases both, they sprout naturally among the hordes that bring the germs with them from across the sea, and whose first instinct is to hide their sick lest the authorities carry them off to the hospital to be slaughtered, as they firmly believe. The health officers are on constant and sharp lookout for hidden fever-nests. Considering that half of the ready-made clothes that are sold in the big stores, if not a good deal more than half, are made in these tenement rooms, this is not excessive caution. It has happened more than once that a child recovering from smallpox, and in the most contagious stage of the disease, has been found crawling among heaps of half-finished clothing that the next day would be offered for sale on the counter of a Broadway store; or that a typhus fever patient has been discovered in a room whence perhaps a hundred coats had been sent home that week, each one with the wearer’s death-warrant, unseen and unsuspected, basted in the lining.

===Chinese===
匮乏与贫穷，处处同污秽和疾病结成连理，「犹太镇」也不例外。在这样的人堆里，尤其考虑到他们低下的智识水准，想不这样也难。东区分诊所（Eastern Dispensary）就设在他们聚居区的正中心，其管理者们一句话道尽了全部：「这些人所患的疾病，并非起于酗酒或不道德，而是起于无知、缺乏适当的食物，以及他们生活与劳作于其中的污浊空气。」12 希伯来人聚居区的家宅，同时也是它的工场。他们劳作所处的经济境况，随后一章自有论及；这里我们只关心事实本身。在下东区（East Side）随便哪条街上，你还走不满一个街区的长度，就会对此有十足的认识——千百架缝纫机的嗡嗡声，从天光初亮一直开足马力，直到脑力和筋肉一齐垮掉。家中每一个人，从最年幼到最年长，都搭上一把手，整日关在令人作呕的屋子里——饭在这里煮，衣裳也在这里洗了晾干。一间小屋里碰上一打人——男人、女人、孩子——在做活，并非罕事。这一事实解释了那种令从弯道区（the Bend）过来的观者愕然的对照：在那边，全体居民仿佛被一股压不住的冲动驱赶着要上街去；在这里，全部精力却像是都用在守住屋里、躲开街上。倒不是说街上冷清。这些廉租公寓里溢出的人口，走到哪儿都够凑成一大群。光孩子们就够。年纪不够做工，又没地方玩耍——这就是他们的境况。在家里，孩子的位置被搭铺客篡占了，后者尽着爱尔兰人的猪那一份职分——交房租。在街上，小贩大军又把他挤出去。斑疹伤寒和天花在这里孳生，帮着解决拿他怎么办的问题。两者都是污秽病，在那些把病菌从海外一并带来的大群里自自然然地生根发芽；而这些人的头一个本能，就是把病人藏起来，生怕当局把他们拉走、送进医院「宰掉」——他们是笃信这一点的。卫生官员们对藏匿的热病窝点时刻严加侦伺。想想大商店里出售的现成衣裳，一半——兴许还远不止一半——产自这些廉租公寓的屋子，这份警惕便算不得过分。这样的事不止一回：一个天花渐愈、正当传染最烈阶段的孩子，被人发现在成堆的半成品衣裳里爬来爬去，而第二天，这些衣裳就会摆上某家百老汇（Broadway）商店的柜台；又或者，一个斑疹伤寒病人被发现在这样一间屋里——那个星期，从这屋里也许发走过上百件外套，每一件的衬里都粗缝着穿衣人的死刑判决书，无人看见，也无人起疑。

===Original===
The health officers call the Tenth the typhus ward; in the office where deaths are registered it passes as the “suicide ward,” for reasons not hard to understand; and among the police as the “crooked ward,” on account of the number of “crooks,” petty thieves and their allies, the “fences,” receivers of stolen goods, who find the dense crowds congenial. The nearness of the Bowery, the great “thieves’ highway,” helps to keep up the supply of these, but Jewtown does not support its dives. Its troubles with the police are the characteristic crop of its intense business rivalries. Oppression, persecution, have not shorn the Jew of his native combativeness one whit. He is as ready to fight for his rights, or what he considers his rights, in a business transaction﻿—synonymous generally with his advantage﻿—as if he had not been robbed of them for eighteen hundred years. One strong impression survives with him from his days of bondage: the power of the law. On the slightest provocation he rushes off to invoke it for his protection. Doubtless the sensation is novel to him, and therefore pleasing. The police at the Eldridge Street station are in a constant turmoil over these everlasting fights. Somebody is always denouncing somebody else, and getting his enemy or himself locked up; frequently both, for the prisoner, when brought in, has generally as plausible a story to tell as his accuser, and as hot a charge to make. The day closes on a wild conflict of rival interests. Another dawns with the prisoner in court, but no complainant. Over night the case has been settled on a business basis, and the police dismiss their prisoner in deep disgust.

===Chinese===
卫生官员们管第十选区叫「伤寒选区」；在登记死亡的办公室里，它顶着「自杀选区」的名号，缘由不难明白；在警察口中，它又是「奸邪选区」，因为「奸邪之徒」——小偷小摸之辈及其盟友、收赃销赃的「赃贩」（fences）——在这里人多势众，稠密的人群正合他们的脾胃。鲍厄里——那条赫赫有名的「盗贼大道」——近在咫尺，替这类人源源补充货源；但「犹太镇」却不供养自己的下等酒馆。它同警察之间的纠葛，是它炽烈的生意竞逐长出的特产。压迫、迫害，丝毫没能削去犹太人与生俱来的好斗性。在一桩生意里——这个说法一般就等于他的利益——他为自己争权利，或争他认定的权利，照样寸步不让，仿佛那些权利不曾被夺走过一千八百年。从为奴的岁月里，他保留下一样强烈的印象：法律的权力。稍有风吹草动，他便奔去请法律出面护持。毫无疑问，这滋味对他新鲜，因而可心。埃尔德里奇街（Eldridge Street）警察分局的警察们，为这些永无休止的争斗终日焦头烂额。总有人告发旁人，把仇家或者他自己——常常是两个一起——弄进班房；因为囚犯带到时，讲出的故事往往同原告一样头头是道，提出的控告也一样火爆。这一天在敌对利益的一场混战里收场。第二天破晓，囚犯上了法庭，原告却没了影。一夜之间，案子已按生意路数私了，警察满腔厌恶地把人放掉。

===Original===
These quarrels have sometimes a comic aspect. Thus, with the numerous dancing-schools that are scattered among the synagogues, often keeping them company in the same tenement. They are generally kept by some man who works in the daytime at tailoring, cigarmaking, or something else. The young people in Jewtown are inordinately fond of dancing, and after their day’s hard work will flock to these “schools” for a night’s recreation. But even to their fun they carry their business preferences, and it happens that a school adjourns in a body to make a general raid on the rival establishment across the street, without the ceremony of paying the admission fee. Then the dance breaks up in a general fight, in which, likely enough, someone is badly hurt. The police come in, as usual, and ring down the curtain.

===Chinese===
这些争吵有时还带着喜剧色彩。比如那散布在各犹太会堂之间的众多舞蹈学校——它们常常就在同一栋廉租公寓里与会堂作邻居。办学的一般是某个男人，白天做裁缝、卷雪茄，或者干别的营生。「犹太镇」的年轻人极其痴迷跳舞，一天苦工下来，会成群涌到这些「学校」里消遣一夜。可是连玩乐他们也摆不脱各自的生意立场，于是时有这样的事：一所学校全体休学，浩浩荡荡开到街对面那家对台铺子去大举进攻，连买门票的过场都免了。于是舞会以一场混战散场，十有八九有人挂彩。警察照例登台，落幕。

===Original===
Bitter as are his private feuds, it is not until his religious life is invaded that a real inside view is obtained of this Jew, whom the history of Christian civilization has taught nothing but fear and hatred. There are two or three missions in the district conducting a hopeless propagandism for the Messiah whom the Tenth Ward rejects, and they attract occasional crowds, who come to hear the Christian preacher as the Jews of old gathered to hear the apostles expound the new doctrine. The result is often strikingly similar. “For once,” said a certain well-known minister of an uptown church to me, after such an experience, “I felt justified in comparing myself to Paul preaching salvation to the Jews. They kept still until I spoke of Jesus Christ as the Son of God. Then they got up and fell to arguing among themselves and to threatening me, until it looked as if they meant to take me out in Hester Street and stone me.” As at Jerusalem, the Chief Captain was happily at hand with his centurions, in the person of a sergeant and three policemen, and the preacher was rescued. So, in all matters pertaining to their religious life that tinges all their customs, they stand, these East Side Jews, where the new day that dawned on Calvary left them standing, stubbornly refusing to see the light. A visit to a Jewish house of mourning is like bridging the gap of two thousand years. The inexpressibly sad and sorrowful wail for the dead, as it swells and rises in the hush of all sounds of life, comes back from the ages like a mournful echo of the voice of Rachel “weeping for her children and refusing to be comforted, because they are not.”

===Chinese===
他的私仇纵然火爆，也要等到他的宗教生活遭到侵犯时，才能得到这个犹太人内里的真实一瞥——一部基督教文明史，教给他的只有恐惧与仇恨。区里有那么两三处布道所，为被第十选区弃绝的弥赛亚经营着一场毫无指望的宣传，偶尔也招来人群；他们来听基督教牧师布道，恰如古时的犹太人聚拢来听使徒们阐发新道。结果往往出奇地相似。「就这一回，」上城一座教堂的一位知名牧师在经历过这样一场之后对我说，「我觉得把自己比作向犹太人传救恩的保罗，是问心无愧的。我一讲到把耶稣基督称作上帝之子，他们原本还安安静静。随后便纷纷起身，互相争辩起来，又转而威吓我，直到那架势看上去竟像要把我拖到海斯特街上，用石头砸死。」正如当年在耶路撒冷，千夫长幸而带着百夫长们及时赶到——这一回化身为一名警佐和三名警察——牧师获救了。于是，在一切牵涉宗教生活的事务上——那宗教生活又浸染着他们的全部习俗——这些下东区的犹太人，依旧站在各各他（Calvary）山上那个新日子破晓时把他们撂下的地方，执拗地不肯见那光。走访一户犹太吊丧之家，就像把两千年的鸿沟接通。那为死者发出的、难以言表的凄恻哭号，在一切生之喧响俱寂之际涨起、攀升，从悠悠岁月里传回来，像拉结（Rachel）嗓音的一声哀恸回响——她「为她的儿女哀哭，不肯受安慰，因为他们都不在了」。

===Original===
Attached to many of the synagogues, which among the poorest Jews frequently consist of a scantily furnished room in a rear tenement, with a few wooden stools or benches for the congregation, are Talmudic schools that absorb a share of the growing youth. The schoolmaster is not rarely a man of some attainments who has been stranded there, his native instinct for moneymaking having been smothered in the process that has made of him a learned man. It was of such a school in Eldridge Street that the wicked Isaac Iacob, who killed his enemy, his wife, and himself in one day, was janitor. But the majority of the children seek the public schools, where they are received sometimes with some misgivings on the part of the teachers, who find it necessary to inculcate lessons of cleanliness in the worst cases by practical demonstration with washbowl and soap. “He took hold of the soap as if it were some animal,” said one of these teachers to me after such an experiment upon a new pupil, “and wiped three fingers across his face. He called that washing.” In the Allen Street public school the experienced principal has embodied among the elementary lessons, to keep constantly before the children the duty that clearly lies next to their hands, a characteristic exercise. The question is asked daily from the teacher’s desk: “What must I do to be healthy?” and the whole school responds:

===Chinese===
许多犹太会堂都附设着塔木德学校（Talmudic schools），收容一部分成长中的少年；在最穷的犹太人那里，会堂往往不过是后院廉租公寓（rear tenement）里一间陈设寥落的屋子，给会众摆几条木凳或长椅罢了。学堂先生不少是个有些造诣、却在此搁浅了的人；把他造就成一个学者的那番功夫，也把他生来赚钱的本能闷死了。埃尔德里奇街那样一所学校的看门人，正是那个狠毒的艾萨克·雅各布（Isaac Iacob）——此人在一天之内杀了他的仇人、他的妻子和他自己。但大多数孩子上的是公立学校；老师们接收他们时，有时不无顾虑，在最糟的情形下，还非得靠洗脸盆和肥皂的实地演示来传授清洁课。「他抓起那块肥皂，就像抓起什么活物，」这样在一个新生身上做过实验之后，其中一位老师对我说，「然后抡起三根手指在脸上抹了一把。他管这个叫洗脸。」在艾伦街（Allen Street）公立学校，那位经验老到的校长把一项别具特色的操练编进了基础课里，好叫那份明明白白最迫在手边的本分，时刻摆在孩子们眼前。每天，讲台上都提出这个问题：「要怎样才能健康？」全校齐声应答：

===Original===
> “I must keep my skin clean,
 

 Wear clean clothes,
 

 Breathe pure air,
 

 And live in the sunlight.”

===Chinese===
> 「我必须保持皮肤清洁，
 

 穿干净的衣服，
 

 呼吸纯净的空气，
 

 生活在阳光下。」

===Original===
It seems little less than biting sarcasm to hear them say it, for to not a few of them all these things are known only by name. In their everyday life there is nothing even to suggest any of them. Only the demand of religious custom has power to make their parents clean up at stated intervals, and the young naturally are no better. As scholars, the children of the most ignorant Polish Jew keep fairly abreast of their more favored playmates, until it comes to mental arithmetic, when they leave them behind with a bound. It is surprising to see how strong the instinct of dollars and cents is in them. They can count, and correctly, almost before they can talk.

===Chinese===
听他们念这些，简直不啻辛辣的挖苦，因为他们中间不少人，这一切都只是识其名而已。他们的日常生活里，连一样能叫人联想到这些的字眼都没有。唯有宗教习俗的规约，才有力量叫他们的父母每隔一段时日大扫除一回；年轻人自然也强不到哪儿去。论功课，最无知的波兰犹太人的孩子，同比他们更得优待的玩伴咬得相当紧；一上心算，便一个箭步把后者甩在身后。看着美元美分的本能在他们身上何等强劲，实在叫人吃惊。他们差不多还不会说话，就会数数，而且数得一点不错。

===Original===
Within a few years the police captured on the East Side a band of firebugs who made a business of setting fire to tenements for the insurance on their furniture. There has, unfortunately, been some evidence in the past year that another such conspiracy is on foot. The danger to which these fiends expose their fellow-tenants is appalling. A fire-panic at night in a tenement, by no means among the rare experiences in New York, with the surging, half-smothered crowds on stairs and fire-escapes, the frantic mothers and crying children, the wild struggle to save the little that is their all, is a horror that has few parallels in human experience.

===Chinese===
几年之内，警察在下东区抓获过一伙纵火贼（firebugs），他们专以放火烧廉租公寓、图谋家具保险金为业。不幸的是，过去一年里已有迹象表明，又一桩这样的阴谋正在酝酿。这些恶徒置同楼房客于何等险地，真是骇人听闻。夜里廉租公寓的一场火警大恐慌——这在纽约绝非罕有——楼梯上、太平梯（fire-escape）上涌动着半窒息的人潮，母亲们发疯似的奔突，孩子们啼哭，人人为了抢出那点微薄的全部家当而疯狂挣扎：这是人生经验中少有堪与并论的恐怖。

===Original===
I cannot think without a shudder of one such scene in a First Avenue tenement. It was in the middle of the night. The fire had swept up with sudden fury from a restaurant on the street floor, cutting off escape. Men and women threw themselves from the windows, or were carried down senseless by the firemen. Thirteen half-clad, apparently lifeless bodies were laid on the floor of an adjoining coal-office, and the ambulance surgeons worked over them with sleeves rolled up to the elbows. A half-grown girl with a baby in her arms walked about among the dead and dying with a stunned, vacant look, singing in a low, scared voice to the child. One of the doctors took her arm to lead her out, and patted the cheek of the baby soothingly. It was cold. The baby had been smothered with its father and mother; but the girl, her sister, did not know it. Her reason had fled.

===Chinese===
每想起第一大道（First Avenue）一栋廉租公寓里的那一幕，我都不寒而栗。那是半夜时分。大火从临街一层的餐馆里陡然狂卷上来，截断了退路。男男女女从窗口纵身跳下，或者被消防队员人事不省地抬下楼来。十三具衣衫不整、看样子没了气息的躯体，被安放在隔壁煤炭行的地板上，救护车上的外科医生们挽袖到肘，在他们身上忙着。一个半大的姑娘，怀里抱着个婴儿，神情发怔、目光空洞地在死者与垂死者中间走来走去，用低低的、受了惊的嗓音给孩子唱歌。一位医生拉起她的手臂要领她出去，又疼惜地拍了拍婴儿的脸蛋。脸蛋冰凉。婴儿是同爸爸妈妈一起被闷死的；可那姑娘——婴儿的姐姐——并不知道。她的神志已经逃走了。

===Original===
Thursday night and Friday morning are bargain days in the “Pig-market.” Then is the time to study the ways of this peculiar people to the best advantage. A common pulse beats in the quarters of the Polish Jews and in the Mulberry Bend, though they have little else in common. Life over yonder in fine weather is a perpetual holiday, here a veritable treadmill of industry. Friday brings out all the latent color and picturesqueness of the Italians, as of these Semites. The crowds and the common poverty are the bonds of sympathy between them. The Pig-market is in Hester Street, extending either way from Ludlow Street, and up and down the side streets two or three blocks, as the state of trade demands. The name was given to it probably in derision, for pork is the one ware that is not on sale in the Pig-market. There is scarcely anything else that can be hawked from a wagon that is not to be found, and at ridiculously low prices. Bandanas and tin cups at two cents, peaches at a cent a quart, “damaged” eggs for a song, hats for a quarter, and spectacles, warranted to suit the eye, at the optician’s who has opened shop on a Hester Street doorstep, for thirty five cents; frowsy-looking chickens and half-plucked geese, hung by the neck and protesting with wildly strutting feet even in death against the outrage, are the great staple of the market. Half or a quarter of a chicken can be bought here by those who cannot afford a whole. It took more than ten years of persistent effort on the part of the sanitary authorities to drive the trade in live fowl from the streets to the fowl-market on Gouverneur Slip, where the killing is now done according to Jewish rite by priests detailed for the purpose by the chief rabbi. Since then they have had a characteristic rumpus, that involved the entire Jewish community, over the fees for killing and the mode of collecting them. Here is a woman churning horseradish on a machine she has chained and padlocked to a tree on the sidewalk, lest someone steal it. Beside her a butcher’s stand with cuts at prices the avenues never dreamed of. Old coats are hawked for fifty cents, “as good as new,” and “pants”﻿—there are no trousers in Jewtown, only pants﻿—at anything that can be got. There is a knot of half a dozen “pants” pedlars in the middle of the street, twice as many men of their own race fingering their wares and plucking at the seams with the anxious scrutiny of would-be buyers, though none of them has the least idea of investing in a pair. Yes, stop! This baker, fresh from his trough, bareheaded and with bare arms, has made an offer: for this pair thirty cents; a dollar and forty was the price asked. The pedlar shrugs his shoulders, and turns up his hands with a half pitying, wholly indignant air. What does the baker take him for? Such pants﻿—. The baker has turned to go. With a jump like a panther’s, the man with the pants has him by the sleeve. Will he give eighty cents? Sixty? Fifty? So help him, they are dirt cheap at that. Lose, will he, on the trade, lose all the profit of his day’s peddling. The baker goes on unmoved. Forty then? What, not forty? Take them then for thirty, and wreck the life of a poor man. And the baker takes them and goes, well knowing that at least twenty cents of the thirty, two hundred percent, were clear profit, if indeed the “pants” cost the pedlar anything.

===Chinese===
星期四夜里和星期五早晨，是「猪市」（Pig-market）的大减价日子。要研究这个奇特民族的门道，此时最相宜。波兰犹太人的聚居区和桑树街弯道跳动着同一支脉搏，尽管除此之外它们少有共同之处。晴朗的日子，那边的生活是一场没完没了的假日，这边却是一架不折不扣的劳作踏车。星期五把意大利人潜在的色彩与画意一并带将出来，这些闪米特人（Semites）也一样。人潮和共同的贫穷，是他们之间同情的纽带。「猪市」就在海斯特街，以勒德洛街为界向两头伸展，并顺着横街上下各占两三个街区，全看市况的需要。这名字多半是嘲弄起出来的，因为「猪市」上唯一不卖的货色就是猪肉。凡能用一辆货车叫卖的东西，这里几乎无一不备，而且价钱贱得可笑：印花大头巾和白铁杯两分钱一样，桃子一分钱一夸脱，「受损伤」的鸡蛋贱到近乎白送，帽子两毛五一顶；还有眼镜匠在海斯特街一户门阶上开张营业，三毛五一副，保准合眼。蓬头垢面的母鸡和拔了一半毛的鹅，脖子吊着，一双乱蹬的脚至死还在抗议这桩暴行——它们是这市场上的头等大宗货色。买不起整只的，在这里可以买到半只或四分之一只鸡。卫生当局花了十几年坚持不懈的努力，才把活禽买卖从街上赶进格弗努尔滑道（Gouverneur Slip）的家禽市场；如今宰杀便在那里按犹太教仪轨进行，由首席拉比指派专司此职的祭司执刀。打那以后，为着宰杀费和收费的法子，他们又闹过一场牵动整个犹太社会的、很有特色的口角。这边有个女人，在一台用链子锁在人行道树上的机器上磨辣根，防的就是有人偷。她旁边是个肉摊，分割肉的价格，低得叫大道上做梦也想不到。旧外套喊价五毛，「跟新的一样」；「裤子」（pants）——「犹太镇」没有文雅的 trousers，只有 pants——能给多少算多少。街当中聚着一小拨六七个卖「裤子」的货郎，两倍于他们的本族人摸着货色、抠着针脚，一副准买主才有的急切审视，其实谁也没有一星半点掏钱买一条的意思。对了，站住！这位面包师，刚从揉面槽边过来，光着头，光着胳膊，出了个价：这条三毛；要价可是一块四。货郎耸耸肩，双手一翻，那神气半是怜悯、全是愤慨。他当他是什么人？这样的裤子——。面包师转身要走。卖裤子的人一个豹子似的箭步蹿上去，一把拽住他的袖子。八毛肯不肯？六毛？五毛？皇天在上，这价钱简直是白捡；再让，他就要亏了，要把一天叫卖挣的利钱全亏光。面包师不为所动，抬脚就走。那么四毛？怎么，四毛也不行？那就三毛拿去，毁掉一个可怜人吧。面包师买下裤子走了，心里透亮：这三毛里至少有两毛——百分之二百——是纯利，倘若那条「裤子」当真让货郎破费过什么本钱的话。

===Original===
The suspender pedlar is the mystery of the Pig-market, omnipresent and unfathomable. He is met at every step with his wares dangling over his shoulder, down his back, and in front. Millions of suspenders thus perambulate Jewtown all day on a sort of dress parade. Why suspenders, is the puzzle, and where do they all go to? The “pants” of Jewtown hang down with a common accord, as if they had never known the support of suspenders. It appears to be as characteristic a trait of the race as the long beard and the Sabbath silk hat of ancient pedigree. I have asked again and again. No one has ever been able to tell me what becomes of the suspenders of Jewtown. Perhaps they are hung up as bric-a-brac in its homes, or laid away and saved up as the equivalent of cash. I cannot tell. I only know that more suspenders are hawked about the Pig-market every day than would supply the whole of New York for a year, were they all bought and turned to use.

===Chinese===
卖背带的货郎，是「猪市」的一大谜：无处不在，又深不可测。你步步都撞见他，货色搭在肩上、垂在背后、挂在胸前。千百万条背带就这样成天在「犹太镇」里列队游行，仿佛一场盛装阅兵。为什么偏是背带？这是个谜；它们又都到哪里去了？「犹太镇」的「裤子」却众口一词地往下坠，仿佛从来不曾领受过背带的扶持。这一点看来同长胡子、同那顶家世古老的安息日丝礼帽一样，是这个种族的一大特色。我问了一遍又一遍。从没有人能告诉我「犹太镇」的背带究竟下落如何。也许它们被当作古玩摆设挂进了各家的屋里，也许被收起来攒着，当作现金的等价物。我说不好。我只知道，每天在「猪市」上叫卖的背带，比整个纽约一年用的还多——倘若它们统统被人买去、派上用场的话。

===Original===
The crowds that jostle each other at the wagons and about the sidewalk shops, where a gutter plank on two ash-barrels does duty for a counter! Pushing, struggling, babbling, and shouting in foreign tongues, a veritable Babel of confusion. An English word falls upon the ear almost with a sense of shock, as something unexpected and strange. In the midst of it all there is a sudden wild scattering, a hustling of things from the street into dark cellars, into backyards and byways, a slamming and locking of doors hidden under the improvised shelves and counters. The health officers’ cart is coming down the street, preceded and followed by stalwart policemen, who shovel up with scant ceremony the eatables﻿—musty bread, decayed fish and stale vegetables﻿—indifferent to the curses that are showered on them from stoops and windows, and carry them off to the dump. In the wake of the wagon, as it makes its way to the East River after the raid, follow a line of despoiled hucksters shouting defiance from a safe distance. Their clamor dies away with the noise of the market. The endless panorama of the tenements, rows upon rows, between stony streets, stretches to the north, to the south, and to the west as far as the eye reaches.

===Chinese===
货车旁、人行道摊边，人群你推我搡——那摊子，不过一块檐槽木板架在两只灰渣桶上，便权充了柜台！挤着，扛着，用外国语叽里呱啦地嚷着，好一座名副其实的巴别塔，乱作一团。一个英语词落到耳朵上，几乎叫人吓一跳，仿佛什么意外又稀奇的东西。就在这乱哄哄当中，忽然一阵狂乱的四散，货色从街上飞快地挪进黑洞洞的地窖、后院和小道，藏在临时货架柜台底下的门砰然关死、上了锁。卫生官员的执法车沿街过来了，前后簇拥着魁梧的警察，他们毫不客气地铲起吃食——发霉的面包、腐烂的鱼、蔫坏的蔬菜——对门廊上、窗口里泼下来的咒骂充耳不闻，装车运往垃圾场。突击过后，执法车往东河（East River）方向驶去，车后跟着一行被抄了货的小贩，隔着安全距离高声叫阵。他们的鼓噪，随着市声一同消歇。廉租公寓的无尽画卷，一排接着一排，夹在石头街衢之间，向北、向南、向西伸展，直到目力尽头。

## XI The Sweaters of Jewtown / XI 「犹太镇」的血汗工头

===Original===
Anything like an exhaustive discussion of the economical problem presented by the Tenth Ward13 is beset by difficulties that increase in precise proportion to the efforts put forth to remove them. I have too vivid a recollection of weary days and nights spent in those stewing tenements, trying to get to the bottom of the vexatious question only to find myself in the end as far from the truth as at the beginning, asking with rising wrath Pilate’s question, “What is truth?” to attempt to weary the reader by dragging him with me over that sterile and unprofitable ground. Nor are these pages the place for such a discussion. In it, let me confess it at once and have done with it, I should be like the blind leading the blind; between the real and apparent poverty, the hidden hoards and the unhesitating mendacity of these people, where they conceive their interests to be concerned in one way or another, the reader and I would fall together into the ditch of doubt and conjecture in which I have found company before.

===Chinese===
要想对第十选区（13）（Tenth Ward）所呈现的经济问题作一番穷根究底的讨论，必然是困难重重，而且为排除困难下出的力气越大，困难反倒分毫不差地成比例增长。为把这个恼人的问题追个水落石出，我曾在那些闷热如蒸锅的廉租公寓里熬过多少疲惫的日日夜夜，到头来却发现自己离真相仍同动身时一样遥远，只能带着一阵高过一阵的怒气，问出彼拉多的那句问话——“什么是真理？”——有这样一段记忆犹新的经历，我实不愿再拖累读者，陪我在这片寸草不生、一无所获的地界上重走一遭。何况这些篇章也不是作这种讨论的场合。在这件事上，容我立刻承认、就此了结：我若妄谈，无异于盲人给盲人领路。夹在这些人真实的贫穷与表面的贫穷之间，夹在藏而不露的积蓄与毫不迟疑的谎话之间——凡在他们认定自己利益所系的地方——读者和我势必双双跌进那条怀疑与臆测的沟里去；而在那条沟里，早有先例与我作过伴了。

===Original===
The facts that lie on the surface indicate the causes as clearly as the nature of the trouble. In effect both have been already stated. A friend of mine who manufactures cloth once boasted to me that nowadays, on cheap clothing, New York “beats the world.” “To what,” I asked, “do you attribute it?” “To the cutter’s long knife14 and the Polish Jew,” he said. Which of the two has cut deepest into the workman’s wages is not a doubtful question. Practically the Jew has monopolized the business since the battle between East Broadway and Broadway ended in a complete victory for the East Side and cheap labor, and transferred to it the control of the trade in cheap clothing. Yet, not satisfied with having won the field, he strives as hotly with his own for the profit of half a cent as he fought with his Christian competitor for the dollar. If the victory is a barren one, the blame is his own. His price is not what he can get, but the lowest he can live for and underbid his neighbor. Just what that means we shall see. The manufacturer knows it, and is not slow to take advantage of his knowledge. He makes him hungry for work by keeping it from him as long as possible; then drives the closest bargain he can with the sweater.

===Chinese===
浮在表面上的事实，把这一麻烦的性质指得多清楚，就把它的成因指得多清楚。实际上，这两层意思前文都已经说过了。我有一位织布制造商朋友，有一次向我夸口，说如今在廉价服装上，纽约「打遍天下无敌手」。“你把这归功于什么呢？”我问他。“一靠裁剪刀（14），”他说，“二靠波兰犹太人。”这两样里头哪一样把工人的工资削得最深，并不是个存疑的问题。自从东百老汇（East Broadway）与百老汇（Broadway）之间的那场争夺以下东区和廉价劳工的全胜告终、把廉价服装业的控制权转到下东区手里以后，犹太人实际上已经垄断了这门生意。然而他不以夺得战场为满足，转身便同自己的同胞火辣辣地争夺半分钱的利润，那股狠劲，不亚于当年同基督教竞争者争夺每一块美元。倘若这场胜利落得一场空，罪责只能记在他自己头上。他要的价，不是他能讨到的最高价，而是他赖以活命的最低价，好把邻人的出价压下去。这究竟意味着什么，我们往下自会看到。制造商心里一清二楚，而且毫不迟延地利用这一点：他尽量把活计扣住不发，让包工头（sweater）饿着肚子等活上门，然后再同他做成一手顶顶刻薄的买卖。

===Original===
Many harsh things have been said of the “sweater,” that really apply to the system in which he is a necessary, logical link. It can at least be said of him that he is no worse than the conditions that created him. The sweater is simply the middleman, the subcontractor, a workman like his fellows, perhaps with the single distinction from the rest that he knows a little English; perhaps not even that, but with the accidental possession of two or three sewing-machines, or of credit enough to hire them, as his capital, who drums up work among the clothing-houses. Of workmen he can always get enough. Every shipload from German ports brings them to his door in droves, clamoring for work. The sun sets upon the day of the arrival of many a Polish Jew, finding him at work in an East Side tenement, treading the machine and “learning the trade.” Often there are two, sometimes three, sets of sweaters on one job. They work with the rest when they are not drumming up trade, driving their “hands” as they drive their machine, for all they are worth, and making a profit on their work, of course, though in most cases not nearly as extravagant a percentage, probably, as is often supposed. If it resolves itself into a margin of five or six cents, or even less, on a dozen pairs of boys’ trousers, for instance, it is nevertheless enough to make the contractor with his thrifty instincts independent. The workman growls, not at the hard labor, or poor pay, but over the pennies another is coining out of his sweat, and on the first opportunity turns sweater himself, and takes his revenge by driving an even closer bargain than his rival tyrant, thus reducing his profits.

===Chinese===
关于「包工头」，人们说过许多狠话，其实那些话多半该落在他所处的制度头上——他不过是这套制度里一个必然的、合乎逻辑的环节。至少有一点可以说他：他不比造就他的那些境况更坏。包工头无非是个中间人、分包商（subcontractor），一个同伙计们一样的工人，至多同旁人有一点分别：他懂几句英语；或许连这一点也没有，只是碰巧手头有两三台缝纫机，或者恰有足以租进几台的信用，便拿它当本钱，到各家服装行去兜揽活计。工人他从来不怕凑不齐。从德国各港口开来的每一船人，都把一伙一伙的工人送到他门口，嚷着要活干。多少个波兰犹太人，登岸的当天太阳还没落，就已经在下东区某座廉租公寓里踩起缝纫机「学手艺」了。一件活计上，往往套着两层、有时三层包工头。他们不去兜生意的当儿，也同大家一起做活，像踩自己的机器那样死命驱使他们的「工人」，自然也从众人的活计上落一份利；不过在大多数情形下，那利钱的成数恐怕远没有人们惯常设想的那么惊人。譬如说，哪怕一打男童裤子上只净落五六个分钱、甚至更少的利头，也尽够这位天性节俭的承包人自立门户了。工人咕哝，不是怨苦役重、工钱薄，而是怨另一个人正从他的汗水里一枚一枚铸出铜子儿；于是一有机会，他自己也摇身变作包工头，用比他的对头暴君更刻薄的一手做买卖来施行报复，把对方的利头再削掉一层。

===Original===
The sweater knows well that the isolation of the workman in his helpless ignorance is his sure foundation, and he has done what he could﻿—with merciless severity where he could﻿—to smother every symptom of awakening intelligence in his slaves. In this effort to perpetuate his despotism he has had the effectual assistance of his own system and the sharp competition that keep the men on starvation wages; of their constitutional greed, that will not permit the sacrifice of temporary advantage, however slight, for permanent good, and above all, of the hungry hordes of immigrants to whom no argument appeals save the cry for bread. Within very recent times he has, however, been forced to partial surrender by the organization of the men to a considerable extent into trades unions, and by experiments in cooperation, under intelligent leadership, that presage the sweater’s doom. But as long as the ignorant crowds continue to come and to herd in these tenements, his grip can never be shaken off. And the supply across the seas is apparently inexhaustible.
