Her father, she told the doctor, had made good wages at it; but she received only five cents for six hundred of the little three-cornered bags, and her fingers had to be very swift and handle the paste-brush very deftly to bring her earnings up to twenty-five and thirty cents a day. She paid four dollars a month for her room. The rest went to buy food for herself and the children. The physician’s purse, rather than her skill, had healing for their complaint.

===Chinese===
印花布晨衣一打 1 块 5 毛——手艺顶熟的缝衣女一天能做八到十件，寻常手艺的五到六件——领带一打 25 分到 75 分，做满一打便算一个像样的好工日——这些便是妇女工钱的样本。可人们偏偏还要纳闷，廉租公寓里做出的活计怎么那般粗劣！意大利廉价劳力近来也占据了这片可怜的田地，身后还拖着包工头。家庭之外的妇女行当里，几乎无一不是工钱早已跌到最低点，如今更跌到实实在在饿肚子的地步。最近，一位心和毕生事业都系于穷人的女医生让我注意到一桩案子：一个带着两个幼子的寡妇，被她发现正在下东区一间阁楼里糊纸袋。她告诉医生，她父亲从前干这一行挣过体面的工钱；可她糊六百只小三角袋，只挣五分钱，手指还得飞快，浆糊刷子还得使得极巧，一天的收入才上得到两毛五到三毛。她的房钱每月 4 块，剩下的钱全拿去给自己和孩子买吃食。能治她们这个病症的，是这位医生的钱袋，而不是她的医术。

===Original===
I have aimed to set down a few dry facts merely. They carry their own comment. Back of the shop with its weary, grinding toil﻿—the home in the tenement, of which it was said in a report of the State Labor Bureau: “Decency and womanly reserve cannot be maintained there﻿—what wonder so many fall away from virtue?” Of the outlook, what? Last Christmas Eve my business took me to an obscure street among the West Side tenements. An old woman had just fallen on the doorstep, stricken with paralysis. The doctor said she would never again move her right hand or foot. The whole side was dead. By her bedside, in their cheerless room, sat the patient’s aged sister, a hopeless cripple, in dumb despair. Forty years ago the sisters had come, five in number then, with their mother, from the North of Ireland to make their home and earn a living among strangers. They were lace embroiderers and found work easily at good wages. All the rest had died as the years went by. The two remained and, firmly resolved to lead an honest life, worked on though wages fell and fell as age and toil stiffened their once nimble fingers and dimmed their sight. Then one of them dropped out, her hands palsied and her courage gone. Still the other toiled on, resting neither by night nor by day, that the sister might not want. Now that she too had been stricken, as she was going to the store for the work that was to keep them through the holidays, the battle was over at last. There was before them starvation, or the poorhouse. And the proud spirits of the sisters, helpless now, quailed at the outlook.

===Chinese===
我所图的，不过是记下几条干巴巴的事实。它们自带评注。在那间令人疲惫、磨折人的苦役铺子背后，是廉租公寓里的家——州劳工局（State Labor Bureau）的一份报告里说到它：「体面与女性的矜持在那里无从维持——这么多女子失身落德，何足为奇？」至于前景，又如何呢？去年圣诞节前夜，我因事走到西区廉租公寓之间的一条僻街。一位老太太刚在门阶上倒下，中了风。医生说，她的右手右脚从此再动弹不得了。整个半边身子都死了。在那间没有一丝暖意的屋子里，病人年迈的姐姐坐在床边——一个再无指望的残废人——哑口无言地陷在绝望里。四十年前，姐妹几个——那时一共五人——随母亲从爱尔兰北部来到陌生人中间，要在这里安家、谋生。她们是花边绣工，活计好找，工钱也体面。其余几个随着岁月相继凋零。留下的这两个立志清清白白地活着，尽管工钱一跌再跌，年纪与劳苦把她们当年灵巧的手指熬僵、把她们的眼睛熬花，仍不肯撒手。后来其中一个垮了下来，双手瘫了，勇气也散了。另一个依旧苦做下去，昼夜不得歇息，只为叫姐姐不至短了什么。如今连她也倒了——倒在她去铺子取活计的路上，那活计原是要撑着她们过完节期的——这一仗终究打完了。摆在她们面前的，不是饿死，就是济贫院。姐妹俩那两颗骄傲的心，如今无计可施，对着这样的前景颤栗了。

===Original===
These were old, with life behind them. For them nothing was left but to sit in the shadow and wait. But of the thousands, who are travelling the road they trod to the end, with the hot blood of youth in their veins, with the love of life and of the beautiful world to which not even sixty cents a day can shut their eyes﻿—who is to blame if their feet find the paths of shame that are “always open to them?” The very paths that have effaced the saving “limit,” and to which it is declared to be “inevitable that they must in many instances resort.” Let the moralist answer. Let the wise economist apply his rule of supply and demand, and let the answer be heard in this city of a thousand charities where justice goes begging.

===Chinese===
这两个已经老了，一生都落在身后了。留给她们的，除了坐进阴影里等待，再没有别的。可是那成千上万的人，正沿着她们踩出的路走到头去，血管里滚着青春的热血，怀着对生命、对那个连一天六毛钱也无法叫她们闭眼不看的美妙世界的爱——她们的脚若寻着了那「永远向她们敞开」的羞耻之路，该归罪于谁？那正是抹去了那道救命的「限度」的路，也正是被宣称为「许多情形下她们势必堕入」的路。让道德家来回答吧。让聪明的经济学家搬出他的供求法则来，且让这回答，在这座慈善名目上千种、公道却只得沿门乞讨的城市里，被人听见。

===Original===
To the everlasting credit of New York’s working-girl let it be said that, rough though her road be, all but hopeless her battle with life, only in the rarest instances does she go astray. As a class she is brave, virtuous, and true. New York’s army of profligate women is not, as in some foreign cities, recruited from her ranks. She is as plucky as she is proud. That “American girls never whimper” became a proverb long ago, and she accepts her lot uncomplainingly, doing the best she can and holding her cherished independence cheap at the cost of a meal, or of half her daily ration, if need be. The home in the tenement and the traditions of her childhood have neither trained her to luxury nor predisposed her in favor of domestic labor in preference to the shop. So, to the world she presents a cheerful, uncomplaining front that sometimes deceives it. Her courage will not be without its reward. Slowly, as the conviction is thrust upon society that woman’s work must enter more and more into its planning, a better day is dawning. The organization of working girls’ clubs, unions, and societies with a community of interests, despite the obstacles to such a movement, bears testimony to it, as to the devotion of the unselfish women who have made their poorer sisters’ cause their own, and will yet wring from an unfair world the justice too long denied her.

===Chinese===
愿有一句话永远为纽约的打工女孩增光：她的路尽管崎岖，她与生活的搏斗尽管几乎无望，她误入歧途的却只是最罕见的例外。作为一个阶层，她勇敢、贞静而真诚。纽约那支堕落女子的大军，并不像在某些外国城市里那样，从她的队伍中招募兵员。她既骄傲，又刚勇。「美国姑娘从不呜咽」早成了一句谚语；她无怨无尤地领受自己的命运，竭尽所能地做事，必要时，一顿饭、或一日口粮之半，便是她为保住那份珍重独立而甘付的小小代价。廉租公寓里的家和童年的传统，既没有把她娇养成贪图安逸的性子，也没有让她宁选帮佣而不选铺子。于是，她向世人摆出一副快快活活、无怨无尤的面孔，有时竟把世人都骗过了。她的勇气不会没有报偿。随着一个信念日渐强加于社会——妇女的劳动必须越来越多地进入社会的谋划——一个更好的时日正在破晓。打工女孩们那些利益与共的俱乐部、工会和协会的组织，尽管这类运动阻力重重，却正为这一点作证，也为那些无私女性的奉献作证：她们把穷姐妹的事业当作了自己的事业，终将从这不公的世界里，为她拧出那被拖欠得太久的公道来。

## XXI Pauperism in the Tenements / XXI 廉租公寓里的贫困化

===Original===
The reader who has followed with me the fate of the Other Half thus far, may not experience much of a shock at being told that in eight years 135,595 families in New York were registered as asking or receiving charity. Perhaps, however, the intelligence will rouse him that for five years past one person in every ten who died in this city was buried in the Potter’s Field. These facts tell a terrible story. The first means that in a population of a million and a half, very nearly, if not quite, half a million persons were driven, or chose, to beg for food, or to accept it in charity at some period of the eight years, if not during the whole of it. There is no mistake about these figures. They are drawn from the records of the Charity Organization Society, and represent the time during which it has been in existence. It is not even pretended that the record is complete. To be well within the limits, the Society’s statisticians allow only three and a half to the family, instead of the four and a half that are accepted as the standard of calculations which deal with New York’s population as a whole. They estimate upon the basis of their everyday experience that, allowing for those who have died, moved away, or become for the time being at least self-supporting, eighty-five percent of the registry are still within, or lingering upon, the borders of dependence. Precisely how the case stands with this great horde of the indigent is shown by a classification of 5,169 cases that were investigated by the Society in one year. This was the way it turned out: 327 worthy of continuous relief, or 6.4 percent; 1,269 worthy of temporary relief, or 24.4 percent; 2,698 in need of work, rather than relief, or 52.2 percent; 875 unworthy of relief, or 17 percent.

===Chinese===
一路跟随我考察「另一半人」命运的读者，听到纽约在八年里有 135,595 户人家登记在册、申请或接受过慈善救济，也许不会感到多大的震动。然而另一个消息兴许会让他警醒：过去五年里，本市每十个死者当中，就有一人葬入乱葬岗（Potter’s Field）。这两桩事实道出了一个可怕的故事。前者意味着，在一百五十万人口当中，即使不足、也几乎有五十万人在那八年中的某一段时期——若非全程——被迫或自愿地去乞讨口粮，或接受慈善施舍。这些数字不会有错。它们取自慈善组织协会（Charity Organization Society）的档案，所涵盖的正是该会成立以来的年月。甚至没有人假称这份记录已经完备。为了稳妥地留有余地，该会的统计人员计算户均规模时只按每户三口半计，而不采用四口半——后者才是涉及纽约全市人口的计算所通行的标准。他们根据自己日常工作的经验估计：即便扣除已经亡故、迁走，或至少一时已能自食其力的人，登记在册者当中仍有百分之85处于依赖救济的境地，或在这一边缘徘徊。这庞大一群穷困者的实际境况究竟如何，从该会一年之内经手的 5,169 个案例的分类中可以看得一清二楚。分类结果如下：327 例值得长期救济，合百分之6.4；1,269 例值得临时救济，合百分之24.4；2,698 例需要的不是救济而是工作，合百分之52.2；875 例不值得救济，合百分之17。

===Original===
That is, nearly six and a half percent, of all were utterly helpless﻿—orphans, cripples, or the very aged; nearly one-fourth needed just a lift to start them on the road of independence, or of permanent pauperism, according to the wisdom with which the lever was applied. More than half were destitute because they had no work and were unable to find any, and one-sixth were frauds, professional beggars, training their children to follow in their footsteps﻿—a veritable “tribe of Ishmael,” tightening its grip on society as the years pass, until society shall summon up pluck to say with Paul, “if any man will not work neither shall he eat,” and stick to it. It is worthy of note that almost precisely the same results followed a similar investigation in Boston. There were a few more helpless cases of the sort true charity accounts it a gain to care for, but the proportion of a given lot that was crippled for want of work, or unworthy, was exactly the same as in this city. The bankrupt in hope, in courage, in purse, and in purpose, are not peculiar to New York. They are found the world over, but we have our full share. If further proof were wanted, it is found in the prevalence of pauper burials. The Potter’s Field stands ever for utter, hopeless surrender. The last the poor will let go, however miserable their lot in life, is the hope of a decent burial. But for the five years ending with 1888 the average of burials in the Potter’s Field has been 10.03 percent of all. In 1889 it was 9.64. In that year the proportion to the total mortality of those who died in hospitals, institutions, and in the Almshouse was as 1 in 5.

===Chinese===
这就是说，占总数将近百分之6.5的人全然无告——孤儿、残疾者，或风烛残年的老人；将近四分之一的人只消推上一把，便可上路——通向自立之路，或通向永久的贫民化之路，全看这一杠杆用得明智与否。一半以上的人陷于穷困，是因为没有工作，又寻不见工作；六分之一是骗子，是职业乞丐，训练自家孩子步其后尘——一个不折不扣的「以实玛利部落」（tribe of Ishmael），年复一年把社会越攥越紧，直到社会鼓起胆量，同保罗（Paul）一道说出「若有人不肯做工，就不可吃饭」，并且坚持到底。值得注意的是，波士顿（Boston）一次同类调查所得的结果几乎分毫不差。那边全然无告者的案例略多一些——真正的慈善原把照看这一类人算作一桩收获——但每一批人当中，因无工可做而陷于困顿者、以及不配受救济者所占的比例，与本市恰相同。在希望上、勇气上、钱袋上、志向上都破了产的人，并非纽约独有的产物。普天之下哪里都有，只是我们也占足了自家的份额。若还需进一步的证明，济贫葬礼之盛行便是明证。乱葬岗永远意味着彻底的、无可救药的屈服。穷人在世无论命运多惨，最后才肯撒手的，是入土为安的一线指望。而截至1888年的五年间，葬入乱葬岗者平均占全部葬礼的百分之10.03。1889年为百分之9.64。那一年，死于医院、各类收容机构及救济院（Almshouse）者，占总死亡人数的五分之一。

===Original===
The 135,595 families inhabited no fewer than 31,000 different tenements. I say tenements advisedly, though the society calls them buildings, because at least ninety-nine percent were found in the big barracks, the rest in shanties scattered here and there, and now and then a fraud or an exceptional case of distress in a dwelling-house of better class. Here, undoubtedly, allowance must be made for the constant moving about of those who live on charity, which enables one active beggar to blacklist a dozen houses in the year. Still the great mass of the tenements are shown to be harboring alms-seekers. They might almost as safely harbor the smallpox. That scourge is not more contagious than the alms-seeker’s complaint. There are houses that have been corrupted through and through by this pestilence, until their very atmosphere breathes beggary. More than a hundred and twenty pauper families have been reported from time to time as living in one such tenement.

===Chinese===
这 135,595 户人家散居在不少于31,000 座不同的廉租公寓里。我特意说「廉租公寓」，尽管该会称它们为「房屋」，因为其中至少百分之99见于那些大兵营式的公寓楼，其余散落在各处的棚屋里，偶尔才有骗子或个别特例的困厄者现身于高一等的独户住宅。这里自然要把靠慈善度日者频繁搬迁的因素计算在内——一个腿脚勤快的乞丐，一年之内就能让一打房子挂上黑名单。尽管如此，绝大部分廉租公寓仍被查明窝藏着乞讨者。它们窝藏天花，几乎也不会更危险多少。那场瘟疫的传染性，未必强过乞讨者身上这种毛病。有些房屋已被这场疫病里里外外腐蚀了个透，连屋里的空气都在散发乞讨的气息。据报道，曾有一座这样的廉租公寓里，先后住着一百二十多户贫民。

===Original===
The truth is that pauperism grows in the tenements as naturally as weeds in a garden lot. A moral distemper, like crime, it finds there its most fertile soil. All the surroundings of tenement-house life favor its growth, and where once it has taken root it is harder to dislodge than the most virulent of physical diseases. The thief is infinitely easier to deal with than the pauper, because the very fact of his being a thief presupposes some bottom to the man. Granted that it is bad, there is still something, a possible handle by which to catch him. To the pauper there is none. He is as hopeless as his own poverty. I speak of the *pauper*, not of the honestly poor. There is a sharp line between the two; but athwart it stands the tenement, all the time blurring and blotting it out. “It all comes down to character in the end,” was the verdict of a philanthropist whose life has been spent wrestling with this weary problem. And so it comes down to the tenement, the destroyer of individuality and character everywhere. “In nine years,” said a wise and charitable physician, sadly, to me, “I have known of but a single case of permanent improvement in a poor tenement family.” I have known of some, whose experience, extending over an even longer stretch, was little better.

===Chinese===
事实是，贫民化在廉租公寓里滋长，就像园圃里长杂草一样自然。它是一种道德上的失调，同犯罪一样，在那里找到了自己最肥沃的土壤。廉租公寓生活的一切环境都有利于它生长；而它一旦扎根，就比最恶性的身体疾病更难铲除。窃贼比贫民容易对付得多，因为「他是个窃贼」这一事实本身，就预设了此人还有几分底子。就算那底子是坏的，总还有点什么，还有一个可以下手抓他的把柄。对贫民，却什么把柄也没有。他同他自己的贫穷一样无药可救。我说的是*贫民*（pauper），不是老老实实的穷人。两者之间横着一道分明的界线；可廉租公寓偏偏横亘在这道界线上，时时刻刻把它涂抹得模糊不清。「归根结底，一切都归结到品性上」，一位毕生同这个令人厌倦的问题缠斗的慈善家如此断言。于是，一切都归结到廉租公寓——这个到处毁灭个性与品性的东西。「九年里」，一位睿智而仁厚的医生哀伤地对我说，「我只见过一户贫穷的廉租公寓人家得到永久改善的例子。」我也认识一些人，行医的年头拉得更长，所见却也好不了多少。

===Original===
The beggar follows the “tough’s” rule of life that the world owes him a living, but his scheme of collecting it stops short of violence. He has not the pluck to rob even a drunken man. His highest flights take in at most an unguarded clothesline, or a little child sent to buy bread or beer with the pennies he clutches tightly as he skips along. Even then he prefers to attain his end by stratagem rather than by force, though occasionally, when the coast is clear, he rises to the height of the bully. The ways he finds of “collecting” under the cloak of undeserved poverty are numberless, and often reflect credit on the man’s ingenuity, if not on the man himself. I remember the shock with which my first experience with his kind﻿—her kind, rather, in this case: the beggar was a woman﻿—came home to me. On my way to and from the office I had been giving charity regularly, as I fondly believed, to an old woman who sat in Chatham Square with a baby done up in a bundle of rags, moaning piteously in sunshine and rain, “Please, help the poor.” It was the baby I pitied and thought I was doing my little to help, until one night I was just in time to rescue it from rolling out of her lap, and found the bundle I had been wasting my pennies upon just rags and nothing more, and the old hag dead drunk. Since then I have encountered bogus babies, borrowed babies, and drugged babies in the streets, and fought shy of them all. Most of them, I am glad to say, have been banished from the street since; but they are still occasionally to be found. It was only last winter that the officers of the Society for the Prevention of Cruelty to Children arrested an Italian woman who was begging along Madison Avenue with a poor little wreck of a girl, whose rags and pinched face were calculated to tug hard at the purse-strings of a miser. Over five dollars in nickles and pennies were taken from the woman’s pockets, and when her story of poverty and hunger was investigated at the family’s home in a Baxter Street tenement, bankbooks turned up that showed the Masonis to be regular pauper capitalists, able to draw their check for three thousand dollars, had they been so disposed. The woman was fined $250, a worse punishment undoubtedly than to have sent her to prison for the rest of her natural life. Her class has, unhappily, representatives in New York that have not yet been brought to grief.

===Chinese===
乞丐信奉「恶棍」的那条人生准则——世界欠他一口饭吃，只是他讨债的盘算止步于暴力之前。他连抢劫一个醉汉的胆量都没有。他胆子最大的时候，至多敢打一条无人看管的晾衣绳的主意，或是打一个小孩的主意——那孩子被差去买面包或啤酒，一路蹦跳，把钱攥得紧紧的。即便在这种时候，他也宁用计谋、不肯用力气；不过偶尔四下无人时，他也能升起几分恶霸的气概。他打着「不该受的穷」的幌子四处「收账」的花样数不胜数，常常叫人不得不佩服此人的机巧——如果不是佩服此人的话。我还记得头一回领教这类角色时所受的震动——更确切地说，是这一类中的「她」：那乞丐是个女人。上下班路上，我曾照例施舍给一个坐在查塔姆广场（Chatham Square）的老妇——她怀里抱着一个用破布裹成的襁褓，日头下也好、雨里也好，哀哀切切地呻吟着：「行行好，救救穷人吧。」我可怜的是那个婴儿，也自以为尽了绵薄之力；直到一天夜里，我恰巧赶得上从她怀里往下滚的一瞬间把那襁褓抢救下来，才发现我一直浪费铜板的那个包袱里只有破布，别的什么也没有，而那老妖婆早已烂醉如泥。自那以后，我在街上遇到过假婴儿、借来的婴儿、灌了药的婴儿，从此见了他们一概躲着走。我很欣慰地说，这类把戏大多已从街头绝迹；但偶尔仍能撞见。就在去年冬天，防止虐待儿童协会（Society for the Prevention of Cruelty to Children）的稽查员逮捕了一个沿麦迪逊大道（Madison Avenue）行乞的意大利女人，她带着一个可怜的小残骸似的女孩，那孩子的破衣烂衫和瘦削的小脸，就是算计好了要去狠狠拽一拽守财奴的钱袋绳。从女人口袋里搜出了五美元还多的镍币和铜币；等到她那贫困饥饿的哭诉，在她位于巴克斯特街一座廉租公寓的家中得到核查时，翻出来的银行存折表明，马索尼一家（the Masonis）是不折不扣的贫民资本家，只要愿意，开一张三千美元的支票毫无问题。那女人被罚款 250 美元——这无疑比把她送进监狱关到寿终正寝还要狠的惩罚。不幸的是，她这一行当在纽约还有尚未栽跟头的同道。

===Original===
Nothing short of making street begging a crime has availed to clear our city of this pest to an appreciable extent. By how much of an effort this result has been accomplished may be gleaned from the fact that the Charity Organization Society alone, in five years, caused the taking up of 2,594 street beggars, and the arrest and conviction of 1,474 persistent offenders. Last year it dealt with 612 perambulating mendicants. The police report only 19 arrests for begging during the year 1889, but the real facts of the case are found under the heading “vagrancy.” In all, 2,633 persons were charged with this offence, 947 of them women. A goodly proportion of these latter came from the low groggeries of the Tenth Ward, where a peculiar variety of the female tramp-beggar is at home, the “scrub.” The scrub is one degree perhaps above the average pauper in this, that she is willing to work at least one day in the week, generally the Jewish Sabbath. The orthodox Jew can do no work of any sort from Friday evening till sunset on Saturday, and this interim the scrub fills out in Ludlow Street. The pittance she receives for this vicarious sacrifice of herself upon the altar of the ancient faith buys her rum for at least two days of the week at one of the neighborhood “morgues.” She lives through the other four by begging. There are distilleries in Jewtown, or just across its borders, that depend almost wholly on her custom. Recently, when one in Hester Street was raided because the neighbors had complained of the boisterous hilarity of the hags over their beer, thirty-two aged “scrubs” were marched off to the station-house.

===Chinese===
唯有把街头行乞定为犯罪，才在可观的程度上肃清了本市这一祸害。这一成果花了多大力气，从下面的事实可见一斑：单是慈善组织协会一家，五年之内就促使2,594 名街头乞丐被收容，1,474 名屡教不改者被逮捕定罪。去年一年，它处理了612 名走街串巷的乞讨者。警察方面只报告了1889 年全年 19 起行乞被捕案，但案子的实情要到「流浪罪」的名目底下去找。以这项罪名被指控的共 2,633 人，其中947 名是妇女。后一部分人中有相当一批来自第十选区的下等酒窟，那里盘踞着女性流浪乞讨者中的一个特殊变种——「擦洗婆」（scrub）。擦洗婆或许比寻常贫民高出一档，就在于她肯一星期干上至少一天活，通常是在犹太安息日（Jewish Sabbath）。正统的犹太教徒从星期五傍晚到星期六日落什么活都不能干，这段空当便由擦洗婆在勒德洛街（Ludlow Street）补上。她把自己替代性地祭上古老信仰的祭坛换来那点微薄酬劳，够她在附近哪家「陈尸所」（“morgues”）里买上至少两天的朗姆酒。余下四天，她靠行乞活命。「犹太镇」（Jewtown）境内或紧贴其边界的地方，有几家蒸馏酒坊几乎全指望她的光顾。不久前，海斯特街（Hester Street）上有一家因为四邻抱怨那帮老妖婆对着啤酒喧哗取乐而遭查抄，三十二个上了年纪的「擦洗婆」被押进了警察分局。

===Original===
It is curious to find preconceived notions quite upset in a review of the nationalities that go to make up this squad of street beggars. The Irish head the list with fifteen percent, and the native American is only a little way behind with twelve percent, while the Italian, who in his own country turns beggary into a fine art, has less than two percent. Eight percent were Germans. The relative prevalence of the races in our population does not account for this showing. Various causes operate, no doubt, to produce it. Chief among them is, I think, the tenement itself. It has no power to corrupt the Italian, who comes here in almost every instance to work﻿—no beggar would ever emigrate from anywhere unless forced to do so. He is distinctly on its lowest level from the start. With the Irishman the case is different. The tenement, especially its lowest type, appears to possess a peculiar affinity for the worse nature of the Celt, to whose best and strongest instincts it does violence, and soonest and most thoroughly corrupts him. The “native” twelve percent represent the result of this process, the hereditary beggar of the second or third generation in the slums.

===Chinese===
把组成这支街头乞丐大军的各个民族清点一遍，会发现先入之见被完全推翻，颇有意味。爱尔兰人以百分之15居于榜首，土生美国人不远不近地跟在后面，占百分之12；而在本国把乞讨经营成一门精美艺术的意大利人，所占还不到百分之2。百分之8是德国人。诸种族在本市人口中的相对多寡，解释不了这份成绩单。无疑是种种原因共同作用，才造成了这个结果，而其中首要的，我想，正是廉租公寓本身。它腐蚀不了意大利人——后者来这里几乎无一例外是为了做工——任何乞丐都绝不会背井离乡移民他处，除非被逼到那份上。他从一开始就分明处在廉租公寓的最底层。爱尔兰人的情况则不同。廉租公寓，尤其是其最低劣的类型，似乎对凯尔特人（Celt）天性中较坏的那一面有一种特殊的亲和力，对其最好、最强健的本能横加摧折，把人败坏得也最快、最彻底。那百分之12的「土生」者，代表的正是这一过程的结果——贫民窟里第二第三代世袭的乞丐。

===Original===
The blind beggar alone is winked at in New York’s streets, because the authorities do not know what else to do with him. There is no provision for him anywhere after he is old enough to strike out for himself. The annual pittance of thirty or forty dollars which he receives from the city serves to keep his landlord in good humor; for the rest his misfortune and his thin disguise of selling pencils on the street corners must provide. Until the city affords him some systematic way of earning his living by work (as Philadelphia has done, for instance) to banish him from the street would be tantamount to sentencing him to death by starvation. So he possesses it in peace, that is, if he is blind in good earnest, and begs without “encumbrance.” Professional mendicancy does not hesitate to make use of the greatest of human afflictions as a pretence for enlisting the sympathy upon which it thrives. Many New Yorkers will remember the French schoolmaster who was “blinded by a shell at the siege of Paris,” but miraculously recovered his sight when arrested and deprived of his children by the officers of Mr. Gerry’s society. When last heard of he kept a “museum” in Hartford, and acted the overseer with financial success. His sign with its pitiful tale, that was a familiar sight in our streets for years and earned for him the capital upon which he started his business, might have found a place among the curiosities exhibited there, had it not been kept in a different sort of museum here as a memento of his rascality. There was another of his tribe, a woman, who begged for years with a deformed child in her arms, which she was found to have hired at an almshouse in Genoa for fifteen francs a month. It was a good investment, for she proved to be possessed of a comfortable fortune. Some time before that, the Society for the Prevention of Cruelty to Children, that found her out, had broken up the dreadful padrone system, a real slave trade in Italian children, who were bought of poor parents across the sea and made to beg their way on foot through France to the port whence they were shipped to this city, to be beaten and starved here by their cruel masters and sent out to beg, often after merciless mutilation to make them “take” better with a pitying public.

===Chinese===
唯独盲乞丐在纽约街头被睁一只眼闭一只眼，因为当局不知道拿他还能有什么别的办法。他一到可以自谋出路的年纪，便无处再有任何安置。他每年从市里领到的三四十美元的微薄津贴，只够让房东保持好脾气；其余的生计，就得靠他的不幸遭际，加上街头巷尾卖铅笔的这一层薄薄幌子去张罗了。在市政为他提供某种靠劳动谋生的系统办法之前（比如费城（Philadelphia）已有的那种），把他赶出街头就等于判他饿死。于是他便安安稳稳地占着街头——当然，前提是他真的瞎了，而且行乞时不带「拖累」。职业乞讨这一行从不犹豫，把人类最大的苦难用作幌子，去征募它赖以为生的同情。许多纽约人还记得那个法国小学教员，自称「在巴黎围城战中被一发炮弹夺去了视力」，可当他被捕、他带的孩子们被格里先生（Mr. Gerry）的协会稽查员带走时，他的视力又奇迹般地复了原。最后一次得到他的消息时，他在哈特福德（Hartford）开了一家「博物馆」，摇身当起监工来，进账颇为可观。他那块写着凄惨故事的牌子，多年里是我们街头熟悉的景致，也为他挣下了创业的本钱——它本可以在那家博物馆的展品中占一席之地，若不是被留在了此地另一类博物馆里，作为他奸猾行径的纪念。他那一行里还有一个女人，多年来怀抱着一个畸形儿行乞，后来查明，那孩子是她从热那亚（Genoa）一家救济院按每月十五法郎雇来的。这是桩划算的投资，因为她名下已被证明有一笔相当可观的财产。在那之前一些时候，正是查出她底细的防止虐待儿童协会，粉碎了骇人听闻的「帕德罗内」制度（padrone system）——一场真正的意大利儿童奴隶贸易：孩子们从海那边穷苦父母手中被买来，被迫一路徒步穿行法国、一路行乞，走到把他们装船运来本市的港口，再在这里挨残暴主人的毒打与饿馁，被赶出去乞讨——往往还要先被无情地弄成残废，好在心软的公众那里更「吃得开」。

===Original===
But, after all, the tenement offers a better chance of fraud on impulsive but thoughtless charity, than all the wretchedness of the street, and with fewer risks. To the tenderhearted and unwary it is, in itself, the strongest plea for help. When such a cry goes up as was heard recently from a Mott Street den, where the family of a “sick” husband, a despairing mother, and half a dozen children in rags and dirt were destitute of the “first necessities of life,” it is not to be wondered at that a stream of gold comes pouring in to relieve. It happens too often, as in that case, that a little critical inquiry or reference to the “black list” of the Charity Organization Society, justly dreaded only by the frauds, discovers the “sickness” to stand for laziness, and the destitution to be the family’s stock in trade; and the community receives a shock that for once is downright wholesome, if it imposes a check on an undiscriminating charity that is worse than none at all.

===Chinese===
但说到底，比起街头的一切惨状，廉租公寓为欺诈那种一时冲动而欠思量的慈善提供了更好的机会，风险也更小。在心软而不设防的人眼里，廉租公寓本身就是最响亮的求救呼吁。最近从莫特街（Mott Street）一处廉租窟里传出的那一声呼喊便是如此：「病」丈夫的一家、绝望的母亲、六七个衣衫褴褛满身污垢的孩子，缺了「生活的第一必需品」——闻者无不倾泻金流前去周济，这毫不奇怪。然而这类事往往像那桩案子一样，稍加挑剔的查问，或对照一下慈善组织协会的「黑名单」——那名单只有骗子才怕得有理——便会发现「疾病」原来是懒惰，「赤贫」原来是这一家的看家营生；而公众所受的这一记冲击，倘若能刹住那种不分青红皂白的施舍——那种慈善比全不给还糟——倒是一次难得的有益震动。

===Original===
The case referred to furnished an apt illustration of how thoroughly corrupting pauperism is in such a setting. The tenement woke up early to the gold mine that was being worked under its roof, and before the day was three hours old the stream of callers who responded to the newspaper appeal found the alley blocked by a couple of “toughs,” who exacted toll of a silver quarter from each tearful sympathizer with the misery in the attic.

===Chinese===
上面提到的这桩案子，恰可说明贫民化在这种环境里的腐蚀何等彻底。廉租公寓早早醒悟到自家屋顶下正开采着一座金矿；那天还没过三个钟头，响应报纸呼吁而涌来的探望人流就发现，巷子已被两个「恶棍」把住，向每一个为阁楼上的苦难落泪的同情者强收两角五分银币的买路钱。

===Original===
A volume might be written about the tricks of the professional beggar, and the uses to which he turns the tenement in his trade. The Boston “widow” whose husband turned up alive and well after she had buried him seventeen times with tears and lamentation, and made the public pay for the weekly funerals, is not without representatives in New York. The “gentleman tramp” is a familiar type from our streets, and the “once respectable Methodist” who patronized all the revivals in town with his profitable story of repentance, only to fall from grace into the saloon door nearest the church after the service was over, merely transferred the scene of his operations from the tenement to the church as the proper setting for his specialty. There is enough of real suffering in the homes of the poor to make one wish that there were some effective way of enforcing Paul’s plan of starving the drones into the paths of self-support: no work, nothing to eat.

===Chinese===
职业乞丐的种种伎俩，以及他在这一行里如何利用廉租公寓，足可写成一厚本书。波士顿那位「寡妇」，哭哭啼啼、哀哀切切地把丈夫下葬了十七回之后，丈夫却活蹦乱跳地现了身，每周的丧事全叫公众掏钱——这样的人在纽约也不乏传人。「绅士流浪汉」（“gentleman tramp”）是我们街面上的熟面孔；那位「曾经体面的卫理公会教徒」（Methodist），挟着他那桩生利的忏悔故事光顾全城每一场奋兴布道会，待礼拜一散，便从恩典中跌落，一头栽进离教堂最近的酒馆门里——他不过是把营生的舞台从廉租公寓搬到了教堂，好给他的绝活配上合适的布景。穷人家里真实的苦难已经够多了，足够叫人盼望有某种切实有效的办法，来推行保罗的计划，把雄蜂饿上自食其力的正路：不做工，就没饭吃。

===Original===
The message came from one of the Health Department’s summer doctors, last July, to the King’s Daughters’ Tenement-house Committee, that a family with a sick child was absolutely famishing in an uptown tenement. The address was not given. The doctor had forgotten to write it down, and before he could be found and a visitor sent to the house the baby was dead, and the mother had gone mad. The nurse found the father, who was an honest laborer long out of work, packing the little corpse in an orange-box partly filled with straw, that he might take it to the Morgue for pauper burial. There was absolutely not a crust to eat in the house, and the other children were crying for food. The great immediate need in that case, as in more than half of all according to the record, was work and living wages. Alms do not meet the emergency at all. They frequently aggravate it, degrading and pauperizing where true help should aim at raising the sufferer to self-respect and self-dependence. The experience of the Charity Organization Society in raising, in eight years, 4,500 families out of the rut of pauperism into proud, if modest, independence, without alms, but by a system of “friendly visitation,” and the work of the Society for Improving the Condition of the Poor and kindred organizations along the same line, shows what can be done by well-directed effort. It is estimated that New York spends in public and private charity every year a round $8,000,000. A small part of this sum intelligently invested in a great labor bureau, that would bring the seeker of work and the one with work to give together under auspices offering some degree of mutual security, would certainly repay the amount of the investment in the saving of much capital now worse than wasted, and would be prolific of the best results. The ultimate and greatest need, however, the real remedy, is to remove the cause﻿—the tenement that was built for “a class of whom nothing was expected,” and which has come fully up to the expectation. Tenement-house reform holds the key to the problem of pauperism in the city. We can never get rid of either the tenement or the pauper. The two will always exist together in New York. But by reforming the one, we can do more toward exterminating the other than can be done by all other means together that have yet been invented, or ever will be.

===Chinese===
去年七月，卫生局（Health Department）的一名「夏季医生」给「国王之女会」（King’s Daughters）廉租公寓委员会送去口信：上城一座廉租公寓里有一户人家，孩子病着，全家正濒临饿死的绝境。地址没有留下。医生忘了写，等找到他、再派探访员赶到那户人家时，婴儿已经死了，母亲也疯了。护士找到那位父亲——一个失业已久的老实劳工——他正把小小的尸体装进一个半填着稻草的橘子箱，好送去陈尸所（the Morgue）作济贫葬礼。家里实在连一口吃的都没有，其余的孩子饿得直哭。这桩案子里的燃眉之急——登记册上半数以上的案子也是如此——是工作，是养家糊口的工资。施舍根本应付不了这种急难，反倒常常使之加剧：真正的扶助应当着眼于把受苦的人扶到自尊与自立，施舍却在贬损人、把人往贫民化的路上推。慈善组织协会八年之间不靠施舍，而凭一套「友好探访」（“friendly visitation”）制度，把 4,500 户人家从贫民化的车辙里拉了出来，扶上虽不宽裕却足以自豪的自立之路；贫民状况改善协会（Society for Improving the Condition of the Poor）及同类组织沿同一方向的努力，也昭示了方向端正的努力能有何等作为。据估计，纽约每年在公共与私人慈善上足足花去 8,000,000 美元。把这笔钱的一小部分明智地投给一所大型劳工介绍所（labor bureau），让找活干的人和有活可给的人在能提供某种相互保障的机制下碰头，单是从如今近乎白白糟蹋的大量资金中省下的数目，就必定能抵偿这笔投资，更会结出最好的果实。然而归根结底最大的需要、真正的救治，是铲除病根——那座为「一个不被寄予任何期望的阶级」盖起来的廉租公寓：它倒是完全达到了这一期望。廉租公寓改革握着本市贫民化问题的钥匙。廉租公寓也好，贫民也好，我们永远摆脱不了其中任何一个。这两者在纽约将永远并存。但只要革新前者，我们在根除后者上所能做到的，就超过迄今发明过、或今后将发明的一切其他手段的总和。

## XXII The Wrecks and the Waste / XXII 沉沦者与虚耗

===Original===
Pauperdom is to blame for the unjust yoking of poverty with punishment, “charities” with “correction,” in our municipal ministering to the needs of the Nether Half. The shadow of the workhouse points like a scornful finger toward its neighbor, the almshouse, when the sun sets behind the teeming city across the East River, as if, could its stones speak, it would say before night drops its black curtain between them: “You and I are brothers. I am not more bankrupt in moral purpose than you. A common parent begat us. Twin breasts, the tenement and the saloon, nourished us. Vice and unthrift go hand in hand. Pauper, behold thy brother!” And the almshouse owns the bitter relationship in silence.

===Chinese===
贫民界（Pauperdom）难辞其咎：在我们市政当局照拂「下一半人」（the Nether Half）之需的种种安排中，贫穷与惩罚、「慈善」与「惩戒」被不公正地拴作了一处。每当太阳落到东河（East River）对岸人烟稠密的城市背后，济贫院（workhouse）的影子便像一根轻蔑的手指，指向它的邻居救济院（almshouse），仿佛它的砖石若能开口，要在夜幕将黑幕垂落在两者之间之前说道：“你我本是兄弟。论道德宗旨之破产，我并不比你更甚。我们由同一位父母所生。廉租公寓与酒馆这对孪生的乳房哺育了我们。罪恶与败家携手同行。贫民啊，看哪，你的兄弟！”而救济院默默认下了这层苦涩的亲缘。

===Original===
Over on the islands that lie strung along the river and far up the Sound the Nether Half hides its deformity, except on show-days, when distinguished visitors have to be entertained and the sore is uncovered by the authorities with due municipal pride in the exhibit. I shall spare the reader the sight. The aim of these pages has been to lay bare its source. But a brief glance at our proscribed population is needed to give background and tone to the picture. The review begins with the Charity Hospital with its thousand helpless human wrecks; takes in the penitentiary, where the “tough” from Battle Row and Poverty Gap is made to earn behind stone walls the living the world owes him; a thoughtless, jolly convict-band with opportunity at last “to think” behind the iron bars, but little desire to improve it; governed like unruly boys, which in fact most of them are. Three of them were taken from the dinner-table while I was there one day, for sticking pins into each other, and were set with their faces to the wall in sight of six hundred of their comrades for punishment. Pleading incessantly for tobacco, when the keeper’s back is turned, as the next best thing to the whiskey they cannot get, though they can plainly make out the saloon-signs across the stream where they robbed or “slugged” their way to prison. Every once in a while the longing gets the best of some prisoner from the penitentiary or the workhouse, and he risks his life in the swift currents to reach the goal that tantalizes him with the promise of “just one more drunk.” The chances are at least even of his being run down by some passing steamer and drowned, even if he is not overtaken by the armed guards who patrol the shore in boats, or his strength does not give out.

===Chinese===
在沿河串成一线、一直绵延到海峡（the Sound）深处的那些岛上，「下一半人」藏起了自身的畸形——唯有「开放日」例外：那一天要款待尊贵的来客，当局便怀着对这项展品应有的市政自豪，把疮疤揭开示众。这番景象，笔者就免了读者这一趟。本书这些篇章的宗旨，在于揭开它的根源。但要给这幅图景补出背景与色调，还须对我们这群遭放逐的人匆匆一瞥。这一巡视从慈善医院（Charity Hospital）开始，院中躺着上千名无依无靠的沉沦者；再把监狱（penitentiary）纳入行程——来自「厮杀排」（Battle Row）与「贫困谷」（Poverty Gap）的「恶棍」在石墙之内被迫去挣那份世界欠他的生计；这是一伙没心没肺、乐呵呵的囚犯，终于得了机会在铁栏后面“思考”，却没什么心思把这机会派上用场；他们像一帮不服管教的男孩似的被管束着——事实上他们大半也确实是男孩。有一天我在那里，饭桌上就有三个因为互别大头针被带走，罚他们面壁而立，当着六百名同伙的面示众。看守一转过身，他们就缠着讨烟抽，当作那得不到的威士忌的次好替身——虽说隔着一条河，那些酒馆的招牌他们望得一清二楚，他们正是在那边靠抢劫或“打闷棍”挣进了这座监狱。隔三差五，这股馋劲便压倒了监狱或济贫院里的某个犯人，他拿性命去闯湍急的水流，奔向那个拿“再醉一回”的许诺撩拨他的目标。即便不曾在驾艇沿河巡缉的武装看守手里被截获，气力也尚未耗尽，他被某艘过往汽船撞翻溺毙的可能也至少各占一半。

===Original===
This workhouse comes next, with the broken-down hordes from the dives, the lodging-houses, and the tramps’ nests, the “hell-box”22 rather than the repair-shop of the city. In 1889 the registry at the workhouse footed up 22,477, of whom some had been there as many as twenty times before. It is the popular summer resort of the slums, but business is brisk at this stand the year round. Not a few of its patrons drift back periodically without the formality of a commitment, to take their chances on the island when there is no escape from the alternative of work in the city. Work, but not too much work, is the motto of the establishment. The “workhouse step” is an institution that must be observed on the island, in order to draw any comparison between it and the snail’s pace that shall do justice to the snail. Nature and man’s art have made these islands beautiful; but weeds grow luxuriantly in their gardens, and spiders spin their cobwebs unmolested in the borders of sweet-smelling box. The work which two score of hired men could do well is too much for these thousands.

===Chinese===
接下来轮到济贫院，连同从酒窟、廉价寄宿所和流浪汉巢穴里垮下来的那一群群人——它是这座城市的一只「废铅字箱」（hell-box）（22），而不是修字车间。1889 年，济贫院的登记总数达 22,477 人次，其中有些人先前已来过足足二十次。它是贫民窟里众望所归的消夏胜地，不过这个摊子一年到头生意兴隆。它的主顾当中，不少人不时自行飘回岛来，连押送收容的官样手续也一并免了：城里既逃不开「做工」这个选项，便到岛上去碰碰运气。要劳动，但不要太多劳动，是这家机构的座右铭。「济贫院步」（workhouse step）是岛上必须见识一番的制度：唯有见识过，才谈得上拿它同蜗牛的步速作一比较，而这一比较还不致冤枉了蜗牛。大自然与人的手艺把这些岛屿装点得十分美丽；可是园中杂草葳蕤，蜘蛛在芬芳的黄杨篱边安然结网，无惊无扰。雇上四十名工人便能做好的活计，对这几千人来说却未免太多。

===Original===
Rows of old women, some smoking stumpy, black clay-pipes, others knitting or idling, all grumbling, sit or stand under the trees that hedge in the almshouse, or limp about in the sunshine, leaning on crutches or bean-pole staffs. They are a “growler-gang” of another sort than may be seen in session on the rocks of the opposite shore at that very moment. They grumble and growl from sunrise to sunset, at the weather, the breakfast, the dinner, the supper; at pork and beans as at corned beef and cabbage; at their Thanksgiving dinner as at the half rations of the sick ward; at the past that had no joy, at the present whose comfort they deny, and at the future without promise. The crusty old men in the next building are not a circumstance to them. The warden, who was in charge of the almshouse for many years, had become so snappish and profane by constant association with a thousand cross old women that I approached him with some misgivings, to request his permission to “take” a group of a hundred or so who were within shot of my camera. He misunderstood me.

===Chinese===
一排排老妇人，有的抽着又短又粗的黑泥烟斗，有的织着毛线，有的闲坐着，个个满腹牢骚；她们坐在或立在围拢救济院的那圈树底下，或是在阳光里拄着拐杖、撑着豆架竿似的木棍，一瘸一拐地走动。她们是一支别样的「打酒帮」（growler-gang，英语 growl 兼有「咕哝咆哮」之义，此处语带双关）——不同于此刻正在对岸岩石上聚众「开会」的那一支。从日出到日落，她们咕哝个不停、咆哮个不停：怨天气，怨早饭、午饭、晚饭；怨猪肉炖豆子，也怨咸牛肉煮卷心菜；怨感恩节的大餐，也怨病房里的半份口粮；怨不曾有过欢乐的过去，怨她们不肯认账的眼前安逸，也怨毫无指望的将来。隔壁那栋楼里脾气乖戾的老头儿，同她们一比根本不算回事。主持救济院多年的那位院长，因为成天同上千个坏脾气的老妇人厮混，变得又暴躁又爱骂街，以致我去求见他时心里带着几分忐忑，想请他允准我“拍”下正落在我相机射程之内的一百来个老人。他误会了我的来意。

===Original===
“Take them?” he yelled. “Take the thousand of them and be welcome. They will never be still, by —﻿—﻿—, till they are sent up on Hart’s Island in a box, and I’ll be blamed if I don’t think they will growl then at the style of the funeral.”

===Chinese===
“带走她们？”他嚷道，“一千个全带走，尽管欢迎。她们是绝不肯安生的，凭—﻿—﻿—，除非装进箱子送上哈特岛（Hart's Island）；而且要是我不信她们到时候还要对葬礼的排场挑剔咕哝，那才怪呢。”

===Original===
And he threw his arms around me in an outburst of enthusiasm over the wondrous good luck that had sent a friend indeed to his door. I felt it to be a painful duty to undeceive him. When I told him that I simply wanted the old women’s picture, he turned away in speechless disgust, and to his dying day, I have no doubt, remembered my call as the day of the champion fool’s visit to the island.

===Chinese===
说着他喜出望外，张开双臂一把搂住了我——天大的好运，竟把一位货真价实的朋友送上了他的门。叫明真相成了我一桩难受的差事。待我讲明我不过是想给老太太们照张相，他厌恶得一句话也说不出来，转身便走；我毫不怀疑，直到他闭眼的那天，他都把我的来访记作头号大傻瓜光临该岛的日子。

===Original===
When it is known that many of these old people have been sent to the almshouse to die by their heartless children, for whom they had worked faithfully as long as they were able, their growling and discontent is not hard to understand. Bitter poverty threw them all “on the county,” often on the wrong county at that. Very many of them are old-country poor, sent, there is reason to believe, to America by the authorities to get rid of the obligation to support them. “The almshouse,” wrote a good missionary, “affords a sad illustration of St. Paul’s description of the ‘last days.’ The class from which comes our poorhouse population is to a large extent ‘without natural affection.’ ” I was reminded by his words of what my friend, the doctor, had said to me a little while before: “Many a mother has told me at her child’s deathbed, ‘I cannot afford to lose it. It costs too much to bury it.’ And when the little one did die there was no time for the mother’s grief. The question crowded on at once, ‘where shall the money come from?’ Natural feelings and affections are smothered in the tenements.” The doctor’s experience furnished a sadly appropriate text for the priest’s sermon.

===Chinese===
一旦知道这些老人中有许多是被自己狠心的儿女送进救济院来等死的——而只要还做得动，他们就曾为这些儿女忠心耿耿地劳作——他们的咕哝与不满便不难理解。悲惨的贫困把他们统统抛给「县里」养活，而且往往还抛错了县。他们当中有许多是旧大陆的穷人，有理由相信，是被那边的官府打发到美国来的，好甩脱供养他们的义务。“救济院，”一位好心的传教士写道，“为圣保罗（St. Paul）笔下‘末世’的描述提供了一个可悲的例证。我们的济贫人口所出的那个阶层，在很大程度上‘没有亲情’。”他的话使我想起我的朋友那位医生不久前对我说过的话：“多少位母亲在孩子咽气的床边对我说：‘我赔不起这条命。埋掉他要花的钱太多了。’而等到小家伙真的死了，做母亲的连悲伤的工夫都没有。问题立刻挤上前来：‘钱从哪儿来？’天性与亲情，都在廉租公寓里被闷死了。”医生的这番阅历，恰为这位传教士的讲道提供了一段悲哀而贴切的经文。

===Original===
Pitiful as these are, sights and sounds infinitely more saddening await us beyond the gate that shuts this world of woe off from one whence the light of hope and reason have gone out together. The shuffling of many feet on the macadamized roads heralds the approach of a host of women, hundreds upon hundreds﻿—beyond the turn in the road they still keep coming, marching with the faltering step, the unseeing look and the incessant, senseless chatter that betrays the darkened mind. The lunatic women of the Blackwell’s Island Asylum are taking their afternoon walk. Beyond, on the wide lawn, moves another still stranger procession, a file of women in the asylum dress of dull gray, hitched to a queer little wagon that, with its gaudy adornments, suggests a cross between a baby-carriage and a circus-chariot. One crazy woman is strapped in the seat; forty tug at the rope to which they are securely bound. This is the “chain-gang,” so called once in scoffing ignorance of the humane purpose the contrivance serves. These are the patients afflicted with suicidal mania, who cannot be trusted at large for a moment with the river in sight, yet must have their daily walk as a necessary part of their treatment. So this wagon was invented by a clever doctor to afford them at once exercise and amusement. A merry-go-round in the grounds suggests a variation of this scheme. Ghastly suggestion of mirth, with that stricken host advancing on its aimless journey! As we stop to see it pass, the plaintive strains of a familiar song float through a barred window in the gray stone building. The voice is sweet, but inexpressibly sad: “Oh, how my heart grows weary, far from﻿—” The song breaks off suddenly in a low, troubled laugh. She has forgotten, forgotten﻿—. A woman in the ranks, whose head has been turned toward the window, throws up her hands with a scream. The rest stir uneasily. The nurse is by her side in an instant with words half soothing, half stern. A messenger comes in haste from the asylum to ask us not to stop. Strangers may not linger where the patients pass. It is apt to excite them. As we go in with him the human file is passing yet, quiet restored. The troubled voice of the unseen singer still gropes vainly among the lost memories of the past for the missing key: “Oh! how my heart grows weary, far from﻿—”

===Chinese===
这些固然可怜，但在那扇门之外，还有无限更令人心酸的声色在等着我们——那扇门把这个世界与另一个世界隔开，而在后一个世界里，希望之光与理性之光已一同熄灭。碎石路上许多双脚的拖沓声，预告着一大队妇女的临近，成百上千——过了道路的拐弯，她们仍在源源而来，迈着蹒跚的步子，眼神空洞，嘴里絮絮地发出无意义的聒噪，泄露了已然昏暗的心智。布莱克韦尔岛疯人院（Blackwell's Island Asylum）的女病人正在作午后的散步。再往前，宽阔的草坪上行进着另一支更为古怪的队伍：一列穿着暗灰色病院号衣的妇女，拴在一辆古怪的小车上；那车装饰得花花绿绿，像是婴儿车与马戏彩车的杂糅。一个疯女人用皮带缚在座位上；四十个人拉着那根把她们彼此牢牢系住的绳子。这就是「锁链队」（chain-gang）——这名字当初是带着讥讽的无知叫开的，全不知这套装置原怀着仁慈的用意。这些是患了自杀狂的病人，只要望得见那条河，便一刻也放任不得，然而每日的散步又是治疗中必不可少的一环。于是便有一位聪明的医生发明了这辆车，让她们一举而兼得运动与娱乐。院场里的一架旋转木马，显示出同一构想的又一种变化。多么鬼气森森的欢乐假象——那支受难的队伍正沿着漫无目的的行程行进！我们驻足观看队伍经过时，一缕耳熟的哀婉歌声从灰石楼房的一扇铁窗里飘出。那嗓音甜美，却含着说不出的悲哀：“啊，我的心多么疲倦，远离——”歌声戛然而止，化作一声低低的、烦乱的笑。她忘了，忘了——。队伍里有一个一直把头朝那扇窗转着的女人，尖叫着举起双手。其余的人不安地骚动起来。护士霎时已到她身边，半是抚慰、半是严厉地说着话。一名信使从院里急急赶来，请我们不要停留。病人经过之处，生人不得逗留：那容易惊动她们。我们随他进门时，那条人的行列仍在通过，秩序已告恢复。那位看不见的歌手那烦乱的声音，仍在逝去的记忆中徒然摸索，寻觅那丢失的音：“啊！我的心多么疲倦，远离——”

===Original===
“Who is she, doctor?”

===Chinese===
“她是谁，医生？”

===Original===
“Hopeless case. She will never see home again.”

===Chinese===
“没指望的病例。她再也见不到家了。”

===Original===
An average of seventeen hundred women this asylum harbors; the asylum for men up on Ward’s Island even more. Altogether 1,419 patients were admitted to the city asylums for the insane in 1889, and at the end of the year 4,913 remained in them. There is a constant ominous increase in this class of helpless unfortunates that are thrown on the city’s charity. Quite two hundred are added year by year, and the asylums were long since so overcrowded that a great “farm” had to be established on Long Island to receive the surplus. The strain of our hurried, overworked life has something to do with this. Poverty has more. For these are all of the poor. It is the harvest of sixty and a hundredfold, the “fearful rolling up and rolling down from generation to generation, through all the ages, of the weakness, vice, and moral darkness of the past.”23 The curse of the island haunts all that come once within its reach. “No man or woman,” says Dr. Louis L. Seaman, who speaks from many years’ experience in a position that gave him full opportunity to observe the facts, “who is ‘sent up’ to these colonies ever returns to the city scot-free. There is a lien, visible or hidden, upon his or her present or future, which too often proves stronger than the best purposes and fairest opportunities of social rehabilitation. The under world holds in rigorous bondage every unfortunate or miscreant who has once ‘served time.’ There is often tragic interest in the struggles of the ensnared wretches to break away from the meshes spun about them. But the maelstrom has no bowels of mercy; and the would-be fugitives are flung back again and again into the devouring whirlpool of crime and poverty, until the end is reached on the dissecting-table, or in the Potter’s Field. What can the moralist or scientist do by way of resuscitation? Very little at best. The flotsam and jetsam are mere shreds and fragments of wasted lives. Such a ministry must begin at the sources﻿—is necessarily prophylactic, nutritive, educational. On these islands there are no flexible twigs, only gnarled, blasted, blighted trunks, insensible to moral or social influences.”

===Chinese===
这座疯人院平均收容一千七百名妇女；设在沃德岛（Ward's Island）上的男子疯人院收容的更多。1889 年，全市诸疯人院共收治病人 1,419 名，年终留在院中的有 4,913 名。这一类被抛给城市慈善照管的无助不幸者，其人数一直在不祥地增长着。每年新增足有二百名，各疯人院早已人满为患，不得不在长岛（Long Island）辟建一座大「农庄」来收容盈余。我们这匆忙而操劳过度的生活所带来的紧张，与此不无关系；贫困的关系更大。因为他们全都出自穷人。这是六十倍与一百倍的收成，是“过去世代的软弱、罪恶与道德黑暗，一代又一代、贯穿历朝历代，可怕地翻卷上来又翻卷下去”（23）。岛的诅咒，纠缠上一切一旦落入其势力范围的人。“无论男女，”路易斯·L·西曼医生（Dr. Louis L. Seaman）说——他多年供职于一个足以从容观察事实的职位，此言有本有据——“凡是曾被‘押送’到这些岛上居留地的人，从没有谁能清清白白地回到城里。他或她的现在与未来，都压着一笔或明或暗的留置权，而这笔留置权往往比社会改造的最良苦用意与最美好机会还要强硬。这个地下世界把每一个不幸‘服过刑’的人或恶徒，都攥在严苛的束缚之中。落网的可怜虫们挣扎着要摆脱缠住他们的罗网，其中每每含着悲剧的意味。但这大漩涡没有一副慈悲心肠；想逃的人被一次又一次抛回那吞没人的犯罪与贫困的漩涡，直到终点落在解剖台上，或者落进乱葬岗（Potter's Field）。道德家或科学家还能凭什么使他们起死回生？充其量微乎其微。这些浮货与沉屑，不过是虚耗生命的残片碎块。这样一种救济事工必须从源头做起——它必然是预防的、滋养的、教育的。在这些岛上，没有柔韧的枝条，只有虬曲、枯焦、凋殒的树干，对道德或社会的影响一概麻木不仁。”

===Original===
Sad words, but true. The commonest keeper soon learns to pick out almost at sight the “cases” that will leave the penitentiary, the workhouse, the almshouse, only to return again and again, each time more hopeless, to spend their wasted lives in the bondage of the island.

===Chinese===
悲哀的话，然而真实。最寻常的看守也很快便学会，几乎一眼就能挑出那些「病例」：他们出了监狱、济贫院、救济院，只为一次又一次地回来，一次比一次更无指望，把他们已然虚耗的生命消磨在岛的枷锁之下。

===Original===
The alcoholic cells in Bellevue Hospital are a way-station for a goodly share of them on their journeys back and forth across the East River. Last year they held altogether 3,694 prisoners, considerably more than one-fourth of the whole number of 13,813 patients that went in through the hospital gates. The daily average of “cases” in this, the hospital of the poor, is over six hundred. The average daily census of all the prisons, hospitals, workhouses, and asylums in the charge of the Department of Charities and Correction last year was about 14,000, and about one employee was required for every ten of this army to keep its machinery running smoothly. The total number admitted in 1889 to all the jails and institutions in the city and on the islands was 138,332. To the almshouse alone 38,600 were admitted; 9,765 were there to start the new year with, and 553 were born with the dark shadow of the poorhouse overhanging their lives, making a total of 48,918. In the care of all their wards the commissioners expended $2,343,372. The appropriation for the police force in 1889 was $4,409,550.94, and for the criminal courts and their machinery $403,190. Thus the first cost of maintaining our standing army of paupers, criminals, and sick poor, by direct taxation, was last year $7,156,112.94.

===Chinese===
贝尔维尤医院（Bellevue Hospital）里收押醉汉的牢房，是他们当中相当一批人往来东河两岸途中的中途小站。去年这些牢房共收押 3,694 人，远过走进这家医院大门的全部 13,813 名病人的四分之一。在这所穷人的医院里，「病例」的日均数在六百以上。归慈善与惩戒局（Department of Charities and Correction）管辖的所有监狱、医院、济贫院与疯人院，去年日均人口约为 14,000；为使这部机器顺滑运转，这支大军大约每十人便需要一名雇员。1889 年，全市及诸岛所有监狱与收容机构收容的总人数为 138,332。仅救济院一处便收容了 38,600 人；9,765 人留在院中迎接新年，另有 553 名婴儿在济贫院黑影罩顶的境况里降生人世，合计 48,918 人。在照管所有这些收容对象上，慈善专员们支出了 $2,343,372。1889 年警察队伍的经费拨款为 $4,409,550.94，刑事法庭及其机器为 $403,190。这样，靠直接征税来供养我们这支由贫民、罪犯与患病穷人组成的常备军，去年的头一笔开销便是 $7,156,112.94。

## XXIII The Man with the Knife / XXIII 持刀者

===Original===
A man stood at the corner of Fifth Avenue and Fourteenth Street the other day, looking gloomily at the carriages that rolled by, carrying the wealth and fashion of the avenues to and from the big stores downtown. He was poor, and hungry, and ragged. This thought was in his mind: “They behind their well-fed teams have no thought for the morrow; they know hunger only by name, and ride down to spend in an hour’s shopping what would keep me and my little ones from want a whole year.” There rose up before him the picture of those little ones crying for bread around the cold and cheerless hearth﻿—then he sprang into the throng and slashed about him with a knife, blindly seeking to kill, to revenge.

===Chinese===
前几天，有个人站在第五大道（Fifth Avenue）与第十四街（Fourteenth Street）的街角，阴郁地望着眼前辚辚驶过的一辆辆马车——它们载着大道上的财富与时髦人物，往返于下城的大商店之间。他又穷，又饿，衣衫褴褛。他心里转着这样一个念头：“他们坐在喂得膘肥体壮的马队后面，不为明天发一点愁；饥饿于他们只是个名字；他们坐车下城，个把钟头的购物花销，就够供养我和我的孩子们一整年，叫我们免于匮乏。”他的眼前浮现出那幅图景：孩子们围在冷冷清清、不见一丝暖意的炉边哭喊着要面包——于是他纵身扑进人群，抡起刀朝四周乱砍，昏头昏脑地只想杀人，只想报仇。

===Original===
The man was arrested, of course, and locked up. Today he is probably in a madhouse, forgotten. And the carriages roll by to and from the big stores with their gay throng of shoppers. The world forgets easily, too easily, what it does not like to remember.

===Chinese===
这个人自然被抓了起来，关进了牢里。今天他多半已经待在疯人院里，被人忘掉了。而马车依旧载着成群衣香鬓影的购物客，来来往往地行驶在大商店之间。这个世界的忘性很大，大到过头：凡是它不爱记住的，一概忘掉。

===Original===
Nevertheless the man and his knife had a mission. They spoke in their ignorant, impatient way the warning one of the most conservative, dispassionate of public bodies had sounded only a little while before: “Our only fear is that reform may come in a burst of public indignation destructive to property and to good morals.”24 They represented one solution of the problem of ignorant poverty versus ignorant wealth that has come down to us unsolved, the danger-cry of which we have lately heard in the shout that never should have been raised on American soil﻿—the shout of “the masses against the classes”﻿—the solution of violence.

===Chinese===
然而，这个人和他的刀却负有一重使命。他们以自己那愚昧而急不可耐的方式，说出了最保守、最不动感情的公共团体之一不久前刚刚敲响的警告：“我们唯一害怕的，是改革会在一阵对财产和良善道德俱有破坏性的公愤中骤然而来。”（24）他们代表着愚昧的贫穷对垒愚昧的财富这一难题的一种解法——这道难题未经解决便传到了我们手里，它的危险呼声我们近来已经听到，就在那一声本不该在美国土地上喊出的呐喊里——「大众对抗阶级」的呐喊——这解法便是暴力。

===Original===
There is another solution, that of justice. The choice is between the two. Which shall it be?

===Chinese===
还有另一种解法，那就是正义。二者必居其一。究竟要哪一种？

===Original===
“Well!” say some well-meaning people; “we don’t see the need of putting it in that way. We have been down among the tenements, looked them over. There are a good many people there; they are not comfortable, perhaps. What would you have? They are poor. And their houses are not such hovels as we have seen and read of in the slums of the Old World. They are decent in comparison. Why, some of them have brownstone fronts. You will own at least that they make a decent show.”

===Chinese===
“哎呀！”一些好心人说，“我们看不出有什么必要把话说到这个地步。我们也下到廉租公寓那边去看过、查过。那里人是不少；也许过得不算舒坦。可不然你还要怎样呢？他们穷嘛。况且他们的房子，也不是我们在旧大陆的贫民窟里见过、读到过的那种草窝窝。两相比较，算是体面的了。嗐，有些还带着褐石门面呢。至少你得承认，它们看上去确实有副体面的模样。”

===Original===
Yes! that is true. The worst tenements in New York do not, as a rule, *look bad*. Neither Hell’s Kitchen, nor Murderers’ Row bears its true character stamped on the front. They are not quite old enough, perhaps. The same is true of their tenants. The New York tough may be ready to kill where his London brother would do little more than scowl; yet, as a general thing he is less repulsively brutal in looks. Here again the reason may be the same: the breed is not so old. A few generations more in the slums, and all that will be changed. To get at the pregnant facts of tenement-house life one must look beneath the surface. Many an apple has a fair skin and a rotten core. There is a much better argument for the tenements in the assurance of the Registrar of Vital Statistics that the death-rate of these houses has of late been brought below the general death-rate of the city, and that it is lowest in the biggest houses. This means two things: one, that the almost exclusive attention given to the tenements by the sanitary authorities in twenty years has borne some fruit, and that the newer tenements are better than the old﻿—there is some hope in that; the other, that the whole strain of tenement-house dwellers has been bred down to the conditions under which it exists, that the struggle with corruption has begotten the power to resist it. This is a familiar law of nature, necessary to its first and strongest impulse of self-preservation. To a certain extent, we are all creatures of the conditions that surround us, physically and morally. But is the knowledge reassuring? In the light of what we have seen, does not the question arise: what sort of creature, then, this of the tenement? I tried to draw his likeness from observation in telling the story of the “tough.” Has it nothing to suggest the man with the knife?

===Chinese===
是啊！这话不假。纽约最糟的廉租公寓，一般说来*看上去*并不糟。无论是地狱厨房（Hell’s Kitchen）还是「凶杀排」（Murderers’ Row），都没有把自身的真实品性印在门脸上。也许它们的年头还不够老吧。它们的房客也是如此。纽约的恶棍在他那位伦敦兄弟多半只会瞪眼皱眉的地方，也许就动起了杀机；然而一般而论，论相貌他倒没有那么面目狰狞、令人憎恶。这里的原因或许还是同一个：这一血统的年头还不那么老。在贫民窟里再泡上几代，这一切都会改变。要触及廉租公寓生活中那些意味深长的事实，必须看到表面底下去。多少苹果皮色光鲜，芯子却已烂掉。倒是有一个对廉租公寓有利得多的论据：生命统计官（Registrar of Vital Statistics）打包票说，这类房屋的死亡率近来已被压到全市总死亡率以下，而且越是最大的房子，死亡率越低。这意味着两件事：其一，二十年来卫生当局对廉租公寓几乎倾注了独家独份的关注，总算结出了一些果实，新建的廉租公寓也比旧的强——这里面有些希望；其二，廉租公寓住户的整个血统，已经被培育得降格迁就自己所处的境况，与腐败的搏斗本身孕育出了抵抗腐败的气力。这是自然界一条人们熟知的法则，为自然界那第一等、最强烈的自我保存冲动所必需。在某种程度上，无论肉体还是道德，我们全都是环绕自身的种种境况的造物。可是，知道了这一点就能叫人安心吗？就拿我们目睹的一切来说，难道不会冒出这样一个疑问：那么，这廉租公寓里养出来的，又是一副什么品性的造物呢？我在讲「恶棍」的故事时，曾试着凭观察描画他的肖像。难道那幅画像就丝毫不能让人联想到那持刀者吗？

===Original===
I will go further. I am not willing even to admit it to be an unqualified advantage that our New York tenements have less of the slum look than those of older cities. It helps to delay the recognition of their true character on the part of the well-meaning, but uninstructed, who are always in the majority.

===Chinese===
我还要再进一步。纽约的廉租公寓比老城市的少了几分贫民窟的面相，对这一点，我甚至不肯承认是一种十足的好处。它恰恰拖延了那些心怀善意却未经指点的人对其真实品性的认识——而这班人永远占多数。

===Original===
The “dangerous classes” of New York long ago compelled recognition. They are dangerous less because of their own crimes than because of the criminal ignorance of those who are not of their kind. The danger to society comes not from the poverty of the tenements, but from the ill-spent wealth that reared them, that it might earn a usurious interest from a class from which “nothing else was expected.” That was the broad foundation laid down, and the edifice built upon it corresponds to the groundwork. That this is well understood on the “unsafe” side of the line that separates the rich from the poor, much better than by those who have all the advantages of discriminating education, is good cause for disquietude. In it a keen foresight may again dimly discern the shadow of the man with the knife.

===Chinese===
纽约的「危险阶级」（dangerous classes）早就逼得人们不得不承认其存在。他们的危险，与其说在于自身的种种罪行，不如说在于不属于他们那类的人身上那种犯罪般的无知。社会所面临的危险，不来自廉租公寓里的贫穷，而来自把廉租公寓盖了起来、好从一个「不被寄予任何期望」的阶级身上榨取高利的那笔用错了地方的财富。当初铺下的就是这样一块宽大的地基，在上面盖起的大厦也正与地基相称。这一切，在分隔贫富的那条界线的「不安全」一侧，反倒被理解得透彻得多，远胜过那些坐享明辨是非的教育之一切好处的人——这可是个叫人不安的充分理由。在其中，敏锐的先见之明或许又能依稀辨认出持刀者的影子。

===Original===
Two years ago a great meeting was held at Chickering Hall﻿—I have spoken of it before﻿—a meeting that discussed for days and nights the question how to banish this spectre; how to lay hold with good influences of this enormous mass of more than a million people, who were drifting away faster and faster from the safe moorings of the old faith. Clergymen and laymen from all the Protestant denominations took part in the discussion; nor was a good word forgotten for the brethren of the other great Christian fold who labor among the poor. Much was said that was good and true, and ways were found of reaching the spiritual needs of the tenement population that promise success. But at no time throughout the conference was the real keynote of the situation so boldly struck as has been done by a few farseeing business men, who had listened to the cry of that Christian builder: “How shall the love of God be understood by those who have been nurtured in sight only of the greed of man?” Their practical programme of “Philanthropy and five percent” has set examples in tenement building that show, though they are yet few and scattered, what may in time be accomplished even with such poor, opportunities as New York offers today of undoing the old wrong. This is the gospel of justice, the solution that must be sought as the one alternative to the man with the knife.

===Chinese===
两年前，奇克林音乐厅（Chickering Hall）开过一次大会——我先前已经说起过它——那次大会连日连夜讨论的，正是如何驱走这个幽灵；如何用良善的影响笼住这一百多万人组成的庞大人群——他们正越来越快地漂离旧信仰那安稳的泊地。新教各派的教士和平信徒都参加了讨论；对另一大基督教阵营中在穷人中间劳作的弟兄们，也没有忘了送上几句好话。会上说了许多又好又真的话，也找到了若干触及廉租公寓人群心灵需求的门路，而且有望成功。但在整个大会期间，局势真正的基调，从来不曾有人像几位有远见的实业家那样大胆地敲响过——他们听进了那位基督徒营造商的呼喊：“在只目睹过人之贪婪的环境中长大的人，怎样才能理解上帝的爱？”他们那套「慈善与百分之五的回报」（“Philanthropy and five percent”）的务实纲领，已在廉租公寓营造上树立了一些范例；这些范例虽然还寥寥无几、零零散散，却昭示了假以时日，纵然只有纽约今日所提供的这般可怜的机会，也能把旧日的冤屈纠正到什么地步。这就是正义的福音，是除了持刀者之外、必须寻求的唯一替代解法。

===Original===
“Are you not looking too much to the material condition of these people,” said a good minister to me after a lecture in a Harlem church last winter, “and forgetting the inner man?” I told him, “No! for you cannot expect to find an inner man to appeal to in the worst tenement-house surroundings. You must first put the man where he can respect himself. To reverse the argument of the apple: you cannot expect to find a sound core in a rotten fruit.”

===Chinese===
去年冬天，我在哈莱姆（Harlem）一座教堂演讲完之后，一位善良的牧师对我说：“你是不是太看重这些人的物质境况了，而忘掉了人内在的心灵？”我告诉他：“不！因为在最恶劣的廉租公寓环境里，你别指望找到一个可供吁求的内在心灵。你必须先把这个人放到他能够自尊的地方。把苹果的论证倒过来用：你不能指望从一个烂果子里找到完好的芯子。”

## XXIV What Has Been Done / XXIV 已经做了什么

===Original===
In twenty years what has been done in New York to solve the tenement-house problem?

===Chinese===
二十年来，纽约为解决廉租公寓问题做了些什么？

===Original===
The law has done what it could. That was not always a great deal, seldom more than barely sufficient for the moment. An aroused municipal conscience endowed the Health Department with almost autocratic powers in dealing with this subject, but the desire to educate rather than force the community into a better way dictated their exercise with a slow conservatism that did not always seem wise to the impatient reformer. New York has its St. Antoine, and it has often sadly missed a Napoleon III to clean up and make light in the dark corners. The obstacles, too, have been many and great. Nevertheless the authorities have not been idle, though it is a grave question whether all the improvements made under the sanitary regulations of recent years deserve the name. Tenements quite as bad as the worst are too numerous yet; but one tremendous factor for evil in the lives of the poor has been taken by the throat, and something has unquestionably been done, where that was possible, to lift those lives out of the rut where they were equally beyond the reach of hope and of ambition. It is no longer lawful to construct barracks to cover the whole of a lot. Air and sunlight have a legal claim, and the day of rear tenements is past. Two years ago a hundred thousand people burrowed in these inhuman dens; but some have been torn down since. Their number will decrease steadily until they shall have become a bad tradition of a heedless past. The dark, unventilated bedroom is going with them, and the open sewer. The day is at hand when the greatest of all evils that now curse life in the tenements﻿—the dearth of water in the hot summer days﻿—will also have been remedied, and a long step taken toward the moral and physical redemption of their tenants.

===Chinese===
法律已经尽了它所能尽的力量。这所能并不总是很大，很少超出勉强应付一时的限度。觉醒的市政良知赋予了卫生局（Health Department）在处理这一问题上近乎专断的权力；然而，「教导而非强迫」社会走上更好道路的意愿，决定了这些权力的行使带着一种迟缓的保守主义——在不耐烦的改革者看来，这种保守并不总是显得明智。纽约也有自己的圣安托万（St. Antoine，巴黎的贫民窟），却常常痛感缺少一位拿破仑三世（Napoleon III），来把那些黑暗的角落打扫干净、照进光亮。障碍也向来又多又大。尽管如此，当局并非无所作为；只是，近年卫生规章之下做出的一切改良是否都当得起这个名目，仍是一个严重的问题。同最坏的廉租公寓一样糟糕的房子，今天仍为数太多；但穷人生活里一个为祸至巨的因素已被扼住了咽喉，而且凡有可为之处，都已确确实实做了一些事，把那些生命从旧车辙里往上拉——在那车辙里，希望够不着他们，抱负也够不着他们。把营房盖满整块宅地，如今已是非法。空气和阳光有了法律上的权利，后院廉租公寓（rear tenement）的时代已经过去。两年前，还有十万人蛰居在这些非人的窟穴里；但此后已有一些被拆掉了。它们的数目将稳步减少，直到沦为一段漫不经心的往昔留下的糟糕旧话。黑暗而不通风的卧室将随它们一同消失，露天的阴沟亦然。那一天已经临近：如今荼毒廉租公寓生活的诸恶之中最大的一桩——酷暑时节的缺水——也将得到救治，而朝着其房客道德与肉体的救赎，长长的一步已经迈出。

===Original===
Public sentiment has done something also, but very far from enough. As a rule, it has slumbered peacefully until some flagrant outrage on decency and the health of the community aroused it to noisy but ephemeral indignation, or until a dreaded epidemic knocked at our door. It is this unsteadiness of purpose that has been to a large extent responsible for the apparent lagging of the authorities in cases not involving immediate danger to the general health. The law needs a much stronger and readier backing of a thoroughly enlightened public sentiment to make it as effective as it might be made. It is to be remembered that the health officers, in dealing with this subject of dangerous houses, are constantly trenching upon what each landlord considers his private rights, for which he is ready and bound to fight to the last. Nothing short of the strongest pressure will avail to convince him that these individual rights are to be surrendered for the clear benefit of the whole. It is easy enough to convince a man that he ought not to harbor the thief who steals people’s property; but to make him see that he has no right to slowly kill his neighbors, or his tenants, by making a deathtrap of his house, seems to be the hardest of all tasks. It is apparently the slowness of the process that obscures his mental sight. The man who will fight an order to repair the plumbing in his house through every court he can reach, would suffer tortures rather than shed the blood of a fellow-man by actual violence. Clearly, it is a matter of education on the part of the landlord no less than the tenant.

===Chinese===
公众的情感也做了一些事，但远远不够。大体而言，它一直安然酣睡，直到某桩公然冒犯体统与公众健康的暴行把它惊起，激起一阵喧腾而短暂的义愤，或是直到一场令人畏惧的疫病来敲我们的门。正是这种目的上的摇摆不定，在很大程度上要为当局在那些并不危及公众健康之迫切危险的案件上的表面迟缓负责。法律需要一股彻底开明的公众情感更坚强、更随时可恃的支援，才能变得像它本可以做到的那样有效。应当记住：卫生官员们在处理危险房屋这一问题时，无时无刻不在触动每位房东视为一己私有的权利——为着这些权利，他随时准备、也有义务奋战到底。非有最强硬的压力，不足以叫他相信：这些个人权利应当交出来，以换取整体的明显利益。要说服一个人不该窝藏偷人财产的贼，容易得很；可要叫他明白，他无权把自己的房子变成死亡陷阱、慢慢地害死邻居或房客，却似乎是天下头等难事。遮蔽他心智眼目的，显然正是这一过程的缓慢。一个肯为修理房中管道的命令打遍一切够得着的法庭的人，宁受千般苦刑，也不肯以实际的暴力流一个同胞的血。显然，这是房东一方与房客一方同样需要受教育的事。
===Original===
In spite of this, the landlord has done his share; chiefly perhaps by yielding﻿—not always gracefully﻿—when it was no longer of any use to fight. There have been exceptions, however: men and women who have mended and built with an eye to the real welfare of their tenants as well as to their own pockets. Let it be well understood that the two are inseparable, if any good is to come of it. The business of housing the poor, if it is to amount to anything, must be business, as it was business with our fathers to put them where they are. As charity, pastime, or fad, it will miserably fail, always and everywhere. This is an inexorable rule, now thoroughly well understood in England and continental Europe, and by all who have given the matter serious thought here. Call it poetic justice, or divine justice, or anything else, it is a hard fact, not to be gotten over. Upon any other plan than the assumption that the workman has a just claim to a decent home, and the right to demand it, any scheme for his relief fails. It must be a fair exchange of the man’s money for what he can afford to buy at a reasonable price. Any charity scheme merely turns him into a pauper, however it may be disguised, and drowns him hopelessly in the mire out of which it proposed to pull him. And this principle must pervade the whole plan. Expert management of model tenements succeeds where amateur management, with the best intentions, gives up the task, discouraged, as a flat failure. Some of the best-conceived enterprises, backed by abundant capital and goodwill, have been wrecked on this rock. Sentiment, having prompted the effort, forgot to stand aside and let business make it.

===Chinese===
尽管如此，房东也尽了自己的一份；主要的，也许是在再斗也无济于事的时候让了步——让得并不总是那么体面。但也有例外：有些男女房东修房造屋时，既看着自己的钱袋，也看着房客真正的福祉。请把这一点弄个明白：若要这事结出半点善果，两者是分不开的。穷人的住房事业，若要成得了气候，就必须是一门生意，正如当年把穷人安置到如今这般田地，在我们的父辈手里本也是一门生意。当作慈善、消遣或一时的风尚，它无论何时何地都必将悲惨地失败。这是一条无情的铁律，如今在英格兰和欧洲大陆已被人透彻了解，凡在此间认真思索过这个问题的人也无不了解。管它叫诗性的正义、神圣的正义，或者随便什么别的名字，它是一个绕不过去的硬事实。任何救助工人的方案，若不建立在这样的假定之上——工人对一个体面的住家有着正当的索求，并且有权利提出这一索求——都免不了失败。它必须是这个人的金钱同他以合理价格买得起的居所之间的公平交易。任何慈善方案，无论怎样乔装打扮，都无非把他变成一个贫民，把他无可救药地重新淹进它原本打算把他拽出来的那滩泥沼。而这一条原则必须贯穿整个方案。由行家掌管的模范廉租公寓（model tenement）获得成功；由外行掌管——哪怕怀着最善良的用意——则灰心丧气地撂下任务，认作彻底的失败。一些构想最完善的实业，资本雄厚、善意充盈，正是撞碎在这块礁石上。情感既然促成了这项努力，却忘了退到一旁，让生意把它做成。

===Original===
Business, in a wider sense, has done more than all other agencies together to wipe out the worst tenements. It has been New York’s real Napoleon III, from whose decree there was no appeal. In ten years I have seen plague-spots disappear before its onward march, with which health officers, police, and sanitary science had struggled vainly since such struggling began as a serious business. And the process goes on still. Unfortunately, the crowding in some of the most densely packed quarters downtown has made the property there so valuable, that relief from this source is less confidently to be expected, at all events in the near future. Still, their time may come also. It comes so quickly sometimes as to fairly take one’s breath away. More than once I have returned, after a few brief weeks, to some specimen rookery in which I was interested, to find it gone and an army of workmen delving twenty feet underground to lay the foundation of a mighty warehouse. That was the case with the “Big Flat” in Mott Street. I had not had occasion to visit it for several months last winter, and when I went there, entirely unprepared for a change, I could not find it. It had always been conspicuous enough in the landscape before, and I marvelled much at my own stupidity until, by examining the number of the house, I found out that I had gone right. It was the “flat” that had disappeared. In its place towered a six-story carriage factory with business going on on every floor, as if it had been there for years and years.

===Chinese===
从更广的意义上说，实业为铲除最坏的廉租公寓所做的，超过其余一切力量加在一起的总和。它一直是纽约真正的拿破仑三世，它的裁决不容上诉。十年之间，我眼看着一个个瘟疫窝点在它进军的脚步前消失——对于这些窝点，卫生官员、警察和卫生科学自从把同它们的搏斗当一门正经事业以来，一直徒劳无功。这一进程至今仍在继续。不幸的是，下城几处人口最稠密地段的拥挤，已使那里的地产如此值钱，以致对来自这一源头的救济，至少在不久的将来，不能抱太大的指望。不过，它们的时辰也会到来。有时它来得快得简直叫人背过气去。不止一次，我在短短几个星期之后回到某处我惦记着的大杂院标本跟前，发现它已经没了踪影，一支工人大军正在二十英尺深的地下掘土，为一座巍峨的仓库奠地基。莫特街（Mott Street）那座「大公寓」（“Big Flat”）便是如此。去年冬天我好几个月不曾有机会去访它；待到前往时，全没有料到会有什么变化，竟找它不到。它从前在那一带的街景里向来够惹眼的了，我直纳闷自己怎会蠢成这样，直到看清那房子的门牌，才明白我去的地方并没有错——是那座「公寓」消失了。原地之上矗立起一座六层的马车制造厂，每个楼层里生意都在照常进行，仿佛它已经在那里立了许多许多年。

===Original===
This same “Big Flat” furnished a good illustration of why some well-meant efforts in tenement building have failed. Like Gotham Court, it was originally built as a model tenement, but speedily came to rival the Court in foulness. It became a regular hotbed of thieves and peace-breakers, and made no end of trouble for the police. The immediate reason, outside of the lack of proper supervision, was that it had open access to two streets in a neighborhood where thieves and “toughs” abounded. These took advantage of an arrangement that had been supposed by the builders to be a real advantage as a means of ventilation, and their occupancy drove honest folk away. Murderers’ Alley, of which I have spoken elsewhere, and the sanitary inspector’s experiment with building a brick wall athwart it to shut off travel through the block, is a parallel case.

===Chinese===
正是这座「大公寓」，为廉租公寓建筑中某些出于好意的尝试何以失败，提供了一个好的例证。同哥谭院（Gotham Court）一样，它当初也是作为模范廉租公寓盖起来的，却很快就在污秽上同哥谭院一争高下。它成了窃贼和捣乱分子的固定温床，给警察惹来没完没了的麻烦。直接的缘由——撇开缺乏适当的管理不谈——在于它向两条街道同时敞开，而那正是一个窃贼与「恶棍」多产的地界。这些家伙利用了营造商本以为是一桩真正优点的布局——一种通风的手段——他们住了进来，他们的盘踞把正派的住户统统赶跑了。我在别处讲过的「凶手巷」，连同卫生检查员横贯巷中砌一道砖墙、截断穿街区通行的实验，是一个平行的案例。
===Original===
The causes that operate to obstruct efforts to better the lot of the tenement population are, in our day, largely found among the tenants themselves. This is true particularly of the poorest. They are shiftless, destructive, and stupid; in a word, they are what the tenements have made them. It is a dreary old truth that those who would fight for the poor must fight the poor to do it. It must be confessed that there is little enough in their past experience to inspire confidence in the sincerity of the effort to help them. I recall the discomfiture of a certain well-known philanthropist, since deceased, whose heart beat responsive to other suffering than that of human kind. He was a large owner of tenement property, and once undertook to fit out his houses with stationary tubs, sanitary plumbing, wood-closets, and all the latest improvements. He introduced his rough tenants to all this magnificence without taking the precaution of providing a competent housekeeper, to see that the new acquaintances got on together. He felt that his tenants ought to be grateful for the interest he took in them. They were. They found the boards in the wood-closets fine kindling wood, while the pipes and faucets were as good as cash at the junk shop. In three months the owner had to remove what was left of his improvements. The pipes were cut and the houses running full of water, the stationary tubs were put to all sorts of uses except washing, and of the wood-closets not a trace was left. The philanthropist was ever after a firm believer in the total depravity of tenement-house people. Others have been led to like reasoning by as plausible arguments, without discovering that the shiftlessness and ignorance that offended them were the consistent crop of the tenement they were trying to reform, and had to be included in the effort. The owners of a block of model tenements uptown had got their tenants comfortably settled, and were indulging in high hopes of their redemption under proper management, when a contractor ran up a row of “skin” tenements, shaky but fair to look at, with brownstone trimmings and gewgaws. The result was to tempt a lot of the well-housed tenants away. It was a very astonishing instance of perversity to the planners of the benevolent scheme; but, after all, there was nothing strange in it. It is all a matter of education, as I said about the landlord.

===Chinese===
在今日，阻碍人们改善廉租公寓人口境遇的种种原因，很大程度上要到房客们自己身上去找。最贫穷的阶层尤其如此。他们得过且过、破坏成性、愚昧迟钝；一言以蔽之，他们就是廉租公寓把他们造就成的那个样子。那些愿意为穷人奋斗的人，必须先同穷人交战，才斗得成这件事——这是一条凄凉的老真理。必须承认，他们过往的经历里，几乎没有什么东西能让人相信帮扶他们的努力是出于诚意。我记得某位著名慈善家——如今已经去世——的一番败兴遭遇，他那颗心对人类之外别的苦难也有感应。他是廉租公寓产业的一位大业主，有一次着手给他的房子装上固定洗衣盆、卫生管道、柴房和全部最新式的改良设施。他把粗野的房客们引进这一整片辉煌，却不曾采取一点防备，安排一名得力的管家来照应，好叫这些新相识彼此处得来。他觉得房客们理应感激他对他们的关切。他们也确实感激。他们发现柴房里的木板是上好的引火柴，而管子和龙头送到废品铺里，同现钱一般好使。不出三个月，业主只好把他那些改良设施的残余拆除搬走。管子被割断，房子里淌满了水；固定洗衣盆被派上洗涤之外的种种用场；柴房则连一丝痕迹也不剩了。这位慈善家从此坚信廉租公寓住户彻头彻尾地堕落，再没有动摇过。另一些人也被同样貌似有理的论证引到了同一套推理上，却没有发觉：触怒他们的那种得过且过与愚昧无知，正是他们试图改良的那座廉租公寓一以贯之的收成，理应一并纳入改良的努力之中。上城一处模范廉租公寓街区的业主们刚把房客舒舒服服安顿停当，正满怀他们经正当管理终得救赎的殷切期望，一个营造商却在旁边抢起了一排「皮壳」廉租公寓（“skin” tenements）——摇摇欲坠，外表却颇可观，镶着褐砂石的饰线和花里胡哨的小摆设。结果是一大批住得妥妥帖帖的房客被引了出去。在仁善方案的规划者眼里，这是一桩令人错愕的乖张；可归根结底，其中毫无奇怪可言。这全是教育的问题，正如我谈到房东时说过的那样。

===Original===
That the education comes slowly need excite no surprise. The forces on the other side are ever active. The faculty of the tenement for appropriating to itself every foul thing that comes within its reach, and piling up and intensifying its corruption until out of all proportion to the beginning, is something marvellous. Drop a case of scarlet fever, of measles, or of diphtheria into one of these barracks, and, unless it is caught at the very start and stamped out, the contagion of the one case will sweep block after block, and half people a graveyard. Let the police break up a vile dive, goaded by the angry protests of the neighborhood﻿—forthwith the outcasts set in circulation by the raid betake themselves to the tenements, where in their hired rooms, safe from interference, they set up as many independent centres of contagion, infinitely more destructive, each and every one, than was the known dive before. I am not willing to affirm that this is the police reason for letting so many of the dives alone; but it might well be. They are perfectly familiar with the process, and entirely helpless to prevent it.

===Chinese===
这种教育来得缓慢，不必为之惊诧。站在另一边的诸般力量无时无刻不在活动。廉租公寓有一种本领：把够得着的每一件肮脏物事都据为己有，把自己的腐败层层堆叠、步步加深，直到同开端全然不成比例——这本领实在令人惊叹。往这些营房中的一座里投进一例猩红热、麻疹或白喉，只要不在一开头就逮住它、把它扑灭，这一例的传染就会扫过一个又一个街区，给一座墓园送去半园的新住户。让警察在街坊愤怒抗议的驱迫之下查抄一家下等酒窟——顷刻之间，被这场突查放出去流转的沦落之人便投奔到廉租公寓里去；在他们租下的房间里，谁也干涉不着，他们竖起一个又一个独立的传染中心，每一个都比先前那家已知的酒窟具有大出无穷的毁灭性。我不愿断言，这就是警察对那么多酒窟撒手不管的理由；但它尽可以就是。他们对这一过程烂熟于心，却全然无力阻止。

===Original===
This faculty, as inherent in the problem itself﻿—the prodigious increase of the tenement-house population that goes on without cessation, and its consequent greater crowding﻿—is the chief obstacle to its solution. In 1869 there were 14,872 tenements in New York, with a population of 468,492 persons. In 1879 the number of the tenements was estimated at 21,000, and their tenants had passed the half-million mark. At the end of the year 1888, when a regular census was made for the first time since 1869, the showing was: 32,390 tenements, with a population of 1,093,701 souls. Today we have 37,316 tenements, including 2,630 rear houses, and their population is over 1,250,000. A large share of this added population, especially of that which came to us from abroad, crowds in below Fourteenth Street, where the population is already packed beyond reason, and confounds all attempts to make matters better there. At the same time new slums are constantly growing up uptown, and have to be kept down with a firm hand. This drift of the population to the great cities has to be taken into account as a steady factor. It will probably increase rather than decrease for many years to come. At the beginning of the century the percentage of our population that lived in cities was as one in twenty-five. In 1880 it was one in four and one-half, and in 1890 the census will in all probability show it to be one in four. Against such tendencies, in the absence of suburban outlets for the crowding masses, all remedial measures must prove more or less ineffective. The “confident belief” expressed by the Board of Health in 1874, that rapid transit would solve the problem, is now known to have been a vain hope.

===Chinese===
这一本领，作为问题本身所固有——廉租公寓人口那无休无止的惊人增长，以及随之而来的愈加拥挤——正是解决问题的首要障碍。1869 年，纽约有 14,872 座廉租公寓，人口 468,492 人。1879 年，廉租公寓的数目估计为 21,000 座，房客已越过五十万大关。到 1888 年底，自 1869 年以来头一回进行正规清点时，统计的结果是：32,390 座廉租公寓，人口 1,093,701 人。今天我们有 37,316 座廉租公寓，其中包括 2,630 座后屋（rear house），人口超过 1,250,000。这新增人口的一大宗，尤其是从海外来到我们这里的那些，都挤在第十四街（Fourteenth Street）以南——那里的人口早已挤得超出常理，一切使局面好转的尝试在那里都归于徒劳。与此同时，上城新的贫民窟还在不断长出来，得用一只坚定的手把它们压下去。人口向大城市的这一流动，必须当作一个稳定的因素来计入。往后许多年里，它多半会有增无减。本世纪初，我国人口住在城市里的比例为二十五分之一；1880 年为四点五分之一；1890 年的人口普查十之八九会显示为四分之一。面对这样的趋势，又没有供拥挤大众疏解的郊区出路，一切补救措施都必定多少归于无效。卫生委员会（Board of Health）1874 年表达过的「坚定的信念」——说快速交通（rapid transit）将会解决这个问题——如今已知道不过是一场空望。
===Original===
Workingmen, in New York at all events, will live near their work, no matter at what sacrifice of comfort﻿—one might almost say at whatever cost, and the city will never be less crowded than it is. To distribute the crowds as evenly as possible is the effort of the authorities, where nothing better can be done. In the first six months of the present year 1,068 persons were turned out of not quite two hundred tenements below Houston Street by the sanitary police on their midnight inspections, and this covered only a very small part of that field. The uptown tenements were practically left to take care of themselves in this respect.

===Chinese===
至少在纽约，工人总要住在离活计近的地方，不管牺牲多少安逸——几乎可以说不惜任何代价；而这座城市也永远不会比现在更不拥挤。在别无善策之处，当局的努力便是把人群尽可能均匀地摊开。今年头六个月里，卫生警察在午夜巡查中从休斯敦街（Houston Street）以南不到两百座廉租公寓里清理出了 1,068 人，而这只覆盖了那一地域很小的一部分。上城的廉租公寓在这方面实际上被放任自流。

===Original===
The quick change of economic conditions in the city that often outpaces all plans of relief, rendering useless today what met the demands of the situation well enough yesterday, is another cause of perplexity. A common obstacle also﻿—I am inclined to think quite as common as in Ireland, though we hear less of it in the newspapers﻿—is the absentee landlord. The home article, who fights for his rights, as he chooses to consider them, is bad enough; but the absentee landlord is responsible for no end of trouble. He was one of the first obstructions the sanitary reformers stumbled over, when the Health Department took hold. It reported in 1869 that many of the tenants were entirely uncared for, and that the only answer to their requests to have the houses put in order was an invitation to pay their rent or get out. “Inquiry often disclosed the fact that the owner of the property was a wealthy gentleman or lady, either living in an aristocratic part of the city, or in a neighboring city, or, as was occasionally found to be the case, in Europe. The property is usually managed entirely by an agent, whose instructions are simple but emphatic: Collect the rent in advance, or, failing, eject the occupants.” The Committee having the matter in charge proposed to compel owners of tenements with ten families or more to put a housekeeper in the house, who should be held responsible to the Health Department. Unluckily the powers of the Board gave out at that point, and the proposition was never acted upon. Could it have been, much trouble would have been spared the Health Board, and untold suffering the tenants in many houses. The tribe of absentee landlords is by no means extinct in New York. Not a few who fled from across the sea to avoid being crushed by his heel there have groaned under it here, scarcely profiting by the exchange. Sometimes﻿—it can hardly be said in extenuation﻿—the heel that crunches is applied in saddening ignorance. I recall the angry indignation of one of these absentee landlords, a worthy man who, living far away in the country, had inherited city property, when he saw the condition of his slum tenements. The man was shocked beyond expression, all the more because he did not know whom to blame except himself for the state of things that had aroused his wrath, and yet, conscious of the integrity of his intentions, felt that he should not justly be held responsible.

===Chinese===
城中经济状况的急遽变化常常跑在一切救济计划的前头，使昨天还足以应付局面的东西到今天便告无用，这是又一重令人困惑的缘由。还有一种常见的障碍——我倾向于认为它同爱尔兰一样常见，尽管报纸上较少听说——就是在外的房东（absentee landlord）。本地货色的那一种，为着他自认的权利奋战不休，已经够糟的了；而在外房东则要对没完没了的麻烦负责。当年卫生局接手此事的时候，他便是卫生改革者们最早绊上的障碍物之一。卫生局 1869 年报告说，许多房客完全无人照管，他们要求把房屋修整好的请求，得到的惟一答复是一纸通牒：交房租，不然就搬走。「调查常常揭出这样的事实：房产的主人是一位富有的绅士或女士，住在城中某个上流地段，或者住在邻近的城市，偶尔也发现住在欧洲。房产通常全权交由一名经纪人打理，其指示简单而斩钉截铁：预收租金；收不到，就把住户撵出去。」负责此事的委员会提议，强制十户及以上人家的廉租公寓业主在宅内安置一名管家，令其对卫生局负责。不幸的是，委员会的权限到那一点便告用尽，这项提议从未付诸实行。倘若当初实行得了，卫生委员会本可省去许多麻烦，许多房屋里的房客也可免遭说不尽的苦难。在外房东这一族在纽约远没有绝迹。不少人为躲避大洋彼岸那只踩下来的脚跟而逃到这边，却又在同一只脚下呻吟，从这场对调里几乎没占到什么便宜。有时候——这很难说是开脱之词——那只踩碾下来的脚跟，是在令人悲哀的无知之中落下的。我记得这样一位在外房东的愤怒：一个可敬的人，住在遥远的乡间，继承了一份城里的产业；待他看到自己那些贫民窟廉租公寓的境况时，不由怒不可遏。此人震惊得无以言表，尤其因为这局面激起了他的怒火，而他除了自己之外不知道该怪谁；可他又自觉心地纯正，觉得要他来担这份责任并不公道。

===Original===
The experience of this landlord points directly to the remedy which the law failed to supply to the early reformers. It has since been fully demonstrated that a competent agent on the premises, a man of the best and the highest stamp, who knows how to instruct and guide with a firm hand, is a prerequisite to the success of any reform tenement scheme. This is a plain business proposition, that has been proved entirely sound in some notable instances of tenement building, of which more hereafter. Even among the poorer tenements, those are always the best in which the owner himself lives. It is a hopeful sign in any case. The difficulty of procuring such assistance without having to pay a ruinous price, is one of the obstructions that have vexed in this city efforts to solve the problem of housing the poor properly, because it presupposes that the effort must be made on a larger scale than has often been attempted.

===Chinese===
