这位房东的经历，径直指向了法律未能交到早期改革者手中的那剂药方。此后已经充分证明：宅内有一名得力的代理人——一个品格最好、最高尚的人，懂得怎样以坚定的手加以教导和引路——是任何改革性廉租公寓方案成功的前提。这是一条明白的生意命题，已在几个著名的廉租公寓建筑实例中得到充分验证，这些实例容后再述。即便在较贫穷的廉租公寓中间，房东本人住在内的那些也总是最好的。无论在哪一种情形下，这都是一个有希望的迹象。而不必付出毁灭性代价就觅得这样一名助手之难，正是本市为妥善解决穷人住房问题所做的种种努力为之苦恼的障碍之一，因为它预设了这项努力必须以比通常所尝试的更大的规模展开。
===Original===
The readiness with which the tenants respond to intelligent efforts in their behalf, when made under fair conditions, is as surprising as it is gratifying, and fully proves the claim that tenants are only satisfied in filthy and unwholesome surroundings because nothing better is offered. The moral effect is as great as the improvement of their physical health. It is clearly discernible in the better class of tenement dwellers today. The change in the character of the colored population in the few years since it began to move out of the wicked rookeries of the old “Africa” to the decent tenements in Yorkville, furnishes a notable illustration, and a still better one is found in the contrast between the model tenement in the Mulberry Street Bend and the barracks across the way, of which I spoke in the chapter devoted to the Italian. The Italian himself is the strongest argument of all. With his fatal contentment in the filthiest surroundings, he gives undoubted evidence of having in him the instinct of cleanliness that, properly cultivated, would work his rescue in a very little while. It is a queer contradiction, but the fact is patent to anyone who has observed the man in his home-life. And he is not alone in this. I came across an instance, this past summer, of how a refined, benevolent personality works like a leaven in even the roughest tenement-house crowd. This was no model tenement; far from it. It was a towering barrack in the Tenth Ward, sheltering more than twenty families. All the light and air that entered its interior came through an air-shaft two feet square, upon which two bedrooms and the hall gave in every story. In three years I had known of two domestic tragedies, prompted by poverty and justifiable disgust with life, occurring in the house, and had come to look upon it as a typically bad tenement, quite beyond the pale of possible improvement. What was my surprise, when chance led me to it once more after a while, to find the character of the occupants entirely changed. Some of the old ones were there still, but they did not seem to be the same people. I discovered the secret to be the new housekeeper, a tidy, mild-mannered, but exceedingly strict little body, who had a natural faculty of drawing her depraved surroundings within the beneficent sphere of her strong sympathy, and withal of exacting respect for her orders. The worst elements had been banished from the house in short order under her management, and for the rest a new era of self-respect had dawned. They were, as a body, as vastly superior to the general run of their class as they had before seemed below it. And this had been effected in the short space of a single year.

===Chinese===
房客们只要在公平的条件下得到为他们着想的明智努力，其响应之迅速，既令人惊讶又令人欣慰，并充分证明了这样一个论断：房客们之所以满足于污秽不洁的环境，只因没有更好的东西摆到他们面前。道德上的成效，不亚于他们身体健康的改善。这在今天较上等的廉租公寓住户身上清晰可辨。有色人种人口近几年迁出旧「非洲」（Africa）那些邪恶的大杂院、搬进约克维尔（Yorkville）体面廉租公寓以来品格上的变化，提供了一个显著的例证；而一个更佳的例证，见于桑树街弯道（Mulberry Street Bend）的模范廉租公寓与马路对面那些营房的对照之中——那是我在专写意大利人的那一章里讲过的。意大利人本人则是最有力的论据。他虽在最污秽的环境里怀着那种认命的知足，却无疑显出他身上带着清洁的天性；这天性一经适当培植，本可以在很短的时间内完成对他的拯救。这是一个古怪的矛盾，但任何一个在家庭生活里观察过此人的人，都看得出这是桩明白的事实。而在这点上，他并非独此一家。今年夏天我碰到过一个实例，可见一个教养有素、宅心仁厚的品格，即便在最粗野的廉租公寓人群中，也能像面里的酵母一样发挥作用。那可不是什么模范廉租公寓，差得远。那是第十选区（Tenth Ward）一座高耸的营房，容庇着二十多户人家。进入它内部的全部光线和空气，都来自一口两英尺见方的通风井（air-shaft），每层楼各有两间卧室和一道走廊开向井口。三年之内，我知悉这宅子里发生过两起家庭惨剧，皆由贫穷和对生活有理由的厌弃促发，于是渐渐把它看作一座典型的坏廉租公寓，全然落在可望改良的范围之外。待到机缘隔了一阵又把我引到那里，我发觉住户的品格已彻底改观，这叫我何等惊讶！有些老住户还在，人却仿佛不是原来那些人了。我发现秘密就出在新来的女管家身上——一个整洁、举止温和、却极其严格的小个子女人，天生便有本领，把她那堕落败坏的周遭拢进她强烈同情心的善惠势力圈里，同时又能叫人恪遵她的号令。最坏的分子在她的管理之下，没费多少工夫便被逐出了宅子；其余的人则迎来一个自尊的新纪元。作为一个整体，他们远远高出其同类的寻常水准，恰如先前他们显得远远低于那一水准。而这一切，是在短短一年的工夫里做成的。

===Original===
My observations on this point are more than confirmed by those of nearly all the practical tenement reformers I have known, who have patiently held to the course they had laid down. One of these, whose experience exceeds that of all of the rest together, and whose influence for good has been very great, said to me recently: “I hold that not ten percent of the people now living in tenements would refuse to avail themselves of the best improved conditions offered, and come fully up to the use of them, properly instructed; but they cannot get them. They are up to them now, fully, if the chances were only offered. They don’t have to come up. It is all a gigantic mistake on the part of the public, of which these poor people are the victims. I have built homes for more than five hundred families in fourteen years, and I have been getting daily more faith in human nature from my work among the poor tenants, though approaching that nature on a plane and under conditions that could scarcely promise better for disappointment.” It is true that my friend has built his houses in Brooklyn; but human nature does not differ greatly on the two shores of the East River. For those who think it does, it may be well to remember that only five years ago the Tenement House Commission summed up the situation in this city in the declaration that, “the condition of the tenants is in advance of the houses which they occupy,” quite the severest arraignment of the tenement that had yet been uttered.

===Chinese===
我在这一点上的观察，得到了我认识的几乎所有务实的廉租公寓改革者的印证乃至更甚，他们都耐着性子坚持着自己定下的路线。其中一位，其经验超过其余诸人加在一起的总和，其向善的影响非常之大，最近对我说：「我的看法是：如今住在廉租公寓里的人，不到百分之十会拒绝利用向他们提供的最完善的改良条件，并在适当的指导下完全胜任这些条件的用法；只是他们得不到罢了。只要机会真给了出来，他们现在就已经达得到了，完完全全达得到。他们根本用不着往上够。这是公众方面一个天大的错误，这些穷人正是它的受害者。十四年里，我为五百多户人家盖了住房；我在贫穷房客中间做的工，使我一天比一天更相信人性——尽管我接近这种天性时所凭借的那一层面和那些条件，几乎再好不过地预示着失望。」我这位朋友的房子是盖在布鲁克林（Brooklyn）的，这话不假；可人性在东河（East River）两岸并不会相差太远。若有谁以为不然，那么不妨记住：仅仅五年以前，廉租公寓调查委员会（Tenement House Commission）在本市情况的总结中宣告：「房客的状况，超前于他们所居住的房屋」——这是迄至当时对廉租公寓最严厉的一纸控状。

===Original===
The many philanthropic efforts that have been made in the last few years to render less intolerable the lot of the tenants in the homes where many of them must continue to live, have undoubtedly had their effect in creating a disposition to accept better things, that will make plainer sailing for future builders of model tenements. In many ways, as in the “College Settlement” of courageous girls, the Neighborhood Guilds, through the efforts of the King’s Daughters, and numerous other schemes of practical mission work, the poor and the well-to-do have been brought closer together, in an everyday companionship that cannot but be productive of the best results, to the one who gives no less than to the one who receives. And thus, as a good lady wrote to me once, though the problem stands yet unsolved, more perplexing than ever; though the bright spots in the dreary picture be too often bright only by comparison, and many of the expedients hit upon for relief sad makeshifts, we can dimly discern behind it all that good is somehow working out of even this slough of despond the while it is deepening and widening in our sight, and in His own good season, if we labor on with courage and patience, will bear fruit sixty and a hundred fold.

===Chinese===
近几年来，为减轻许多房客在其不得不继续栖身的家中那份难以忍受的命运，人们做了许多慈善的努力；这些努力无疑已经收效，培植出一种乐于接受更好事物的意向，将使日后的模范廉租公寓建造者们的航程更为平坦。以许多方式——比如勇敢姑娘们的「大学睦邻社」（College Settlement）、借「国王之女会」（King’s Daughters）之力办起的邻里公会（Neighborhood Guilds），以及无数其他务实的传教工作方案——穷人与小康之家被拉得更近了，处在一种日常的交往之中；这种交往对给予的一方，无亚于对接受的一方，都势必结出最好的果实。于是，正如一位好心的女士有一次写信对我说的：尽管问题依然悬而未解，比以往更加令人困惑；尽管这幅阴郁画面上的亮点，往往只因两相对照才显得明亮，许多为解困想出的应急之道，也只是可悲的权宜之计；我们仍能隐约看出，在这一切的背后，善正以某种方式，甚至从这绝望的泥沼（slough of despond，典出班扬《天路历程》）之中酝酿出来——虽然它在我们眼前正愈陷愈深、愈扩愈宽；只要我们怀着勇气与耐心劳作不辍，到了祂自己认定的好时节，便会结出六十倍与一百倍的果实。

## XXV How the Case Stands / XXV 现状如何

===Original===
What, then, are the bald facts with which we have to deal in New York?
1. That we have a tremendous, ever swelling crowd of wage-earners which it is our business to house decently.
2. That it is not housed decently.
3. That it must be so housed *here* for the present, and for a long time to come, all schemes of suburban relief being as yet utopian, impracticable.
4. That it pays high enough rents to entitle it to be so housed, as a right.
5. That nothing but our own slothfulness is in the way of so housing it, since “the condition of the tenants is in advance of the condition of the houses which they occupy” (Report of Tenement-house Commission).
6. That the security of the one no less than of the other half demands, on sanitary, moral, and economic grounds, that it be decently housed.
7. That it will pay to do it. As an investment, I mean, and in hard cash. This I shall immediately proceed to prove.
8. That the tenement has come to stay, and must itself be the solution of the problem with which it confronts us.

===Chinese===
那么，我们在纽约所要对付的，究竟是哪些赤裸裸的事实？
1. 我们有着一支庞大而不断膨胀的挣工钱者的人群，让他们住得像样，是我们的本分。
2. 他们如今住得并不像样。
3. 眼下、乃至此后很长一个时期，他们都必须在*这里*、就地得到像样的安顿，因为一切郊区纾解的方案至今仍是乌托邦，行不通。
4. 他们缴的房租已经高到足以使他们有权得到这样的安顿——这是他们应得的权利。
5. 挡着这条路、使我们无法如此安顿他们的，唯有我们自己的怠惰，因为“房客的境况已走在他们所住房屋的境况前头”（《廉租公寓调查委员会报告》）。
6. 这一半人的安全，与「另一半人」的安全一样，都本着卫生、道德与经济的理由，要求他们住得像样。
7. 这样做是划算的。我是说，作为一笔投资，而且以硬碰硬的现金计算。这一点我马上就来证明。
8. 廉租公寓既已长驻不去，就必须由它自身来充当它摆在我们面前这个难题的解。

===Original===
This is the fact from which we cannot get away, however we may deplore it. Doubtless the best would be to get rid of it altogether; but as we cannot, all argument on that score may at this time be dismissed as idle. The practical question is what to do with the tenement. I watched a Mott Street landlord, the owner of a row of barracks that have made no end of trouble for the health authorities for twenty years, solve that question for himself the other day. His way was to give the wretched pile a coat of paint, and put a gorgeous tin cornice on with the year 1890 in letters a yard long. From where I stood watching the operation, I looked down upon the same dirty crowds camping on the roof, foremost among them an Italian mother with two stark-naked children who had apparently never made the acquaintance of a washtub. That was a landlord’s way, and will not get us out of the mire.

===Chinese===
这个事实我们无从回避，无论它多么令人痛心。毫无疑问，最好的办法莫过于把它整个清除掉；但既然做不到，在这个问题上的一切争论，此刻都可以当作废话打发掉。实际的问题在于：拿廉租公寓怎么办。前几天，我眼看着莫特街（Mott Street）的一位房东——他名下有一排二十年来给卫生当局惹下无穷麻烦的营房——自己解答了这个问题。他的办法，是给那座破楼刷上一层油漆，再装上一道气派的马口铁檐口，用一码长的字母拼出 1890 的年号。从我驻足观看施工的地方俯望下去，还是那几群脏兮兮的人照旧在屋顶上扎营，打头的是一位意大利母亲，带着两个光溜溜的、显然从不曾结识过洗衣盆的孩子。这便是一位房东的办法，它可不能把我们从泥沼里捞出来。

===Original===
The “flat” is another way that does not solve the problem. Rather, it extends it. The flat is not a model, though it is a modern, tenement. It gets rid of some of the nuisances of the low tenement, and of the worst of them, the overcrowding﻿—if it gets rid of them at all﻿—at a cost that takes it at once out of the catalogue of “homes for the poor,” while imposing some of the evils from which they suffer upon those who ought to escape from them.

===Chinese===
「公寓」（“flat”）是另一条出路，却同样解决不了问题；毋宁说，它把问题延续了下去。公寓虽是现代的廉租公寓，却算不上模范廉租公寓。它除去了低级廉租公寓的若干弊病，连同其中最恶劣的过度拥挤——倘若它当真除去了的话——但所付的代价，使它当即被从「穷人住所」的名录中除名；与此同时，它还把穷人所受的另一些祸害，加到那些本该免遭其害的人身上。

===Original===
There are three effective ways of dealing with the tenements in New York:
1. By law.
2. By remodelling and making the most out of the old houses.
3. By building new, model tenements.

===Chinese===
在纽约，对付廉租公寓有三条行之有效的途径：
1. 靠法律。
2. 靠改造旧屋，把它们用到极致。
3. 靠兴建全新的模范廉租公寓。

===Original===
Private enterprise﻿—conscience, to put it in the category of duties, where it belongs﻿—must do the lion’s share under these last two heads. Of what the law has effected I have spoken already. The drastic measures adopted in Paris, in Glasgow, and in London are not practicable here on anything like as large a scale. Still it can, under strong pressure of public opinion, rid us of the worst plague-spots. The Mulberry Street Bend will go the way of the Five Points when all the red tape that binds the hands of municipal effort has been unwound. Prizes were offered in public competition, some years ago, for the best plans of modern tenement-houses. It may be that we shall see the day when the building of model tenements will be encouraged by subsidies in the way of a rebate of taxes. Meanwhile the arrest and summary punishment of landlords, or their agents, who persistently violate law and decency, will have a salutary effect. If a few of the wealthy absentee landlords, who are the worst offenders, could be got within the jurisdiction of the city, and by arrest be compelled to employ proper overseers, it would be a proud day for New York. To remedy the overcrowding, with which the night inspections of the sanitary police cannot keep step, tenements may eventually have to be licensed, as now the lodging-houses, to hold so many tenants, and no more; or the State may have to bring down the rents that cause the crowding, by assuming the right to regulate them as it regulates the fares on the elevated roads. I throw out the suggestion, knowing quite well that it is open to attack. It emanated originally from one of the brightest minds that have had to struggle officially with this tenement-house question in the last ten years. In any event, to succeed, reform by law must aim at making it unprofitable to own a bad tenement. At best, it is apt to travel at a snail’s pace, while the enemy it pursues is putting the best foot foremost.

===Chinese===
私营企业——若要把它归进它本属的义务一类，那便叫良心——在后面两项名下必须担起最重的一份。法律已经成就了什么，我已经说过了。巴黎、格拉斯哥与伦敦所采取的那些严厉措施，在这里无法以哪怕相近的规模推行。但在舆论的强大压力之下，它仍能替我们铲除最恶劣的瘟疫窝点。等到捆住市政手脚的官样红线尽数解开，桑树街弯道（Mulberry Street Bend）便会走上五点区（Five Points）走过的那条路。几年前，曾公开悬奖征集新式廉租公寓的最佳设计。也许有朝一日，我们会看到模范廉租公寓的营造以减免税款形式的补贴得到鼓励。与此同时，对屡屡践踏法律与体统的房东或其代理人加以逮捕、从速惩办，将产生有益的效果。倘若那几个身为最恶劣违犯者的富有在外房东（absentee landlords）能被弄到本市司法管辖之内，进而经由逮捕被迫雇用称职的监管人，那将是纽约扬眉吐气的一天。为治理过度拥挤——卫生警察的夜间查访已赶不上它膨胀的脚步——廉租公寓最终或许不得不像如今的廉价寄宿所那样领取执照，限定收住若干房客，多一名也不许；否则，州政府或许不得不把造成拥挤的房租压下来，办法是接过管制房租的权利，一如它管制高架铁路的票价。我抛出这个建议，心里很清楚它易受攻击。它最初出自过去十年间不得不以官方身份与这个廉租公寓问题缠斗的头脑中最明亮的一颗。无论如何，以法律推行的改革若要成功，必须瞄着一个目标：叫人拥有一座恶劣的廉租公寓无利可图。往好里说，它往往也只能以蜗牛的速度前行，而它追赶的敌人却正撒开大步、抢先飞奔。

===Original===
In this matter of profit the law ought to have its strongest ally in the landlord himself, though the reverse is the case. This condition of things I believe to rest on a monstrous error. It cannot be that tenement property that is worth preserving at all can continue to yield larger returns, if allowed to run down, than if properly cared for and kept in good repair. The point must be reached, and soon, where the cost of repairs, necessary with a house full of the lowest, most ignorant tenants, must overbalance the saving of the first few years of neglect; for this class is everywhere the most destructive, as well as the poorest paying. I have the experience of owners, who have found this out to their cost, to back me up in the assertion, even if it were not the statement of a plain business fact that proves itself. I do not include tenement property that is deliberately allowed to fall into decay because at some future time the ground will be valuable for business or other purposes. There is unfortunately enough of that kind in New York, often leasehold property owned by wealthy estates or soulless corporations that oppose all their great influence to the efforts of the law in behalf of their tenants.

===Chinese===
在利润这件事上，法律本该在房东本人身上找到自己最强有力的盟友，实情却恰恰相反。我相信，这种局面立足于一个大得离谱的错误。一处尚值保全的廉租房产，任其败坏下去，竟还能比妥善照料、勤加修缮产出更丰的回报——这是不可能的。有一个临界点必定会到，而且很快：住满最下层、最无知房客的房子的必要修缮开支，终将压过头几年疏于照管省下的那几个钱；因为这一类人在哪里都是破坏性最强、付租最少的。即便这不是一条不证自明的平常生意事实，也有那些吃了亏才认清此理的业主的经验，替我这句话撑腰。我不把这样一种廉租房产算在内：它是被刻意任其朽坏的，因为来日那块地皮将因商业或其他用途而变得值钱。可惜纽约这类房产着实不少，常常是豪族遗产或没有灵魂的公司持有的租地房产，它们把自身全部庞大的影响力，都用来对抗法律为房客利益所作的努力。

===Original===
There is abundant evidence, on the other hand, that it can be made to pay to improve and make the most of the worst tenement property, even in the most wretched locality. The example set by Miss Ellen Collins in her Water Street houses will always stand as a decisive answer to all doubts on this point. It is quite ten years since she bought three old tenements at the corner of Water and Roosevelt Streets, then as now one of the lowest localities in the city. Since then she has leased three more adjoining her purchase, and so much of Water Street has at all events been purified. Her first effort was to let in the light in the hallways, and with the darkness disappeared, as if by magic, the heaps of refuse that used to be piled up beside the sinks. A few of the most refractory tenants disappeared with them, but a very considerable proportion stayed, conforming readily to the new rules, and are there yet. It should here be stated that Miss Collins’s tenants are distinctly of the poorest. Her purpose was to experiment with this class, and her experiment has been more than satisfactory. Her plan was, as she puts it herself, fair play between tenant and landlord. To this end the rents were put as low as consistent with the idea of a business investment that must return a reasonable interest to be successful. The houses were thoroughly refitted with proper plumbing. A competent janitor was put in charge to see that the rules were observed by the tenants, when Miss Collins herself was not there. Of late years she has had to give very little time to personal superintendence, and the caretaker told me only the other day that very little was needed. The houses seemed to run themselves in the groove once laid down. Once the reputed haunt of thieves, they have become the most orderly in the neighborhood. Clothes are left hanging on the lines all night with impunity, and the pretty flowerbeds in the yard where the children not only from the six houses, but of the whole block, play, skip, and swing, are undisturbed. The tenants, by the way, provide the flowers themselves in the spring, and take all the more pride in them because they are their own. The six houses contain forty-five families, and there “has never been any need of putting up a bill.” As to the income from the property, Miss Collins said to me last August: “I have had six and even six and three-quarters percent on the capital invested; on the whole, you may safely say five and a half percent. This I regard as entirely satisfactory.” It should be added that she has persistently refused to let the corner-store, now occupied by a butcher, as a saloon; or her income from it might have been considerably increased.

===Chinese===
另一方面，大量证据表明：即便是坐落在最悲惨地段的最恶劣的廉租房产，加以改良、用到极致，也能做到有利可图。埃伦·柯林斯小姐（Miss Ellen Collins）在水街（Water Street）那几座房子上立下的榜样，将永远作为对这方面一切疑问的决定性回答。她买下水街与罗斯福街（Roosevelt Street）拐角上的三座旧廉租公寓，至今已足足十年；那地方当时和现在一样，都是全城最下等的地段之一。此后她又租下紧挨着所购房产的三座，水街的这一段无论如何算是得到了净化。她做的头一件事，是让光线照进走廊；随着黑暗消散，往日堆在水槽旁的垃圾也仿佛着了魔法似的不见了。几个最不听话的房客同垃圾一道消失了，但相当大的一部分留了下来，欣然遵从新的规矩，至今还住在那里。应当说明的是，柯林斯小姐的房客清一色属于最贫穷的那一类。她的目的正是拿这一类人做实验，而她的实验结果，远超满意之上。她的方案，用她自己的话说，便是房客与房东之间的公平对待（fair play）。为达到这个目的，房租定在这样一个低限上：它须不失为一桩生意投资，而生意投资要算成功，就必须赚回合理的利息。房子彻底翻修，装了像样的管道。一名称职的看管人受命监理，在柯林斯小姐本人不在时督促房客遵守规矩。近年来她已很少需要亲自照管，看管人前两天还告诉我，如今几乎用不着照管了。这些房子仿佛顺着一经铺定的轨道自行运转。它们一度是窃贼出没的名声之地，如今已成全街区最有秩序的宅子。衣裳挂在晾衣绳上过夜也无人染指；院子里漂漂亮亮的花坛——不仅六座房子的孩子、连整个街区的小朋友都在那里游戏、蹦跳、荡秋千——从不遭践踏。顺便说一句，花是房客们春天自己栽的，正因为是自己的，才格外引以为豪。六座房子住着四十五户人家，而那里“从来用不着贴出一张告示”。至于这项产业的收益，柯林斯小姐去年八月对我说：“我拿到过百分之六、甚至百分之六点七五的投入资本回报；总的说来，你大可说是百分之五点五。我认为这完全令人满意。”还应补充一句：她始终拒绝把如今由肉贩租用的那间街角店面租作酒馆，否则她从中得到的收益本可以增加不少。

===Original===
Miss Collins’s experience is of value chiefly as showing what can be accomplished with the worst possible material, by the sort of personal interest in the poor that alone will meet their real needs. All the charity in the world, scattered with the most lavish hand, will not take its place. “Fair play” between landlord and tenant is the key, too long mislaid, that unlocks the door to success everywhere as it did for Miss Collins. She has not lacked imitators whose experience has been akin to her own. The case of Gotham Court has been already cited. On the other hand, instances are not wanting of landlords who have undertaken the task, but have tired of it or sold their property before it had been fully redeemed, with the result that it relapsed into its former bad condition faster than it had improved, and the tenants with it. I am inclined to think that such houses are liable to fall even below the average level. Backsliding in brick and mortar does not greatly differ from similar performances in flesh and blood.

===Chinese===
柯林斯小姐这段经验的可贵之处，主要在于它显示了：凭着那种唯有真正能满足穷人实际需要的、对穷人的切身关怀，连最不堪的材料也能做出成绩来。世上全部的慈善，哪怕撒钱撒得再慷慨，也顶替不了它。房东与房客之间的“公平对待”，是一把搁置太久的钥匙，它在柯林斯小姐手里打开了成功之门，在别处也一样。效法她而经历与她相仿者，也不乏其人。哥谭院（Gotham Court）的案例前面已经引过了。另一方面，也不乏这样的事例：房东承担起这项任务，却在房产尚未完全整治好之前厌倦了，或者把它卖掉，结果房子跌回原先败坏境地的速度比它改良的速度还快，房客也跟着一同跌回去。我倾向于认为，这样的房子甚至可能跌到平均水准以下。砖石灰浆的故态复萌，同血肉之躯的故态复萌，并没有多大分别。

===Original===
Backed by a strong and steady sentiment, such as these pioneers have evinced, that would make it the personal business of wealthy owners with time to spare to look after their tenants, the law would be able in a very short time to work a salutary transformation in the worst quarters, to the lasting advantage, I am well persuaded, of the landlord no less than the tenant. Unfortunately, it is in this quality of personal effort that the sentiment of interest in the poor, upon which we have to depend, is too often lacking. People who are willing to give money feel that that ought to be enough. It is not. The money thus given is too apt to be wasted along with the sentiment that prompted the gift.

===Chinese===
倘若有这些先行者所显示的那种坚定而持久的道义心作后盾——它会促使有闲的富有业主把照管房客当作自己的私事——法律便能在极短的时间内，在最恶劣的街区促成一场有益的转变；我深信，房东由此受惠之持久，将不亚于房客。不幸的是，我们所倚赖的那种关怀穷人的道义心，最常缺少的恰恰是亲身出力这一味。肯出钱的人觉得，出了钱就该足够了。其实不够。这样散出去的钱，多半会连同促使捐输的那份心意一道糟蹋掉。

===Original===
Even when it comes to the third of the ways I spoke of as effective in dealing with the tenement-house problem, the building of model structures, the personal interest in the matter must form a large share of the capital invested, if it is to yield full returns. Where that is the case, there is even less doubt about its paying, with ordinary business management, than in the case of reclaiming an old building, which is, like putting life into a defunct newspaper, pretty apt to be uphill work. Model tenement building has not been attempted in New York on anything like as large a scale as in many other great cities, and it is perhaps owing to this, in a measure, that a belief prevails that it cannot succeed here. This is a wrong notion entirely. The various undertakings of that sort that have been made here under intelligent management have, as far as I know, all been successful.

===Chinese===
即便轮到我所说的解决廉租公寓问题的第三条有效途径——兴建模范房屋——那份对事业的切身关怀，也必须构成所投资本中可观的一份，才可能产生足额的回报。只要具备这一条，那么在寻常的生意经营之下，它之有利可图，比改造一座旧楼还要更少悬念——改造旧楼好比给一份断了气的报纸注入生命，十有八九是桩上坡的苦活。纽约的模范廉租公寓营造，尝试的规模远不及许多其他大城市；也许正因为这一点，人们才普遍相信它在这里成不了。这个想法完全错了。据我所知，凡在此间以明智经营作出的此类尝试，无一不获成功。

===Original===
From the managers of the two best-known experiments in model tenement building in the city, the Improved Dwellings Association and the Tenement-house Building Company, I have letters dated last August, declaring their enterprises eminently successful. There is no reason why their experience should not be conclusive. That the Philadelphia plan is not practicable in New York is not a good reason why our own plan, which is precisely the reverse of our neighbor’s, should not be. In fact it is an argument for its success. The very reason why we cannot house our working masses in cottages, as has been done in Philadelphia﻿—viz., that they must live on Manhattan Island, where the land is too costly for small houses﻿—is the best guarantee of the success of the model tenement house, properly located and managed. The drift in tenement building, as in everything else, is toward concentration, and helps smooth the way. Four families on the floor, twenty in the house, is the rule of today. As the crowds increase, the need of guiding this drift into safe channels becomes more urgent. The larger the scale upon which the model tenement is planned, the more certain the promise of success. The utmost ingenuity cannot build a house for sixteen or twenty families on a lot 25 × 100 feet in the middle of a block like it, that shall give them the amount of air and sunlight to be had by the erection of a dozen or twenty houses on a common plan around a central yard. This was the view of the committee that awarded the prizes for the best plan for the conventional tenement, ten years ago. It coupled its verdict with the emphatic declaration that, in its view, it was “impossible to secure the requirements of physical and moral health within these narrow and arbitrary limits.” Houses have been built since on better plans than any the committee saw, but its judgment stands unimpaired. A point, too, that is not to be overlooked, is the reduced cost of expert superintendence﻿—the first condition of successful management﻿—in the larger buildings.

===Chinese===
本市两场最著名的模范廉租公寓营造实验——改良住宅协会（Improved Dwellings Association）与廉租公寓建筑公司（Tenement-house Building Company）——的主持者们，去年八月都有信给我，宣称他们的事业极为成功。他们的经验没有理由不作数。费城（Philadelphia）的方案在纽约行不通，并不能成为我们自己的方案也行不通的理由——我们的方案恰恰与邻城的相反。事实上，这反倒论证了它的成功。我们之所以不能像费城那样让劳动大众住进小独栋——也就是说，因为他们必须住在曼哈顿岛上，那里的地价太贵，盖不起小房子——而这正是选址得当、经营得法的模范廉租公寓必获成功的最好保证。廉租公寓营造的流向，正如万事万物的流向，都朝着集中而去，这反倒帮着铺平了道路。一层楼四户、一座楼二十户，是今天的成规。随着人潮日增，把这股流向导入安全航道的需要便愈发紧迫。模范廉租公寓规划的规模越大，成功的把握便越确凿。任凭匠心怎样用尽，也无法在街区当中一块 25 × 100 英尺的地皮上，为十六户或二十户人家盖出一座房子，使其空气与阳光比得上十几座或二十座房子循着同一设计、环着一个中央庭院建起来所能给的分量。这正是十年前为传统式廉租公寓最佳设计颁发奖金的那个评委会所持的见解。评委会在裁定的同时郑重宣告：依它之见，“要在这般狭隘而武断的限度之内保全身体健康与道德健康的要求，是不可能的”。此后也确有按比评委会当年所见更佳的设计建成的房子，但它的判断至今不曾折损。还有一点也不容忽视：建筑越大，行家监理的费用便越省——而监理正是经营成功的第一条件。

===Original===
The Improved Dwellings Association put up its block of thirteen houses in East Seventy-second Street nine years ago. Their cost, estimated at about $240,000 with the land, was increased to $285,000 by troubles with the contractor engaged to build them. Thus the Association’s task did not begin under the happiest auspices. Unexpected expenses came to deplete its treasury. The neighborhood was new and not crowded at the start. No expense was spared, and the benefit of all the best and most recent experience in tenement building was given to the tenants. The families were provided with from two to four rooms, all “outer” rooms, of course, at rents ranging from $14 per month for the four on the ground floor, to $6.25 for two rooms on the top floor. Coal lifts, ash-chutes, common laundries in the basement, and free baths, are features of these buildings that were then new enough to be looked upon with suspicion by the doubting Thomases who predicted disaster. There are rooms in the block for 218 families, and when I looked in recently all but nine of the apartments were let. One of the nine was rented while I was in the building. The superintendent told me that he had little trouble with disorderly tenants, though the buildings shelter all sorts of people. Mr. W. Bayard Cutting, the President of the Association, writes to me:

===Chinese===
改良住宅协会在东第七十二街（East Seventy-second Street）的那一排十三座房子，是九年前建起来的。连地皮在内，造价原估计约 $240,000，因与承建承包商的纠葛而增加到 $285,000。协会的事业便这样在一开始就兆头不佳。意外的开支又来耗竭它的金库。这一带在起步时还是新区，并不拥挤。协会不惜工本，把廉租公寓营造方面一切最佳、最新经验的好处都给了房客。各户分得两到四个房间——自然全是「外间」——月租从底层四间的 $14 到顶层两间的 $6.25 不等。运煤升降机、倒灰槽、地下室的公共洗衣房与免费浴室，都是这些房子的特色，在当时新得足以招来那班预言灾祸的“多疑的多马”（doubting Thomases）们的疑心。这一排房子的房间可容 218 户；我最近去探访时，套房只有九套未曾租出。我还在楼里的时候，那九套里又租出去了一套。管理员告诉我，楼里虽然收容着各色人等，他却很少为不守秩序的房客烦心。协会主席 W. 贝亚德·卡廷先生（Mr. W. Bayard Cutting）写信给我：

===Original===
“By the terms of subscription to the stock before incorporation, dividends were limited to five percent on the stock of the Improved Dwellings Association. These dividends have been paid (two percent each six months) ever since the expiration of the first six months of the buildings operation. All surplus has been expended upon the buildings. New and expensive roofs have been put on for the comfort of such tenants as might choose to use them. The buildings have been completely painted inside and out in a manner not contemplated at the outset. An expensive set of fire-escapes has been put on at the command of the Fire Department, and a considerable number of other improvements made. *I regard the experiment as eminently successful and satisfactory*, particularly when it is considered that the buildings were the first erected in this city upon anything like a large scale, where it was proposed to meet the architectural difficulties that present themselves in the tenement-house problem. I have no doubt that the experiment could be tried today with the improved knowledge which has come with time, and a much larger return be shown upon the investment. The results referred to have been attained in spite of the provision which prevents the selling of liquor upon the Association’s premises. You are aware, of course, how much larger rent can be obtained for a liquor saloon than for an ordinary store. An investment at five percent net upon real estate security worth more than the principal sum, ought to be considered desirable.”

===Chinese===
“依照注册之前认股的条款，改良住宅协会股票的股息以百分之五为限。自楼房启用后头六个月期满以来，这份股息（每六个月百分之二）从未间断。全部盈余都花在了楼房上。屋顶换上了造价不菲的新顶，好让愿意上去使用的房客得以舒舒服服。楼房里里外外彻底油漆了一遍，这是当初不曾料到的。应消防局之令装上了一整套昂贵的太平梯（fire-escape），另外还作了不少别的改进。*我认为这项实验极为成功、极为令人满意*，尤其考虑到：这些楼房乃是本城头一批在近乎这样的规模上兴建、立志迎头解决廉租公寓难题中出现的种种建筑难题的建筑。我毫不怀疑，今天凭着与时俱来的更成熟的知识重作这项实验，投资所能显示的回报会大得多。上述种种结果，是在禁止在协会物业内售酒的条款约束之下取得的。您当然清楚，租作酒馆能收到的租金比租作普通店面要高出多少。一笔以价值超过本金的不动产作保、净得百分之五的投资，理应被视为称心的了。”

===Original===
The Tenement House Building Company made its “experiment” in a much more difficult neighborhood, Cherry Street, some six years later. Its houses shelter many Russian Jews, and the difficulty of keeping them in order is correspondingly increased, particularly as there are no ash-chutes in the houses. It has been necessary even to shut the children out of the yards upon which the kitchen windows give, lest they be struck by something thrown out by the tenants, and killed. It is the Cherry Street style, not easily got rid of. Nevertheless, the houses are well kept. Of the one hundred and six “apartments,” only four were vacant in August. Professor Edwin R. A. Seligman, the secretary of the company, writes to me: “The tenements are now a decided success.” In the three years since they were built, they have returned an interest of from five to five and a half percent on the capital invested. The original intention of making the tenants profit-sharers on a plan of rent insurance, under which all earnings above four percent would be put to the credit of the tenants, has not yet been carried out.

===Chinese===
廉租公寓建筑公司把它的「实验」做在一个难办得多的地段——樱桃街（Cherry Street）——那是约莫六年之后的事了。它的房屋收容着许多俄国犹太人（Russian Jews），维持秩序的难度随之加重，尤其因为楼里没有倒灰槽。甚至不得不把孩子挡在厨房窗户所临的院子之外，唯恐他们被房客扔出来的东西砸中丧命。这是樱桃街的做派，一时半会儿改不掉。尽管如此，这些房子照管得很好。一百零六套「套房」里，八月间只有四套空着。公司秘书埃德温·R. A. 塞利格曼教授（Professor Edwin R. A. Seligman）写信给我：“这些廉租公寓如今已是确定无疑的成功。”建成三年以来，它们已带来所投资本百分之五至百分之五点五的利息。让房客按一种「房租保险」方案分享利润——凡超出百分之四的进项都记入房客名下——这一最初打算，尚未付诸实行。

===Original===
A scheme of dividends to tenants on a somewhat similar plan has been carried out by a Brooklyn builder, Mr. A. T. White, who has devoted a life of beneficent activity to tenement building, and whose experience, though it has been altogether across the East River, I regard as justly applying to New York as well. He so regards it himself. Discussing the cost of building, he says: “There is not the slightest reason to doubt that the financial result of a similar undertaking in any tenement-house district of New York City would be equally good.﻿ ﻿… High cost of land is no detriment, provided the value is made by the pressure of people seeking residence there. Rents in New York City bear a higher ratio to Brooklyn rents than would the cost of land and building in the one city to that in the other.” The assertion that Brooklyn furnishes a better class of tenants than the tenement districts in New York would not be worth discussing seriously, even if Mr. White did not meet it himself with the statement that the proportion of day-laborers and sewing-women in his houses is greater than in any of the London model tenements, showing that they reach the humblest classes.

===Chinese===
布鲁克林（Brooklyn）营造商 A. T. 怀特先生（Mr. A. T. White）已把一项大同小异的向房客分红的方案付诸实行。他把一生行善的劳绩献给了廉租公寓营造，他的经验虽然全部得自东河对岸，我却认为完全可以正当地适用于纽约。他自己也这样看。讨论到建造成本时，他说：“丝毫没有理由怀疑，在纽约市任何一个廉租公寓地段兴办同样的事业，财务结果会同样好。 …… 地价高并不是坏事，只要这价值是由寻地安家的人潮压出来的。纽约市的房租之于布鲁克林的房租，其比例高于这一城的地产与建筑成本之于那一城。”有人说布鲁克林供给的房客比纽约廉租公寓地段的好；这话本不值认真一驳——何况怀特先生自己就给出了回答：他楼里日工与缝衣女工所占的比例，比伦敦任何一处模范廉租公寓里的都高，可见他的房子伸到的正是最卑微的阶层。

===Original===
Mr. White has built homes for five hundred poor families since he began his work, and has made it pay well enough to allow good tenants a share in the profits, averaging nearly one month’s rent out of the twelve, as a premium upon promptness and order. The plan of his last tenements, reproduced here, may be justly regarded as the beau ideal of the model tenement for a great city like New York. It embodies all the good features of Sir Sydney Waterlow’s London plan, with improvements suggested by the builder’s own experience. Its chief merit is that it gathers three hundred real homes, not simply three hundred families, under one roof. Three tenants, it will be seen, everywhere live together. Of the rest of the three hundred they may never know, rarely see, one. Each has his private front-door. The common hall, with all that it stands for, has disappeared. The fireproof stairs are outside the house, a perfect fire-escape. Each tenant has his own scullery and ash-flue. There are no air-shafts, for they are not needed. Every room, under the admirable arrangement of the plan, looks out either upon the street or the yard, that is nothing less than a great park with a playground set apart for the children, where they may dig in the sand to their heart’s content. Weekly concerts are given in the park by a brass band. The drying of clothes is done on the roof, where racks are fitted up for the purpose. The outside stairways end in turrets that give the buildings a very smart appearance. Mr. White never has any trouble with his tenants, though he gathers in the poorest; nor do his tenements have anything of the “institution character” that occasionally attaches to ventures of this sort, to their damage. They are like a big village of contented people, who live in peace with one another because they have elbow-room even under one big roof.

===Chinese===
开工以来，怀特先生已为五百户穷苦家庭造起了住家，而且把这桩事业经营得足够有利，好让守规矩的房客分享利润——十二个月的房租里平均将近一个月，作为对缴租及时、行事守序的奖励。他最后这批廉租公寓的平面图已复制于本书此处，它堪称像纽约这样一座大城市的模范廉租公寓的完美典范（beau ideal）。它吸纳了悉尼·沃特洛爵士（Sir Sydney Waterlow）伦敦方案的全部优点，又加上营造商本人经验所提示的改良。它的主要长处，在于把三百个真正的家——而不止是三百户人家——收在同一个屋顶之下。可以看出，走到哪里都是三户合住一处；至于其余那二百多户，他们或许一个也不认识，难得同哪一户打照面。每户有自己独用的前门。公共走廊连同它所代表的一切，都消失了。防火楼梯设在楼外，本身就是十全十美的太平梯。每户有自己的洗涤间和倒灰井道。楼里没有通风井，因为用不着。在这套令人赞叹的设计安排之下，每个房间不是朝街便是朝院——那院子俨然是一座大公园，还专门辟出儿童游戏场，孩子们尽可以在沙地里挖个痛快。公园里每周有铜管乐队举行音乐会。衣裳在屋顶上晾晒，那里为此装了晾架。户外楼梯尽头的塔楼，给这几座房子添上十分神气的模样。怀特先生收容的全是最穷的人，却从来不为房客操心；他的廉租公寓也丝毫不沾这一类事业偶尔难免染上的、只败坏声誉的「机构色彩」。它们就像一座住满知足常乐者的大村庄，人们彼此和睦相处，因为即便同在一个大屋顶下，也各有各的回旋余地。

===Original===
Enough has been said to show that model tenements can be built successfully and made to pay in New York, if the owner will be content with the five or six percent he does not even dream of when investing his funds in “governments” at three or four. It is true that in the latter case he has only to cut off his coupons and cash them. But the extra trouble of looking after his tenement property, that is the condition of his highest and lasting success, is the penalty exacted for the sins of our fathers that “shall be visited upon the children, unto the third and fourth generation.” We shall indeed be well off, if it stop there. I fear there is too much reason to believe that our own iniquities must be added to transmit the curse still further. And yet, such is the leavening influence of a good deed in that dreary desert of sin and suffering, that the erection of a single good tenement has the power to change, gradually but surely, the character of a whole bad block. It sets up a standard to which the neighborhood must rise, if it cannot succeed in dragging it down to its own low level.

===Chinese===
要说的话已经说得够多了，足以表明：在纽约，模范廉租公寓是能够成功兴建并做到有利可图的——只要业主肯满足于百分之五六的回报，这回报是他把资金投进利率不过百分之三四的「公债」（governments）时做梦也想不到的。不错，买公债他只消剪下息票去兑钱。但看管廉租房产多操的那份心，恰是他赢得最高而持久成功的条件——那是为我们父辈的罪愆所索的罚金，那罪愆“必追讨到儿女身上，直到三四代”。倘若刑罚到此为止，我们真要算是幸运的了。我怕有太多理由相信，还得把我们自己的罪孽添上去，把咒诅再传得更远。然而，在那片罪恶与苦难的荒凉沙漠里，一桩善行自有它酵母般的影响力：只要建起一座像样的廉租公寓，就有力量使一整条坏街区的品性渐缓而确定地改变。它立起了一个标准，左邻右舍若不能把它拽落到自己那低下的水准，便只能向它奋起。

---
===Original===
And so this task, too, has come to an end. Whatsoever a man soweth, that shall he also reap. I have aimed to tell the truth as I saw it. If this book shall have borne ever so feeble a hand in garnering a harvest of justice, it has served its purpose. While I was writing these lines I went down to the sea, where thousands from the city were enjoying their summer rest. The ocean slumbered under a cloudless sky. Gentle waves washed lazily over the white sand, where children fled before them with screams of laughter. Standing there and watching their play, I was told that during the fierce storms of winter it happened that this sea, now so calm, rose in rage and beat down, broke over the bluff, sweeping all before it. No barrier built by human hands had power to stay it then. The sea of a mighty population, held in galling fetters, heaves uneasily in the tenements. Once already our city, to which have come the duties and responsibilities of metropolitan greatness before it was able to fairly measure its task, has felt the swell of its resistless flood. If it rise once more, no human power may avail to check it. The gap between the classes in which it surges, unseen, unsuspected by the thoughtless, is widening day by day. No tardy enactment of law, no political expedient, can close it. Against all other dangers our system of government may offer defence and shelter; against this not. I know of but one bridge that will carry us over safe, a bridge founded upon justice and built of human hearts. I believe that the danger of such conditions as are fast growing up around us is greater for the very freedom which they mock. The words of the poet, with whose lines I prefaced this book, are truer today, have far deeper meaning to us, than when they were penned forty years ago:

===Chinese===
于是，这项任务也到了收尾的时候。人种的是什么，收的也是什么。我所求的，是把我看见的真相照实说出来。倘若本书曾在收割正义庄稼的时候出过哪怕一丝微薄之力，它便算尽了它的本分。写下这几行文字的时候，我正到海边去了一趟，成千上万城里人正在那里消受他们的夏日休憩。海洋在无云的天空下沉睡。轻柔的波浪懒洋洋地漫过白色的沙滩，孩子们尖声笑着在浪前奔逃。我站在那里看他们嬉戏，有人告诉我：到了冬天狂风暴雨肆虐的时节，眼前这片如此平静的大海，会骤然狂怒而起，压倒、冲过断崖，把面前的一切扫荡净尽。那时，任何人力筑起的屏障都休想拦住它。这一片浩瀚人口的大海，戴着磨人的镣铐，在廉租公寓里不安地涌动着。我们这座城，在尚未来得及掂量清自己的担子之前，大都会伟业的职分与责任便已压到了它的肩上；它已经有一次领教过那股不可抗拒的洪流的涌起。它若再涌起一次，任何人力恐将无从遏止。阶级之间的那道鸿沟——洪流就在其中翻涌，轻率的人们看不见它、也想不到它——正一天天加宽。任何姗姗来迟的法律条文、任何政治上的权宜之计，都弥不合它。对付其他一切危难，我们的政府体制或能提供防卫与庇护；对付这一种，却不能。我只知道有一座桥能载我们平安渡过去——一座以正义为地基、以人心为砖石的桥。我相信，正在我们四周迅速滋长的这种种状况，其危险之所以更大，恰恰在于它们所嘲弄的正是自由本身。那位诗人——我曾以他的诗行题于本书卷首——的话，今天比四十年前落笔之时更加真实，对我们有远为深长的意味：

===Original===
> “﻿—Think ye that building shall endure
 

 Which shelters the noble and crushes the poor?”

===Chinese===
> “——你们以为那楼宇能够永存，
 

 它荫庇着贵人，却把穷人压得粉碎？”

## Appendix Statistics Bearing on the Tenement Problem / 附录 廉租公寓问题相关统计

===Original===
Statistics of population were left out of the text in the hope that the results of this year’s census would be available as a basis for calculation before the book went to press. They are now at hand, but their correctness is disputed. The statisticians of the Health Department claim that New York’s population has been underestimated a hundred thousand at least, and they appear to have the best of the argument. A recount is called for, and the printer will not wait. Such statistics as follow have been based on the Health Department estimates, except where the census source is given. The extent of the quarrel of official figures may be judged from this one fact, that the ordinarily conservative and careful calculations of the Sanitary Bureau make the death-rate of New York, in 1889, 25.19 for the thousand of a population of 1,575,073, while the census would make it 26.76 in a population of 1,482,273.
===Chinese===
人口统计数字当初未写入正文，是指望本书付印之前，今年人口普查的结果能够到手，用作计算的依据。如今数字已经到手，其正确性却有争议。卫生局的统计学家声称，纽约人口至少被少算了十万，而且看来他们在这场争论中占着上风。重新清点已有人在呼吁，可印刷工人不会等。以下各项统计均以卫生局的估算为依据，凡另注明人口普查出处的除外。官方数字之争闹到多大程度，从下面这一件事即可想见：按卫生处一贯保守而审慎的计算，纽约 1889 年的死亡率为每千人 25.19，人口按 1,575,073 计；而按人口普查计算，死亡率则为 26.76，人口 1,482,273。

===Original===
For every person who dies there are always two disabled by illness, so that there was a regular average of 79,358 New Yorkers on the sick-list at any moment last year. It is usual to count 28 cases of sickness the year round for every death, and this would give a total for the year 1889 of 1,111,082 of illness of all sorts.
===Chinese===
每死亡一人，总有两人因病失能，因此去年任一时刻，平均总有 79,358 名纽约人名列病员名单。惯常的算法是每例死亡全年折计 28 例病患，照此推算，1889 年全年各类病患总数当为 1,111,082 例。

===Original===
This is exclusive of deaths in institutions, properly referable to the tenements in most cases. The adult death-rate is found to decrease in the larger tenements of newer construction. The child mortality increases, reaching 114.04 percent of 1,000 living in houses containing between 60 and 80 tenants. From this point it decreases with the adult death-rate.
===Chinese===
此数未计死于收容机构中的人，而这些人按理在多数情形下本应归于廉租公寓名下。人们发现，在建筑年代较新、规模较大的廉租公寓中，成人死亡率有所下降；儿童死亡率则随之上升，在住有 60 至 80 名房客的房屋中，达每 1,000 名存活儿童的 114.04％。自此以上，它便与成人死亡率一同回落。

===Original===
Police Statistics
===Chinese===
警察统计

===Original===
Immigration
===Chinese===
移民

===Original===
Tenements
===Chinese===
廉租公寓

===Original===
Corner tenements may cover all of the lot, except 4 feet at the rear. Tenements in the block may only cover seventy-eight percent of the lot. They must have a rear yard 10 feet wide, and air-shafts or open courts equal to twelve percent of the lot.
===Chinese===
街角上的廉租公寓可以覆盖整个地块，只在后面留出 4 英尺。街区内部的廉租公寓则只能覆盖地块的百分之七十八，且须留出 10 英尺宽的后院，以及面积相当于地块百分之十二的通风井或露天庭院。

===Original===
Tenements or apartment houses must not be built over 70 feet high in streets 60 feet wide.
===Chinese===
在宽 60 英尺的街道上，廉租公寓或公寓楼的高度不得超过 70 英尺。

===Original===
Tenements or apartment houses must not be built over 80 feet high in streets wider than 60 feet.
===Chinese===
在宽于 60 英尺的街道上，廉租公寓或公寓楼的高度不得超过 80 英尺。

## Endnotes / 尾注

===Original===
1. Tweed was born and bred in a Fourth Ward tenement. ↩︎

===Chinese===
1. 特威德（Tweed）就是在第四选区（Fourth Ward）的一栋廉租公寓里出生长大的。 ↩︎

===Original===
2. Forty percent was declared by witnesses before a Senate Committee to be a fair average interest on tenement property. Instances were given of its being one hundred percent and over. ↩︎

===Chinese===
2. 有证人在参议院一个委员会（Senate Committee）面前作证称，百分之四十是廉租公寓房产公允的平均利率；还有人举出利率高达百分之一百乃至更高的实例。 ↩︎

===Original===
3. It was not until the winter of 1867 that owners of swine were prohibited by ordinance from letting them run at large in the built-up portions of the city. ↩︎

===Chinese===
3. 直到 1867 年冬，一条条例才禁止养猪人把猪放到城市的建成地段四处乱跑。 ↩︎

===Original===
4. This “unventilated and fever-breeding structure” the year after it was built was picked out by the Council of Hygiene, then just organized, and presented to the Citizens’ Association of New York as a specimen “multiple domicile” in a desirable street, with the following comment: “Here are twelve living-rooms and twenty-one bedrooms, and only six of the latter have any provision or possibility for the admission of light and air, excepting through the family sitting- and living-room; being utterly dark, close, and unventilated. The living-rooms are but 10 × 12 feet; the bedrooms 6½ × 7 feet.” ↩︎

===Chinese===
4. 这座「不通风、滋生热病的建筑」落成后的第二年，就被刚刚成立的卫生理事会（Council of Hygiene）挑了出来，作为一条体面街道上「多户合居」（multiple domicile）住宅的标本，呈交纽约公民协会（Citizens’ Association of New York），并附有如下评语：「这里共有十二间起居室和二十一间卧室，而后者之中，只有六间除借道家庭的起居兼会客之室外，还谈得上有什么引入光线与空气的设施或可能；其余各间全然黑暗、密闭、不通风。起居室不过 10 × 12 英尺；卧室 6½ × 7 英尺。」 ↩︎

===Original===
5. “A lot 50 × 60, contained twenty stables, rented for dwellings at $15 a year each; cost of the whole $600.” ↩︎

===Chinese===
5. 「一块 50 × 60 的地皮，内有二十间马厩，作住屋出租，每间年租 15 美元；整块造价 600 美元。」 ↩︎

===Original===
6. The Sheriff Street Colony of ragpickers, long since gone, is an instance in point. The thrifty Germans saved up money during years of hard work in squalor and apparently wretched poverty to buy a township in a Western State, and the whole colony moved out there in a body. There need be no doubt about their thriving there. ↩︎

===Chinese===
6. 早已消失的谢里夫街（Sheriff Street）拾荒者聚居地便是一个现成的例证。那些勤俭的德国人在长年的龌龊劳作和表面上凄惨的贫困中攒下钱来，在西部一州买下一个镇区（township），整个聚居地便成群结队地迁了过去。他们在那边兴旺发达，是无须怀疑的。 ↩︎

===Original===
7. The process can be observed in the Italian tenements in Harlem (Little Italy), which, since their occupation by these people, have been gradually sinking to the slum level. ↩︎

===Chinese===
7. 这一过程在哈莱姆的意大利人廉租公寓（「小意大利」）里便可观察到：自从被这些人住进之后，那些房子便渐渐沉沦到贫民窟的水准。 ↩︎

===Original===
8. The term child means in the mortality tables a person under five years of age. Children five years old and over figure in the tables as adults. ↩︎

===Chinese===
8. 在死亡率表中，「儿童」一词指五岁以下之人。五岁及五岁以上的儿童在表中均列为成人。 ↩︎

===Original===
9. See City Mission Report, February, 1890, page 77. ↩︎

===Chinese===
9. 参见《城市布道会报告》（City Mission Report），1890 年 2 月号，第 77 页。 ↩︎

===Original===
10. Inspector Byrnes on Lodging-houses, in the *North American Review*, September, 1889. ↩︎

===Chinese===
10. 伯恩斯探长论寄宿所的文章，载《北美评论》（*North American Review*），1889 年 9 月号。 ↩︎

===Original===
11. Deduct 69,111 women lodgers in the police stations. ↩︎

===Chinese===
11. 须扣除在警察局留宿的 69,111 名女性搭铺客。 ↩︎

===Original===
12. Report of Eastern Dispensary for 1889. ↩︎

===Chinese===
12. 东区分诊所（Eastern Dispensary）1889 年度报告。 ↩︎

===Original===
13. I refer to the Tenth Ward always as typical. The district embraced in the discussion really includes the Thirteenth Ward, and in a growing sense large portions of the Seventh and contiguous wards as well. ↩︎

===Chinese===
13. 我提第十选区时，向来是把它当作典型。讨论所涵盖的地域其实包括第十三选区，而且在越来越大的程度上，也包括第七选区及毗邻各选区的大片地段。 ↩︎

===Original===
14. An invention that cuts many garments at once, where the scissors could cut only a few. ↩︎

===Chinese===
14. 一种一次能裁下许多件衣料的发明，而剪刀一次只能裁寥寥几件。 ↩︎

===Original===
15. I was always accompanied on these tours of inquiry by one of their own people who knew of and sympathized with my mission. Without that precaution my errand would have been fruitless; even with him it was often nearly so. ↩︎

===Chinese===
15. 在这些查访中，总有他们本族的一人陪同我，他知道我的来意，也同情我的使命。没有这道防备，我的差事必然徒劳无功；即便有他随行，也常常几乎是白跑一趟。 ↩︎

===Original===
16. The strike of the cloak-makers last summer, that ended in victory, raised their wages considerably, at least for the time being. ↩︎

===Chinese===
16. 去年夏天披风工的罢工以胜利告终，使他们的工钱大为提高——至少暂时如此。 ↩︎

===Original===
17. Suspicions of murder, in the case of a woman who was found dead, covered with bruises, after a day’s running fight with her husband, in which the beer jug had been the bone of contention, brought me to this house, a ramshackle tenement on the tail-end of a lot over near the North River docks. The family in the picture lived above the rooms where the dead woman lay on a bed of straw, overrun by rats, and had been uninterested witnesses of the affray that was an everyday occurrence in the house. A patched and shaky stairway led up to their one bare and miserable room, in comparison with which a whitewashed prison-cell seemed a real palace. A heap of old rags, in which the baby slept serenely, served as the common sleeping-bunk of father, mother, and children﻿—two bright and pretty girls, singularly out of keeping in their clean, if coarse, dresses, with their surroundings. The father, a slow-going, honest English coal-heaver, earned on the average five dollars a week, “when work was fairly brisk,” at the docks. But there were long seasons when it was very “slack,” he said, doubtfully. Yet the prospect did not seem to discourage them. The mother, a pleasant-faced woman, was cheerful, even lighthearted. Her smile seemed the most sadly hopeless of all in the utter wretchedness of the place, cheery though it was meant to be and really was. It seemed doomed to certain disappointment﻿—the one thing there that was yet to know a greater depth of misery. ↩︎

===Chinese===
17. 一名女人被发现死去、浑身瘀伤——此前她与丈夫整整追逐缠斗了一天，争执的祸根是一只啤酒壶——这些谋杀嫌疑把我引到了这座房子：北河（North River）码头附近一块地皮尾端上一栋摇摇欲坠的破烂廉租公寓。照片里的这家人就住在死者所躺那几个房间的楼上；她躺在一张稻草铺上，老鼠在其上乱窜。这家人对那场斗殴漠不关心地做了目击者——在这座房子里，斗殴是天天上演的事。一条补了又补、摇摇晃晃的楼梯通向他们唯一那间空空荡荡、凄凄惨惨的屋子，同它一比，一间刷得雪白的牢房真算得上宫殿了。一堆旧破布充作父亲、母亲和孩子们共同的床铺，婴儿安睡其中；两个女孩子明媚漂亮，虽穿着粗劣却干净的衣裳，与周围的环境格格不入。父亲是个慢性子、老实的英国卸煤工，「活计相当兴旺的时候」在码头平均每周挣五美元。但也有漫长的时节活计十分「清淡」，他说，语气将信将疑。然而这样的前景似乎并没有叫他们气馁。母亲是个面容和悦的女人，快快活活，甚至无忧无虑。在这地方彻头彻尾的凄惨之中，她的微笑虽是出于欢快、也确是欢快，却似乎是万般事物中最悲哀而无望的一样。它似乎注定要落空——那是这地方唯一尚未领教过更深一层苦难的东西。 ↩︎

===Original===
18. Society for the Prevention of Cruelty to Children, Case 42,028, May 16, 1889. ↩︎

===Chinese===
18. 防止虐待儿童协会（Society for the Prevention of Cruelty to Children）案卷第 42,028 号，1889 年 5 月 16 日。 ↩︎

===Original===
19. Colonel Auchmuty’s own statement. ↩︎

===Chinese===
19. 奥克穆蒂上校（Colonel Auchmuty）本人的陈述。 ↩︎

===Original===
20. This very mother will implore the court with tears, the next morning, to let her renegade son off. A poor woman, who claimed to be the widow of a soldier, applied to the Tenement-house Relief Committee of the King’s Daughters last summer, to be sent to some home, as she had neither kith nor kin to care for her. Upon investigation it was found that she had four big sons, all toughs, who beat her regularly and took from her all the money she could earn or beg; she was “a respectable woman, of good habits,” the inquiry developed, and lied only to shield her rascally sons. ↩︎

===Chinese===
20. 就是这个母亲，第二天早上还会流着泪恳求法庭宽纵她的忤逆儿子。去年夏天，一个自称士兵遗孀的穷女人向「国王之女会」（King’s Daughters）廉租公寓救济委员会提出申请，要求把她送进某个收容之所，理由是她无亲无故、无人照管。一查之下才发现，她有四个成年的儿子，全是恶棍，经常毒打她，把她挣来、讨来的钱搜刮一空；调查还查明，她是个「体面规矩、习惯良善的女人」，说谎只是为了庇护她那几个无赖儿子。 ↩︎

===Original===
21. “The percentage of foreign-born prisoners in 1850, as compared with that of natives, was more than five times that of native prisoners, now (1880) it is less than double.” —﻿American Prisons in the Tenth Census ↩︎

===Chinese===
21. 「1850 年，外国出生囚犯所占的百分比，与本土出生者相较，是本土囚犯的五倍多；如今（1880 年）已不足两倍。」——《第十次人口普查中的美国监狱》（American Prisons in the Tenth Census） ↩︎

===Original===
22. In printing-offices the broken, worn-out, and useless type is thrown into the “hell-box,” to be recast at the foundry. ↩︎

===Chinese===
22. 在印刷所里，破损、磨损报废、无用的铅字都被扔进「废铅字箱」（hell-box），送去铸字厂重铸。 ↩︎

===Original===
23. Dr. Louis L. Seaman, late chief of staff of the Blackwell’s Island hospitals: “Social Waste of a Great City,” read before the American Association for the Advancement of Science, 1886. ↩︎

===Chinese===
23. 路易斯·L·西曼医生（Dr. Louis L. Seaman），布莱克韦尔岛诸医院前主任医师：《一个大城市的社会虚耗》（“Social Waste of a Great City”），1886 年宣读于美国科学促进会（American Association for the Advancement of Science）。 ↩︎

===Original===
24. Forty-fourth Annual Report of the Association for Improving the Condition of the Poor. 1887. ↩︎

===Chinese===
24. 《贫民状况改善协会第四十四次年度报告》（Forty-fourth Annual Report of the Association for Improving the Condition of the Poor），1887 年。 ↩︎

===Original===
25. In 1869, a tenement was a house occupied by four families or more. ↩︎

===Chinese===
25. 1869 年，廉租公寓指住有四户或四户以上人家的房屋。 ↩︎

===Original===
26. In 1888, a tenement was a house occupied by three families or more. ↩︎

===Chinese===
26. 1888 年，廉租公寓指住有三户或三户以上人家的房屋。 ↩︎

===Original===
27. These figures represent less than two hundred of the worst tenements below Houston Street. ↩︎

===Chinese===
27. 这些数字所代表的，是休斯敦街（Houston Street）以南不到两百栋最恶劣的廉租公寓。 ↩︎
